(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2})(-\infty)=E\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\chi_{K}j^{K}[u])^{\prime}\,dr_{*}=E\int_{r_{\rm max}-\frac{\delta}{2}}^{r_{\rm max}+\frac{\delta}{2}}\frac{d\chi}{dr}\widetilde{\omega}\Re(iu^{\prime}\overline{u})\,dr+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{K}\widetilde{\omega}\Re(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
\leq EB\delta^{-1}\int_{r_{\rm max}-\frac{\delta}{2}}^{r_{\rm max}+\frac{\delta}{2}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}\,dr+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{K}\widetilde{\omega}\Re(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
\leq\frac{A}{4}\int_{r_{\rm max}-\frac{\delta}{2}}^{r_{\rm max}+\frac{\delta}{2}}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+b\Lambda|u|^{2})\,dr+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{K}\widetilde{\omega}\Re(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*},

where the last inequality follows by taking
A
suitably large compared to
EB\delta^{-1}
, which in turn requires
\omega_{\rm high}
to be sufficiently large.

Analogously, we consider we consider
(E\chi_{T}j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
, where
\chi_{T}
denotes a smooth cut-off function such that

\chi_{T}(r)=\begin{cases}0&r\in[1,r_{\rm max}-\frac{\delta}{2})\\
1&r\in[r_{\rm max}+\frac{\delta}{2},\infty),\end{cases}

with
\left|\frac{d\chi}{dr}\right|\leq B\delta^{-1}
and we repeat the argument above to control
(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2})(\infty)
.
∎

6.5.5.
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\omega}_{\rm nsr}
: non-superradiant,
\omega
-dominated frequencies

In this section, we consider non-superradiant frequencies with
\omega^{2}\gg\Lambda
and we make use of the favourable sign of
\omega^{2}-V
in this frequency regime.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\omega}_{\rm nsr}
. For
a\leq 1
,
E\geq 2
,
\delta>0
, and for
\epsilon_{\rm high}
suitably small, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

c\int_{1}^{R_{\infty}}(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\delta}r^{-1-\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\Lambda)(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\delta}r^{-3}|u|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\Re(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega\Re(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*},

(6.48)

with the function
y
is defined in the proof below.

Proof.

The proof will proceed in
two steps
.

Step 1
: a
j^{y}_{2}[u]
-current

By (
6.29
), there exists a
R_{\infty}>2
, arbitrarily large and uniform in
a,\Lambda,m,\omega
, such that:

\frac{dV}{dr}(r)\leq-\Lambda r^{-3}

for all
r\geq R_{\infty}-1
. We now define
y:(1,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
as follows:

y(r)=\begin{cases}1+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(r-1)^{\delta}}{(R_{\infty}-1)^{\delta}}&r\leq R_{\infty}\\
2-r^{-\delta}&r\geq R_{\infty}+1,\end{cases}

with
\delta>0
. Via a standard mollification argument, we can ensure
y
is smooth and
\frac{dy}{dr}>0
for
r\leq R_{\infty}
. Integrating
(j^{y}_{2}[u])^{\prime}
gives

\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{dy}{dr}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-y\frac{dV}{dr}|u|^{2}\,dr+|u^{\prime}|^{2}(-\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)=2|u^{\prime}|^{2}(\infty)+2\omega^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\Re(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}

(6.49)

For
\omega_{\rm high}
sufficiently large, depending on
\delta
and
R_{\infty}
:

\frac{dy}{dr}(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2}\geq c(r-1)^{-1+\delta}(\omega^{2}+\Lambda)\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[1,R_{\infty})$}.

Step 2
: the
j^{T}[u]
-current

It remains to estimate the boundary terms on the right-hand side of (
6.49
). Since the frequency range is non-superradiant, we can simply consider the current
EJ^{T}[u]
, with
E\geq 2
a constant. Indeed, this gives:

(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2})(\infty)+E\omega\widetilde{\omega}|u|^{2}(-\infty)\leq E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega\Re(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

Note that
\omega\widetilde{\omega}=\omega(\omega-m\upomega_{+})>0
, since
\omega^{2}\gg\Lambda>m^{2}
, so the left-hand side above is non-negative definite.
∎

6.5.6.
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\Lambda}_{\rm nsr}
: non-superradiant,
\Lambda
-dominated frequencies

In this section, we consider non-superradiant frequencies with
\Lambda\gg\omega^{2}
. The strategy for obtaining integrated estimates may be thought of as a simplification of the strategy employed in the proof of Proposition
6.9
.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\Lambda}_{\rm nsr}
. For
a\leq 1
with
1-a
suitably small,
E\geq 2
and
\omega_{\rm high}
suitably large, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

c\int_{R_{+}}^{R_{\infty}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\Lambda+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Re((f^{\prime}+h)u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*},

(6.50)

with the functions
f
and
h
defined in the proof of Proposition
6.9
.

Proof.

Suppose
m\omega\geq 0
. Then
m\omega\geq m\upomega_{+}+\alpha\sqrt{\Lambda m^{2}}
, which implies that
\omega^{2}>\alpha^{2}\Lambda
. But we also have that
\omega^{2}<\epsilon_{\rm width}\Lambda
for
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\Lambda}_{\rm nsr}
. For
0<\epsilon_{\rm width}<\alpha^{2}
suitably small, this is a contradiction. It must therefore hold that
m\omega<0
.

Hence, as in the proof of Proposition
6.9
(Condition B), we can apply (iv) of Lemma
6.7
to infer that there exist
\delta>0
and
b>0
suitably small, such that

\displaystyle(r_{\max}-r)\frac{dV}{dr}(r)\geq

\displaystyle\>br^{-4}(r-r_{\max})^{2}m^{2}\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[1,\infty)$},

\displaystyle V(r)-\omega^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\>b\Lambda\quad\textnormal{for $r\in(r_{\rm max}-\delta,r_{\rm max}+\delta)$}.

We can therefore repeat the steps in the proof of Proposition
6.9
(Condition B), with the additional simplification that the boundary terms at
r_{*}=\pm\infty
can immediately be estimated, without the need for cut-off functions, since
m\omega<0
guarantees that we are outside of the superradiant frequency regime.
∎

6.5.7.
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\sim}_{\rm nsr}
: non-superradiant, trapped frequencies

By construction, the frequencies in
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\sim}_{\rm nsr}
are non-superradiant. This frequency range is affected by the existence of trapped null geodesics (away from the event horizon). The corresponding integrated estimates from
[
DRSR16
, Proposition 8.6.1]
in the case
a<1
hold without modification when
a\leq 1
.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\sim}
. For
a\leq 1
,
E\geq 2
and
\omega_{\rm high}
suitably large, there exist constants
b,c>0
and
r_{1}\in(1+b,\infty]
such that:

(i)

For
r\in[1,r_{1})

\omega^{2}-V(r)\geq b\Lambda.

Furthermore, if
r_{1}<\infty
,
V
has a unique nondegenerate maximum
r_{\max}\in[r_{1},\infty)
. Denote with
R_{\rm dec}>1
a constant such that
\frac{dV}{dr}<0
for
r\geq R_{\rm dec}
.

(ii)

c\int_{1}^{R_{\infty}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\Lambda+\omega^{2})(r-1)(r-r_{\rm trap})^{2}|u|^{2}+|u|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\Re(f_{\sim}^{\prime}u\overline{H}-2f_{\sim}u^{\prime}\overline{H})+2\hat{y}\Re(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega\Re(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*},

(6.51)

where
f_{\sim},\hat{y}:(1,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
are smooth functions that satisfy: for
r_{1}<R_{\rm dec}<\infty
,
r_{\rm trap}=r_{\rm max}
and

\displaystyle f_{\sim}(1)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\frac{df_{\sim}}{dr}(r)\geq

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[1,\infty)$},

\displaystyle-f_{\sim}V^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f_{\sim}^{\prime\prime\prime}>

\displaystyle\>b\Lambda\Delta r^{-7}(r-r_{\rm max})^{2}\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[r_{1},\infty)$},

\displaystyle f_{\sim}(r)=

\displaystyle\>1\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[R_{\rm dec},\infty)$},

\displaystyle|f_{\sim}|+\left|\frac{df_{\sim}}{dr}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>B,

\displaystyle\hat{y}(r)=

\displaystyle\>1-e^{C(r_{1}-r)}\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[1,r_{1})$},

\displaystyle\hat{y}(r)=

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[r_{1},\infty)$}

for appropriate positive constants
\delta,B,C>0
with
E\gg C
and
\delta>0
,
R_{+}-1
suitably small.

For
R_{\rm dec}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
,
r_{\rm trap}=0
and

\displaystyle f_{\sim}(r)=

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[1,R_{\rm dec}]$},

\displaystyle\frac{df_{\sim}}{dr}(r)\geq

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[r_{1},R_{\infty}]$},

\displaystyle f_{\sim}(r)=

\displaystyle\>1\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[R_{\rm dec}+1,\infty)$},

\displaystyle|f_{\sim}|+\left|f^{\prime}_{\sim}\right|+\left|f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\sim}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>B,

\displaystyle\hat{y}(r)=

\displaystyle\>1-e^{\tilde{C}(R_{\rm dec}+2-r)}\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[1,R_{\rm dec}+2)$},

\displaystyle\hat{y}(r)=

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{for $r\in[R_{\rm dec}+2,\infty)$},

for appropriate positive constants
B,\tilde{C}>0
with
E\gg\tilde{C}
.

(iii)

If
\Lambda>\omega_{\rm high}m^{2}
, then

|r_{\rm max}-(1+\sqrt{2})|\leq C(\omega_{\rm high}^{-1}+(1-a^{2})).

Proof.

Part (i) and (iii) follows from
[
DRSR16
, Lemma 8.6.1]
and
[
DRSR16
, Proposition 8.6.1]
. Part (iii) follows from (i) of Lemma
6.7
.
∎

6.5.8.
Homogeneous solutions

In this section, we consider solutions to the homogeneous ODE:

u^{\prime\prime}+(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\widetilde{V})u=0,

(6.52)

with
\omega\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0,m\upomega_{+}\}
. We will derive weighted
L^{\infty}
and
L^{2}
estimates for solutions to (
6.52
).

These constitute a key part towards deriving integrated estimates in the bounded frequency range
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}
and they form the greatest departure from the frequency-space analysis in the
|a|<M
setting in
[
DRSR16
]
.

In this section, we will not keep track of the
m
-dependence in the uniform constants appearing in our integrated and pointwise estimates.
Because of this, our final integrated estimates in frequency space will lead to integrated energy estimates in physical space for azimuthal modes with bounded
m
, i.e. the estimates we derive here are not sufficient to be able to consider the sum over all
m
.

We will denote with
u_{\rm hor}
and
u_{\rm inf}
solutions to (
6.52
) that satisfy the following boundary conditions:

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{*}\to-\infty}(u_{\rm hor}^{\prime}+i\widetilde{\omega}u_{\rm hor})(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>0,

(6.53)

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{*}\to+\infty}(u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}-i\omega u_{\rm inf})(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>0,

(6.54)

\displaystyle|u_{\rm hor}|(-\infty)=|u_{\rm inf}|(+\infty)=1.

(6.55)

Note in particular that the norms of
u_{\rm hor}
and
u_{\rm inf}
are fixed at
r_{*}=-\infty
and
r_{*}=+\infty
, respectively.

The main aim of Lemma
6.13
below is to derive
L^{\infty}
-estimates for
u_{\rm hor}
and
u_{\rm inf}
sufficiently close to the event horizon at
r=1
. Since the irregular singular point
r=1
for
\widetilde{\omega}\neq 0
becomes a regular singular point in the limit
\widetilde{\omega}\to 0
, these
L^{\infty}
-estimates do not follow immediately from the expansions obtained from standard ODE theory.

The main strategy for resolving this difficulty is to rescale
r-1
in such a way that the singular point stays irregular in the limit
\widetilde{\omega}\to 0
in terms of the rescaled variable. As a result, the expansions obtained from standard ODE theory remain valid in the limit. This comes at the expense of the region of validity of the expansions
shrinking to zero
as
\widetilde{\omega}\to 0
. More precisely, the
L^{\infty}
estimates near
r=1
obtained in this way will only be valid in the interval
(1,1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)
, where
z_{0}
can be taken arbitrarily large.

Although we only require the estimates of Lemma
6.13
for
a=1
, we will actually derive them uniformly for
a\leq 1
with
1-a\geq 0
sufficiently small.

Lemma 6.13
.

Assume
|\omega|\geq\omega_{0}>0
for some
\omega_{0}>0
. Let
z_{0}>0
be arbitrarily large and assume that there exists a
B>0
such that
\Lambda+|\omega|^{2}\leq Bm^{2}
. Then, there exists a numerical constant
B_{\rm hom}=B_{\rm hom}(B,z_{0},m,\omega_{0})>0
, such that for
1-a
suitably small, solutions
u
to (
6.52
) satisfy the bounds:

\displaystyle||u_{\rm hor}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1,1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}||u^{\prime}_{\rm hor}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1,1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)}\leq

\displaystyle\>B_{\rm hom},

(6.56)

\displaystyle||u_{\rm inf}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1,1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}||u^{\prime}_{\rm inf}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1,1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)}\leq

\displaystyle\>\frac{B_{\rm hom}(|W|+\sqrt{|\omega|}\sqrt{|\widetilde{\omega}|})}{|\widetilde{\omega}|},

(6.57)

\displaystyle||u_{\rm inf}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1+z_{0}^{-1},\infty)}+||u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1+z_{0}^{-1},\infty)}\leq

\displaystyle\>B_{\rm hom},

(6.58)

\displaystyle||u_{\rm hor}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1+z_{0}^{-1},\infty)}+|\omega|^{-1}||u^{\prime}_{\rm hor}||_{L^{\infty}_{r}(1+z_{0}^{-1},\infty)}\leq

\displaystyle\>\frac{B_{\rm hom}(|W|+\sqrt{|\widetilde{\omega}|}\sqrt{|\omega|})}{|\omega|}.

(6.59)

Proof.

We can express

r_{*}=(1+a^{2})\left[-\frac{a^{2}}{1-a^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{1-a^{2}}{r-1}\right)+\log(r-1)\right]+r-1,

so that

e^{\frac{-(1-a^{2})}{a^{2}(1+a^{2})}r_{*}}=\left(1+\frac{1-a^{2}}{r-1}\right)e^{-a^{-2}(1-a^{2})(\log(r-1)+(r-1))}.

By expanding the exponentials, we obtain

(r-1)^{-1}\left[1+\frac{r-1}{1-a^{2}}(e^{-a^{-2}(1-a^{2})(\log(r-1)+(r-1))}-1)\right]\\
=\left[-a^{-2}(1+a^{2})^{-1}r_{*}\right]\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-a^{-2}(1+a^{2})^{-1}(1-a^{2})r_{*})^{k}}{(k+1)!}\right).

(6.60)

By considering separately the cases
(r-1)\leq\epsilon(1-a^{2})
and
(r-1)>\epsilon(1-a^{2})
with
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small, and taking
\delta
suitably small compared to
\epsilon
, we can estimate for
r-1<\delta
:

\frac{r-1}{1-a^{2}}(1-e^{-a^{-2}(1-a^{2})(\log(r-1)+(r-1))})\leq\epsilon.

Therefore, for
r_{*}<-1
, we can express:

(r-1)\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{a^{-2}(1+a^{2})^{-2}|r_{*}|\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(a^{-2}(1+a^{2})^{-1}(1-a^{2})|r_{*}|)^{k}}{(k+1)!}\right)}\leq\frac{(1+\epsilon)a^{2}(1+a^{2})}{|r_{*}|},

(6.61)

from which it also follows that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

(1-a^{2})^{k}(r-1)=O(|r_{*}|^{-k-1}).

By taking derivatives in
r_{*}
on both sides of (
6.60
), we moreover infer that:

(1-a^{2})^{k}(r-1)=O_{\infty}(|r_{*}|^{-k-1}).

(6.62)

Consider the rescaled variable
x=|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|r_{*}
. Then (
6.52
) is equivalent to:

\frac{d^{2}u}{dx^{2}}+m^{2}(1-\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}\widetilde{V})u=0.

(6.63)

By applying (
6.62
), we obtain with respect to the coordinate
x
:

(1-a^{2})^{k}(r-1)=O_{\infty}(|m|^{-1-k}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{1+k}|x|^{-1-k}).

(6.64)

So, by (
6.24
) and (
6.25
), it follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}\widetilde{V}(r)=

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}(|x|^{-1})

The expressions for the rescaled potentials above imply therefore that
x=-\infty
is a irregular singular point for (
6.63
) for all
a\leq 1
, so by standard ODE theory for asymptotic expansions around irregular singular points, see for example
[
Olv74
, §7.2, Theorem 2.1]
, it admits two solutions
u_{\pm}
that can be written as follows:

\displaystyle u_{\pm}(x)=

\displaystyle\>e^{\pm i|m|x}(1\pm\varepsilon_{\pm}(x)),

(6.65)

\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{\pm}(x)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{m}|x|^{-1},

(6.66)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{\pm}}{dx}(x)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{m}|x|^{-2}.

(6.67)

By the boundary conditions for
u_{\rm hor}
, it therefore follows immediately that:

u_{\rm hor}(r_{*})=e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}(1+O_{m}(|x|^{-1})).

so we can conclude that

||u_{\rm hor}||_{L^{\infty}_{r_{*}}(-\infty,-|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}x_{0})}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}||u^{\prime}_{\rm hor}||_{L^{\infty}_{r_{*}}(-\infty,-|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}x_{0})}\leq B_{\rm hom}.

We expand:

u_{\rm inf}(r_{*})=A_{+}u_{\rm hor}(r_{*})+A_{-}\overline{u_{\rm hor}(r_{*})}=A_{+}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}(1+O_{m}(|x|^{-1}))+A_{-}e^{+i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}(1+O_{m}(|x|^{-1})),

with
A_{\pm}(\widetilde{\omega})\in\mathbb{C}
.

By integrating
(j^{T}[u_{\rm inf}]))^{\prime}
and applying the boundary conditions for
u_{\rm inf}
we can express

\begin{split}\omega^{2}=\omega^{2}|u_{\rm inf}|^{2}(\infty)=&-\omega\Re(iu_{\rm inf}^{\prime}\overline{u}_{\rm inf})(-\infty)=-\widetilde{\omega}(|A_{+}|^{2}-|A_{-}|^{2}),\end{split}

So we have that:

|A_{+}|^{2}-|A_{-}|^{2}=-\frac{\omega}{\widetilde{\omega}}.

(6.68)

We can also express the Wronskian
W
as follows:

W=[u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}u_{\rm hor}-u_{\rm hor}^{\prime}u_{\rm inf}](-\infty)=2i\widetilde{\omega}A_{-}.

(6.69)

Hence, there exists a constant
B_{\rm hom}>0
depending only on
x_{0}
, such that

||u_{\rm inf}||_{L^{\infty}_{r_{*}}(-\infty,-|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}x_{0})}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}||u^{\prime}_{\rm inf}||_{L^{\infty}_{r_{*}}(-\infty,-|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}x_{0})}\leq\frac{B_{\rm hom}(|W|+\sqrt{|\omega|}\sqrt{|\widetilde{\omega}|})}{|\widetilde{\omega}|},

with
B_{\rm hom}>0
a constant depending on
m
,
x_{0}
and
B
.

Note further that by (
6.61
), we can find a constant
C>0
such that

(r-1)|r_{*}|\leq C,

so we can restrict the intervals in the
L^{\infty}
-estimates from
(-\infty,-|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}x_{0})_{r_{*}}
to
(1,1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)_{r}
for an appropriately large
z_{0}
. This concludes the proof of (
6.56
) and (
6.57
).

The estimate (
6.58
) follows easily from a similar asymptotic analysis of (
6.52
) at
r_{*}=\infty
, where
u_{\rm inf}
now takes the role of
u_{\rm hor}
above and there is no need to rescale the variable
r_{*}
as we are bounded away from
\omega=0
by the assumption
|\omega|>\omega_{0}
. We have that

\displaystyle u_{\inf}(x)=

\displaystyle\>e^{i\omega r_{*}}(1+\varepsilon_{+}(r_{*})),

\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{+}(r_{*})|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{m}|r|^{-1},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{m}|r|^{-1}.

We can then express in the region
r\geq 1+z_{0}^{-1}
:

u_{\rm hor}(r_{*})=D_{+}u_{\rm inf}(r_{*})+D_{-}\overline{u_{\rm inf}(r_{*})}=D_{+}e^{i\omega r_{*}}(1+|m|^{-1}O(r^{-1}))+D_{-}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}(1+|m|^{-1}O(r^{-1})),

with
D_{\pm}(\widetilde{\omega})\in\mathbb{C}
.
We integrate
(j^{T}[u_{\rm inf}]))^{\prime}
to express:

\begin{split}\widetilde{\omega}\omega=\widetilde{\omega}\omega|u_{\rm hor}|^{2}(-\infty)=\omega\Re(iu_{\rm hor}^{\prime}\overline{u}_{\rm hor})(\infty)=-\omega^{2}(|D_{+}|^{2}-|D_{-}|^{2}),\end{split}

We can also express the Wronskian
W
as follows:

W=[u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}u_{\rm hor}-u_{\rm hor}^{\prime}u_{\rm inf}](\infty)=2i\omega D_{-}.

From the above, it follows that (
6.59
) holds with
B_{\rm hom}
depending only on
z_{0}
.
∎

We will need to establish additionally appropriate control over
u_{\rm inf}
in the region
r\geq 1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|
. For this reason, we need to consider the potential
\check{V}
, which is defined in §
6.4.1
. See (
6.27
) in Lemma
6.5
for the behaviour of
\check{V}
near
r=1
.

In Lemma
3.4
and Proposition
6.14
below, we will restrict to
a=1
.

We now consider the weight functions
\mathfrak{w}
satisfying (
6.5.8
), i.e. solutions to

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}^{\prime\prime}-\widetilde{V}_{\rm stat}\mathfrak{w}=0.

Define the renormalized homogeneous solutions
\check{u}_{\rm inf}
as follows:

\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}=\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}^{-1}u_{\rm inf}.

Then
\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}
satisfies the following equation for
a=1
:

\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}^{\prime\prime}+2\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}^{-1}\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{\pm}\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}^{\prime}+\left[\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}\right]\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}=0.

(6.70)

We introduce the corresponding current
\check{j}^{y}[\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}]
as follows:

\displaystyle\check{j}^{y}[\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}]=

\displaystyle\>y\left(|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}^{\prime}|^{2}+\left[\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}\right]|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\left(\check{j}^{y}[\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}]\right)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{dy}{dr}\left(|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}^{\prime}|^{2}+\left[\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}\right]|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}|^{2}\right)-y\frac{d\check{V}}{dr}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}|^{2}-4y\Re\left(\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}^{-1}\frac{d\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}}{dr}\right)|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}^{\prime}|^{2}.

In the proposition below, we will derive integrated estimates for
\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}
or
\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}
in the region
1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq r<\infty
, using Lemma
6.13
to estimate the boundary term at
r=1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|
.

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
. Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrarily small and
z_{0}
be arbitrarily large. Then, for
\eta_{\flat}
suitably small, there exists a constant
C=C(\eta_{\flat},\epsilon,z_{0})>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\infty}

\displaystyle r^{-1-\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}((1-r^{-1})^{-2}|u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}m^{2}|u_{\rm inf}|^{2})+m^{2}|u_{\rm inf}|^{2}]\,dr

(6.71)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>CB^{2}_{\rm hom}m^{4}(1+|W|^{2}|\omega|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}),

\displaystyle\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{1+\delta}

\displaystyle(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon-2\Im\sqrt{2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}}}(|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}|^{2})+(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,\pm}|^{2}]\,dr

(6.72)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>CB^{2}_{\rm hom}m^{2}(1+|W|^{2}|\omega|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}),

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}

\displaystyle r^{-1-\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}(|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}|u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2}+|m\widetilde{\omega}||u_{\rm inf}|^{2})\,dr

(6.73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>CB^{2}_{\rm hom}m^{2}(1+|W|^{2}|\omega|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}),

where (
6.72
) is valid with
\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}
if
m\widetilde{\omega}<0
and with
\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}
if
m\widetilde{\omega}>0
.

Proof.

Let
0\leq\epsilon<1
and choose
y_{\epsilon}(r)=(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon-\beta}\chi(r)
, with
\beta=2\Im\sqrt{2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}}
and
\chi
a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\leq 1+\delta
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\geq 1+2\delta
, with
\delta>0
suitably small.

We apply (
6.27
) to express:

\begin{split}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}(r)=&\>\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+m\widetilde{\omega}(r-1)+[|m\widetilde{\omega}|+|\Lambda-\Lambda_{+}|]O((r-1)^{2}).\end{split}

Hence,

\frac{d}{dr}\left(y_{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}(r))\right)=-(2-\epsilon-\beta)\chi\widetilde{\omega}^{2}(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon-\beta}+(1-\epsilon+\beta)\chi(-m\widetilde{\omega})(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon-\beta}\\
+[|m\widetilde{\omega}|+|\Lambda-\Lambda_{+}|]O((r-1)^{-1+\epsilon-\beta})+\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon-\beta}g(r)(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}(r)).

(6.74)

Suppose first that
m\widetilde{\omega}<0
. Using that
(r-1)\geq z_{0}|\widetilde{\omega}||m|^{-1}
and taking
\delta
and
\eta_{\flat}
suitably small and
z_{0}
suitably large, together with (
6.10
), we can estimate

\frac{d}{dr}\left(y_{\epsilon}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}(r))\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}|m\widetilde{\omega}|(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon-\beta}.

Furthermore, by (
3.11
), we have that when
2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}\neq 0
,

\frac{dy_{\epsilon}}{dr}-4y_{\epsilon}\Re\left(\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}\frac{d\mathfrak{w}_{+}}{dr}\right)=(\epsilon+\beta)(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon}+O((r-1)^{-2+\epsilon}).

(6.75)

When
2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}=0
, we obtain instead

\frac{dy_{\epsilon}}{dr}-4y_{\epsilon}\Re\left(\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}\frac{d\mathfrak{w}_{+}}{dr}\right)=(\epsilon+\log((r-1)^{-1})^{-1})(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon}+\log(r-1)O((r-1)^{-2+\epsilon}).

(6.76)

We can moreover estimate:

\frac{d\chi}{dr}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V})\geq-C(|m\widetilde{\omega}|+|\Lambda-\Lambda_{+}|)|\check{u}_{\rm inf}|^{2}

when
r\in[1+\delta,2+2\delta]
.

Combining the above observations, we obtain the following estimate for
\epsilon>0
,
\delta>0
suitably small and
m\widetilde{\omega}<0
: let
r_{0}=1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|
, then

\int_{r_{0}}^{1+2\delta}(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon-\beta}\epsilon\chi|\check{u}^{\prime}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}+(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon-\beta}\chi(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}(r-1)^{-1}+|m\widetilde{\omega}|)|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq C\int_{1+\delta}^{1+2\delta}(|m\widetilde{\omega}|+|\Lambda-\Lambda_{+}|)|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\,dr+C\left|m\widetilde{\omega}\right|(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon-\beta}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\Big|_{r=r_{0}}.

(6.77)

By a Hardy inequality in
r
, we can also estimate:

\begin{split}\int_{r_{0}}^{1+2\delta}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon-\beta}\chi|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\,dr\leq&\>C\int_{r_{0}}^{1+2\delta}(\epsilon-\beta)^{-2}(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon-\beta}\chi|\check{u}^{\prime}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\,dr\\
&+C\int_{1+\delta}^{1+2\delta}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\,dr.\end{split}

(6.78)

We can combine (
6.77
), (
6.81
) and (
6.78
) and estimate the resulting boundary terms at
r=r_{0}
via (
6.57
) and Lemma
3.4
, together with (
6.57
) and (
6.58
) to estimate the boundary terms and the integrals in
[1+\delta,1+2\delta]
, in order to obtain for
z_{0}
suitably large and
\eta_{\flat}
suitably small, depending on
\epsilon
:

\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{1+2\delta}(r-1)^{-3+\epsilon-\beta}\chi|\check{u}^{\prime}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}+(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon-\beta}\chi|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}+(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon-\beta}\chi(|m\widetilde{\omega}|+(r-1)^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq CB_{\rm\hom}^{2}(1+|W|^{2}|\omega|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1})m^{2}.

(6.79)

We conclude that (
6.72
) holds. In fact, we can now repeat the argument with
\epsilon=0
by considering
y_{0}
.

By using the properties of
\mathfrak{w}_{+}
from Lemma
3.4
, we obtain

\begin{split}|{u}_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2}=&\>|\mathfrak{w}_{+}|^{2}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}^{\prime}+\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{\prime}\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}\\
\leq&\>C(r-1)^{-1-\beta}[|\log(r-1)||\check{u}_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2}+Cm^{2}(r-1)^{2}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,+}|^{2}],\end{split}

where the
|\log(r-1)|
factor is only required when
2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}=0
.

Hence,

\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{1+2\delta}(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon}\chi(|{u}^{\prime}_{\rm inf}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u_{\rm inf}|^{2})+m^{2}(r-1)^{\epsilon}\chi|{u}_{\rm inf}|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq CB_{\rm\hom}^{2}(1+|W|^{2}|\omega|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1})m^{4}.

(6.80)

To conclude (
6.71
) for
m\widetilde{\omega}<0
, we use that we can add an appropriate integral in
[1+\delta,\infty)
to (
6.80
) after applying (
6.58
).

Now suppose that
m\widetilde{\omega}>0
. Then we consider instead
\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}
together with the current
\check{j}^{-y_{0}}[\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}]
, with
y_{0}=(r-1)^{-2+\beta}
. Using that for
\delta>0
suitably small we now have:

\frac{d}{dr}\left(-y_{0}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}(r))\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}|m\widetilde{\omega}|(r-1)^{-2+\beta}.

and by the leading-order term on the right-hand side of (
6.75
) has a good sign when
\epsilon=0
and
\mathfrak{w}_{+}
is replaced with
\mathfrak{w}_{-}
, we can conclude that for
m\widetilde{\omega}>0
:

\int_{r_{0}}^{1+2\delta}|m\widetilde{\omega}|(r-1)^{-2+\beta}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq C\int_{1+\delta}^{1+2\delta}(|m\widetilde{\omega}|+|\Lambda-\Lambda_{+}|)|\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}|^{2}\,dr+C|m\widetilde{\omega}|(r-1)^{-1+\beta}|\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}|^{2}\Big|_{r=r_{0}}.

(6.81)

From this it follows that

\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{1+2\delta}(r-1)^{-2-\beta}\chi|m\widetilde{\omega}||\check{u}_{\rm inf,-}|^{2}\,dr\leq CB_{\rm\hom}^{2}(1+|W|^{2}|\omega|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1})m^{2}.

Equipped with the above estimate, we can then continue the argument as in the case
m\widetilde{\omega}<0
to conclude that (
6.72
) and (
6.71
) hold also when
m\widetilde{\omega}>0
.

Finally, (
6.73
) follows by simply integrating the estimates in (
6.57
).
∎

We will additionally need an integrated estimate for
u_{\rm hor}
in the region
1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq r\leq 1+\delta
, with
\delta>0
arbitrarily small.

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
. Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrarily small and
z_{0}
be arbitrarily large. Then, for
\eta_{\flat}
suitably small, there exists a constant
C=C(\eta_{\flat},\epsilon,z_{0})>0
such that

\begin{split}\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\infty}&r^{-1-\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}((1-r^{-1})^{-2}|u_{\rm hor}^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}m^{2}|u_{\rm hor}|^{2})+m^{2}|u_{\rm hor}|^{2}]\,dr\\
\leq&\>CB^{2}_{\rm hom}m^{2}(|m\widetilde{\omega}|+|W|^{2}).\end{split}

(6.82)

Proof.

We proceed exactly as in the proof of Proposition
6.14
, replacing
u_{\rm inf}
with
u_{\rm hor}
and applying the
L^{\infty}
estimates for
u_{\rm hor}
from Lemma
6.13
instead of those for
u_{\rm inf}
.
∎

6.5.9.
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
: bounded frequencies near superradiant threshold

The solution
u
to the inhomogeneous ODE (
6.9
) can be expressed via integrals involving the Wronskian and solutions to the homogeneous ODE:

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
a\leq 1
. Then the following identities hold for all
r\geq 1
and for almost every
\omega
:

\displaystyle u(r)=

\displaystyle\>W^{-1}\left[u_{\rm inf}(r)\int_{1}^{r}u_{\rm hor}(r^{\prime})H(r^{\prime})\,\frac{r^{\prime 2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}dr^{\prime}+u_{\rm hor}(r)\int_{r}^{\infty}u_{\rm inf}(r^{\prime})H(r^{\prime})\,\frac{r^{\prime 2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}dr^{\prime}\right],

(6.83)

\displaystyle u^{\prime}(r)=

\displaystyle\>W^{-1}\left[u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}(r)\int_{1}^{r}u_{\rm hor}(r^{\prime})H(r^{\prime})\,\frac{r^{\prime 2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}dr^{\prime}+u_{\rm hor}^{\prime}(r)\int_{r}^{\infty}u_{\rm inf}(r^{\prime})H(r^{\prime})\,\frac{r^{\prime 2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}dr^{\prime}\right].

(6.84)

Proof.

The right-hand sides of (
6.83
) and (
6.84
) are well defined since the integrals are bounded for almost every
\omega
by (ii) of Proposition
6.4
and the (non-quantitative) boundedness of
u_{\rm hor}
,
u_{\rm inf}
, and furthermore,
W^{-1}
is bounded for
\omega\notin\{0,m\upomega_{+}\}
by
[
TdC20
]
[Theorem 1.3]; see also Theorem
6.18
below.

By the smoothness of
H
in
r_{*}
established in (i) of Proposition
6.4
, it therefore follows that the right-hand side of (
6.83
) constitutes a smooth solution to (
6.9
) for almost every
\omega
that moreover satisfies the boundary conditions (
6.32
) and (
6.33
). By uniqueness, it must therefore be equal to
u
for almost every
\omega
.
∎

To obtain estimates for (
6.9
), we will need the following lower bound assumption on the Wronskian:

|\omega\widetilde{\omega}||W^{-1}|^{2}\leq W_{0}.

for
a=1
and for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
satisfying
\Lambda\leq B_{0}m^{2}
, for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
with
B_{0}>0
a uniform constant.

As an immediate corollary of (
6.68
) and (
6.69
), we obtain the validity of the above Wronskian bound in the non-superradiant frequency regime.

Corollary 6.17
.

Let
m\omega\notin(0,m\upomega_{+})
. Then

|\omega\widetilde{\omega}||W^{-1}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Proof.

We combine (
6.68
) and (
6.69
) to obtain:

|W|^{2}=4\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|A_{+}|^{2}+4\omega\widetilde{\omega}\geq 4\omega\widetilde{\omega}.

We conclude the bound on
|W|^{-1}
by using that the assumption on
\omega
implies that
\omega\widetilde{\omega}>0
.
∎

In
[
TdC20
]
, a bound has been obtained for
W^{-1}
that applies also to superradiant frequencies in the case
a=1
and with a constant that may depend on
m
:

Theorem 6.18

(Proposition 6.3 in
[
TdC20
]
)
.

Let
a=1
and
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}
with
|\omega|\geq\omega_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
W_{0}(\omega_{0},\omega_{\rm high},\epsilon_{\rm width},m)>0
such that

|\omega\widetilde{\omega}||W^{-1}|^{2}\leq W_{0}.

(6.85)

The aim of Proposition
6.19
below is to combine the homogeneous estimates from Lemma
6.13
and Proposition
6.14
with the Wronskian bound (
6.85
) and obtain estimates for solutions to the inhomogeneous ODE that are valid in the near-horizon region
r\leq 1+|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|z_{0}
.

So far, we have made no assumptions on
H
in the integrated, fixed-frequency estimates. Now, will assume that
H
can be related to
F=F_{\xi}+G
appearing on the right-hand side of (
6.7
), using that
u
can be related to
\upphi
via a Fourier transform.

Proposition 6.19
.

Let
a=1
and
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}
. Assume that
u
is a solution to (
6.9
).

Then for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small and
z_{0}
arbitrarily large, there exists a constant
C_{m}>0
such that

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}(r-1)(|u|^{2}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-2}|u^{\prime}|^{2})(r)\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq&\>C_{m}B_{\rm hom}^{4}W_{0}\left[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|G_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\right],\end{split}

(6.86)

where
\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}
denotes an indicator function.

Proof.

Let
m
be fixed. We will first estimate:

\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}|m\widetilde{\omega}|\sum_{\ell}\left|W^{-1}u_{\rm inf}(r_{0})\int_{1}^{r_{0}}u_{\rm hor}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}.

for
r_{0}-1\leq z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|
.

We can alternatively write this as

\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r_{0}-1)}\sum_{\ell}|m\widetilde{\omega}|\left|W^{-1}u_{\rm inf}(r_{0})\int_{1}^{r_{0}}u_{\rm hor}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}.

(6.87)

We first apply (
6.57
) and assumption (
6.85
) to estimate:

|m\widetilde{\omega}||W^{-1}u_{\rm inf}|^{2}(r_{0})\leq B_{\rm hom}^{2}W_{0}|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}.

By splitting
u_{\rm hor}=e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}(1+\varepsilon_{+})
according to (
6.65
), we will first exploit the oscillation of
e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}
to estimate (
6.87
).

We apply an inverse Fourier transform and a change of variables, to obtain:

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{1}^{r_{0}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+i\omega t}F\,dtdr\,e^{-im\varphi}S_{m\ell}(\theta;a\omega)d\sigma\right|^{2}\\
=&\>\frac{1}{2\pi}\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}u+im\upomega_{+}(u+v)}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{0}}F\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\,dud\tau\,e^{-im\varphi}S_{m\ell}(\theta;a\omega)d\sigma\right|^{2}.\end{split}

Hence, by applying Plancherel on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r_{0}-1)}|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\sum_{\ell}\left|\int_{1}^{r_{0}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
=&\>\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r_{0}-1)}|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}u+im\omega_{+}(u+v)}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{0}}F\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\,dud\tau\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}d\sigma.\end{split}

We now use that

|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r_{0}-1)}\leq z_{0}^{-1}(r_{0}-1)^{-1}

and we split
F=F_{\xi}+\xi G
and apply Cauchy–Schwarz in
\tau
, using that
\xi
is supported in
\tau\geq 0
and
\dot{\xi}
is supported in
\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}
to further estimate the right-hand side above and obtain

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r_{0}-1)}|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\left|\int_{1}^{r_{0}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq&\>C(r_{0}-1)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}e^{i(\widetilde{\omega}+m\omega_{+})u}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{0}}F_{\xi}\,\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}du\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}d\sigma d\tau\\
+&\>C(r_{0}-1)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}}(1+\tau^{2})^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}e^{i(\widetilde{\omega}+m\omega_{+})u}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{0}}\xi G\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\,du\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}d\sigma d\tau.\end{split}

We apply Plancherel in
u
to estimate the right-hand side further and conclude:

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\geq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r_{0}-1)}|m||\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\sum_{\ell}\left|\int_{1}^{r_{0}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq&\>C(r_{0}-1)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{0}}(|F_{\xi}|^{2}+(1+\tau^{2})^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\xi^{2}|G|^{2})\Delta^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-4}\,d\sigma dud\tau\\
\leq&\>C(r_{0}-1)^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+C(r_{0}-1)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C(r_{0}-1)^{q}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta^{\frac{1-q}{2}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+C(r_{0}-1)^{q}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta^{\frac{1-q}{2}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

(6.88)

We now estimate the contribution of
\epsilon_{+}
. We first integrate by parts to write:

\begin{split}\int_{1}^{r_{0}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\varepsilon_{+}(r)\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr=&\>\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\varepsilon_{+}(r)\frac{d}{dr}\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\\
=&\>\varepsilon_{+}(r_{0})\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\\
&-\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\frac{d\varepsilon_{+}}{dr}(r)\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr.\end{split}

(6.89)

By (
6.66
) and (
6.67
), it follows that for
r_{0}-1\leq z_{0}\frac{|\widetilde{\omega}|}{|m|}

\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{+}(r_{0})|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{m},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{m}|r_{*}|^{-1}.

Hence, the first term on the right-hand side of (
6.89
) can be bounded via (
6.88
) after integrating over
\mathcal{B}_{<}
. We estimate the second term on the right-hand side of (
6.89
) as follows:

\begin{split}\Bigg|\int_{1}^{r_{0}}&\frac{d\varepsilon_{+}}{dr}(r)\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}\,dr\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>\int_{1}^{r_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{1+q}{2}}\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{+}}{dr}(r)\right|\,dr\cdot\sup_{1\leq r\leq r_{0}}(r-1)^{-\frac{1+q}{2}}\left|\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}\right|\\
\leq&\>\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{0})}(r-1)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*})\right|\,dr_{*}\cdot\sup_{1\leq r\leq r_{0}}(r-1)^{-\frac{q}{2}}\left|\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}\right|\end{split}

which can again be bounded via (
6.88
) if we take
q>0
and integrate over
\mathcal{B}_{<}
.

We will next estimate:

\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}(r_{0}-1)|\sum_{\ell}\left|W^{-1}u_{\rm hor}(r_{0})\int_{r_{0}}^{\infty}u_{\rm inf}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}.

We apply (
6.56
) :

|u_{\rm hor}|^{2}(r_{0})\leq B_{\rm hom}^{2}.

We therefore need to estimate

\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}\sum_{\ell}\left|\int_{r_{0}}^{\infty}u_{\rm inf}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}.

(6.90)

We will use the shorthand notation
\mathfrak{w}
for
\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}
and
\check{u}_{\inf}
for
\check{u}_{\inf,\pm}
. We first integrate by parts and denote
\beta=2\Im\sqrt{2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}}
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{r_{0}}^{R_{0}}u_{\rm inf}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr=&\>\int_{r_{0}}^{R_{0}}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf}\frac{d}{dr}\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\\
=&\>(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})(R_{0})\int_{1}^{R_{0}}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\\
&-(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})(r_{0})\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\\
&-\int_{r_{0}}^{R_{0}}\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr.\end{split}

Observe that the contribution of the first two terms on the very right-hand side to the integral over
\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}\sum_{\ell}
can be estimated as above, exploiting the presence of the oscillating factor
e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}
multiplying
H
.

We estimate the third term on the right-hand side of the equation above as follows:

(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}\Bigg|\int_{r_{0}}^{R_{0}}\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\Bigg|^{2}\\
\leq(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}\left(\int_{r_{0}}^{R_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})\right|\,dr\right)^{2}\\
\times\sup_{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}}(r-1)^{-1-q}\left|\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}\right|^{2}.

By the estimates above, we obtain:

\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}(r-1)^{-1-q}\sup_{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}}\sum_{\ell}\left|\int_{1}^{r}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(|F_{\xi}|^{2}+(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\xi^{2}|G|^{2})\Delta^{\frac{1-q}{2}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

It remains to estimate the integral involving
{u}_{\rm inf}
. We apply Lemma
6.13
and the estimates (
6.71
) and (
6.73
) to obtain:

\begin{split}(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}&\Bigg(\int_{r_{0}}^{R_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}\\
\leq&\>2(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}\Bigg(\int_{r_{0}}^{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}(r-1)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}\\
&\>+2(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}\Bigg(\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{R_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{q+1}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm inf})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}\\
\leq&\>C_{m}\widetilde{\omega}(r_{0}-1)|W|^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}(r-1)^{-2+q-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,dr\\
&\times\int_{1}^{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon^{\prime}}\frac{|m|}{|\widetilde{\omega}|}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|{u}_{\rm inf}|^{2}+|{u}_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2})\,dr\\
&\>+C_{m}(r_{0}-1)|W|^{-2}\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{R_{0}}(r-1)^{-1+q-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,dr\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{R_{0}}(r-1)^{-2+\epsilon^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|{u}_{\rm inf}|^{2}+|{u}_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2})\\
\leq&\>C_{m}(r_{0}-1)^{q-\epsilon^{\prime}}W_{0}^{2}B_{\rm hom}^{2}|m\widetilde{\omega}|+C_{m}(R_{0}-1)^{q-\epsilon^{\prime}}B_{\rm hom}^{2}W_{0}^{2}\\
\leq&\>C_{m}W_{0}^{2}B_{\rm hom}^{2}\end{split}

if
q>\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.

We can finally estimate

(r_{0}-1)\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}|W|^{-2}\sum_{\ell}\left|\int_{R_{0}}^{\infty}u_{\rm inf}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}\leq C_{m}W_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C_{m}W_{0}^{2}(r_{0}-1)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}r^{2}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau,

with
R_{0}
arbitrarily large, by writing
u_{\rm inf}=e^{i\omega r_{*}}+O(r^{-1})
and exploiting oscillations as above.

We combine the above estimates and to conclude the estimate involving
u
in (
6.86
). The estimate for
u^{\prime}
can be obtained by using (
6.84
) and repeating the steps above.
∎

In the proposition below, we derive additional estimates away from the horizon, in the region
r>1+z_{0}^{-1}
with
z_{0}>0
arbitrarily small.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
a=1
and
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
and assume that the Wronskian assumption (
6.85
) holds.

Then for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small and
z_{0}
arbitrarily large, there exists a constant
C_{m}>0
such that for
r\geq 1+z_{0}^{-1}
:

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\sum_{\ell}(|u|^{2}+|\omega|^{-2}|u^{\prime}|^{2})(r)\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq&\>C_{m}B_{\rm hom}^{4}W_{0}\left[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\right].\end{split}

(6.91)

Proof.

We will make use of the identity (
6.83
) with
r>1+z_{0}^{-1}
to derive (
6.91
). We estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{<}}\sum_{\ell}(|u|^{2}+\omega^{-2}|u^{\prime}|^{2})(r_{0})\,d\widetilde{\omega}\leq&\>\int_{\mathcal{B}_{<}}\sum_{\ell}\frac{|u_{\rm inf}|^{2}(r_{0})+\omega^{-2}|u_{\rm inf}^{\prime}|^{2}(r_{0})}{|W|^{2}}\left(\int_{1}^{r_{0}}u_{\rm hor}(r)\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})(r)\,dr\right)^{2}\\
+&\>\frac{|u_{\rm hor}|^{2}+\omega^{-2}|u_{\rm hor}^{\prime}|^{2}(r_{0})}{|W|^{2}}\left(\int_{r_{0}}^{\infty}u_{\rm inf}(r)\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})(r)\,dr\right)^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}.\end{split}

(6.92)

with
r_{0}>1+z_{0}^{-1}
. The derivation of (
6.91
) now proceeds much like the derivation of (
6.86
), with the key difference being the application of Proposition
6.15
instead of Proposition
6.14
.

In particular, we integrate by parts to obtain:

\begin{split}W^{-1}&\int_{1}^{r_{0}}u_{\rm hor}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr=W^{-1}\int_{1}^{r_{0}}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor}\frac{d}{dr}\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}dr\\
=&\>W^{-1}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})(r_{0})\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}-W^{-1}\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}dr\end{split}

It follows from (
6.59
) and (
6.85
) that:

|W|^{-2}\left|e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor}\right|^{2}(r_{0})\leq CW_{0}B_{\rm hom}^{2}m^{-2}.

From the proof of Proposition
6.19
, it follows moreover that for any
1\leq r_{1}\leq r_{0}
and
q>0
:

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\sum_{\ell}(r_{1}-1)^{-1-q}\left|\int_{1}^{r_{1}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\frac{H}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr\right|^{2}\,d\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq&\>C(r_{1}-1)^{-1-q}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{1}}(|F_{\xi}|^{2}+(1+\tau^{2})^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\xi^{2}|G|^{2})\Delta^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-4}\,d\sigma dud\tau\\
\leq&\>C(r_{1}-1)^{-1-q}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}}\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+C(r_{1}-1)^{-1-q}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta^{\frac{1-q}{2}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta^{\frac{1-q}{2}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

(6.93)

Hence we can control the first term on the very right-hand side of (
6.92
). We control the second term on the very right-hand side by observing that:

\Bigg|W^{-1}\int_{1}^{r_{0}}\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}dr\Bigg|^{2}\\
\leq|W|^{-2}\left(\int_{1}^{r_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})\right|\,dr\right)^{2}\\
\times\sup_{1\leq r\leq r_{0}}(r-1)^{-1-q}\left|\int_{1}^{r}\frac{He^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}}{\Delta}(r^{\prime 2}+a^{2})\,dr^{\prime}\right|^{2}.

The second factor on the right-hand side above can be estimated via (
6.93
), whereas the first factor can be estimated as follows:

\begin{split}|W|^{-2}\Bigg(\int_{1}^{r_{0}}&(r-1)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}\\
=&\>|W|^{-2}\Bigg(\int_{1}^{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}(r-1)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}\\
&+|W|^{-2}\Bigg(\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{r_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}u_{\rm hor})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}.\end{split}

The first term on the right-hand side has already been estimated in the proof of Proposition
6.19
. The second term, we estimate further by applying Cauchy–Schwarz together with (
6.82
):

\begin{split}|W|^{-2}\Bigg(&\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{r_{0}}(r-1)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}(e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}{u}_{\rm hor})\right|\,dr\Bigg)^{2}\\
\leq&\>C|W|^{-2}\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{r_{0}}(r-1)^{-1+q-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,dr\\
&\>\times\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{r_{0}}(r-1)^{-4+\epsilon^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+(r-1)^{2})|{u}_{\rm hor}|^{2}+(r-1)^{-4+\epsilon^{\prime}}|{u}_{\rm hor}^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq&\>C_{m}B^{2}_{\rm hom}W_{0}^{2},\end{split}

if
q>\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.
∎

We now obtain the desired integrated estimate for the frequency range
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
.

Proposition 6.21
.

Let
a=1
and
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
. Then for any
\delta,\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small, there exists a constant
c=c(\epsilon,\delta,\omega_{\rm high},\eta_{\flat})>0
and
C_{m}=C_{m}(\epsilon,\delta,\omega_{\rm high},\eta_{\flat},m)>0
such that

\begin{split}c\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}&\sum_{\ell}\int_{1}^{\infty}r^{-1-\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}\left(\Lambda|u|^{2}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+(r-1)^{2}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\right)\,drd\widetilde{\omega}\\
\leq&\>\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{<}}}\int_{1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\infty}\chi_{+}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0}(\widetilde{\omega})y_{+}\Re\left(\frac{d(\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}u)}{dr}\overline{\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}H}\right)-\chi_{+}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}>0}(\widetilde{\omega})y_{-}\Re\left(\frac{d(\mathfrak{w}_{-}^{-1}u)}{dr}\overline{\mathfrak{w}_{-}^{-1}H}\right)\,drd\widetilde{\omega}\\
&+C_{m}B_{\rm hom}^{4}\left[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\right],\end{split}

(6.94)

with
\chi_{+}
a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi_{+}(r)=1
for
r\leq 1+\delta
and
\chi_{+}(r)=0
for
r\geq 1+2\delta
,
y_{+}
and
y_{-}
behave to leading order like
(r-1)^{-2\mp 2\Im\sqrt{2m^{2}-\Lambda_{+}-\frac{1}{4}}}
as
r\downarrow 1
and
\mathbf{1}_{r\geq 1+\delta}
,
\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}>0}
and
\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0}
denoting indicator functions.

Proof.

The proof proceeds in
two steps
. First, we will control the left-hand side of (
6.94
) away from
r=1
by using Proposition
6.20
. We will then derive estimates near
r=1
.

Step 1
: estimates away from
r=1

We establish control in the region
r\in[1+\delta,\infty)
, for
\delta>0
arbitrarily small by applying Proposition
6.20
:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{<}}&\sum_{\ell}\int_{1+\delta}^{\infty}r^{-1-\delta^{\prime}}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\Lambda|u|^{2})\,dr\leq C\sup_{r\geq 1+\delta}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{<}}\sum_{\ell}m^{2}(|u|^{2}+\omega^{-2}|u^{\prime}|^{2})(r)\\
\leq&\>CB^{4}_{\rm hom}W_{0}m^{2}\left[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|G_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\right].\end{split}

(6.95)

Step 2
: estimates near
r=1

We proceed exactly as in the proof of Proposition
6.14
by considering the renormalized variables
\check{u}_{\pm}=\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}^{-1}u
, which satisfies the ODE:

\displaystyle\check{u}_{\pm}^{\prime\prime}+2\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}^{-1}\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{\pm}\check{u}_{\pm}^{\prime}+\left[\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\check{V}-\frac{1}{4}(1-a^{2})^{2}(1+O(r-1))+(1-a^{2})O((r-1)^{2})\right]\check{u}_{\pm}=

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{w}_{\pm}^{-1}H

(6.96)

and we additionally keep track of the terms involving
H
. The boundary terms at
r=1+z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|
are estimated via Proposition
6.19
and we apply (
6.95
) to estimate the terms in the region where
r\in[1+\delta,1+2\delta]
.
∎

6.5.10.
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{>}
: bounded frequencies away from the superradiant threshold

In this section, we consider the case of bounded frequencies with
|m\widetilde{\omega}|>\eta_{\flat}m^{2}
. The cases
|\omega|<\omega_{\rm low}
and
|\omega|>\omega_{\rm low}
, with
\omega_{\rm low}
a suitably small constant, need to be done separately. The estimates here follow directly from analogous estimates in
[
DRSR16
]
in the case
a<1
, so we will omit their derivation.

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
0<a_{0}<a\leq 1
,
E\geq 2
and
R_{\infty}>1
arbitrarily large. Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{>}
.

(i)

For
\omega_{\rm low}
suitably small depending on
a_{0}
and
E
and
R_{\infty}
sufficiently large, there exists constants
c(a_{0}),C(a_{0})>0
and functions
y_{\flat,1},\tilde{y}_{\flat},h_{\flat},\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
, such that for
|\omega|\leq\omega_{\rm low}

c\int_{1}^{R_{\infty}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Re(iE\omega\chi_{2}u+2i\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{1}u-h_{\flat}u-2(y_{\flat,1}+\tilde{y}_{\flat})u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*},

and

\displaystyle|\tilde{y}_{\flat}|+|y_{\flat,1}|+|h_{\flat}|+|\chi_{1}|+|\chi_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C,

\displaystyle|\tilde{y}_{\flat}|\leq

\displaystyle\>Ce^{-b(r-1)}

\displaystyle y_{\flat,1}=

\displaystyle\>1\>\textnormal{and}\>h_{\flat}=0\quad\textnormal{for $r\geq R_{\infty}$}.

(ii)

Let
|\omega|>\omega_{\rm low}
. Then there exists constants
c,C>0
depending on
\omega_{\rm low},\omega_{\rm high},\epsilon_{\rm width}
and a function
y_{\flat,2}
, such that for
|\omega|>\omega_{\rm low}
:

c\int_{1}^{R_{\infty}}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr\leq E|\omega||\widetilde{\omega}||u|^{2}(1)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Re(iE\omega u-2y_{\flat,2}u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*},

with

|y_{\flat,2}|\leq C\>\textnormal{and}\>y_{\flat,2}=1\>\quad\textnormal{for $r\geq R_{\infty}$}.

Furthermore,

|\omega||\widetilde{\omega}||u|^{2}(1)\leq CB_{\rm hom}^{4}W_{0}m^{2}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1+\epsilon}r^{2}|G_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg].

(6.97)

Proof.

Part (i) and (ii) follow from
[
DRSR16
, Propositions 8.7.3]
and
[
DRSR16
, Propositions 8.7.4]
, respectively, which work without modification even when
a\uparrow 1
. In the case of (ii), we can additionally apply (
6.86
) to further estimate the boundary term at
r=1
and obtain (
6.97
).
∎

6.5.11.
Combining the frequency ranges

We can combine all the estimates the frequency ranges
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}_{\rm sr},\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\omega}_{\rm nsr},\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\Lambda}_{\rm nsr},\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\sim}_{\rm nsr},\mathcal{G}^{\flat}
together with the
(r-1)
-weighted estimates near
r=1
from §
6.5.2
.

In order to convert the resulting estimate in frequency space to an estimate in physical space, it is advantageous to minimize the need for frequency restrictions in front of the multipliers corresponding to the different frequency ranges. Since we have already closed the relevant estimates in each frequency range separately, it is becomes much easier to consider the same currents in the different frequency ranges and thus make the use of currents more uniform in frequency. However, as the proposition below demonstrates, we cannot completely remove frequency restrictions on the right-hand side of (
6.98
).

Proposition 6.23
.

Let
a=1
. Denote with
u
a solution to (
6.9
), corresponding to a fixed azimuthal mode solution
\phi_{m}
to (
3.1
) satisfying Assumptions
1
and
2
. Then, for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small, there exists a constant
c_{m}>0
such that:

\begin{split}c_{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\sum_{\ell}\int_{1}^{\infty}&\chi_{+}(1-r^{-1})^{-2+\epsilon}|u^{\prime}+i\widetilde{\omega}u|^{2}+(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}r^{-1-\epsilon}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\\
&\>+(\Lambda r^{-3}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}(1-r^{-1})^{-1}r^{-3}+\omega r^{-1-\epsilon})\zeta_{\rm trap}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}|u|^{2}\,dr\\
\leq&\>\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\sum_{\ell}\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}}\cdot\Re(\mathbf{f}\cdot(u,u^{\prime})\cdot\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
&-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\sum_{\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}}2y\Re(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}E(\chi_{K}\widetilde{\omega}+\omega)\Re(iu\overline{H})\\
&\>+(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}\chi_{+}\Re\left(v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\Delta^{-\frac{im}{2}}\overline{H}\right)\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
&\>+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\int_{1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0}y_{+}\Re\left(\frac{d(\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}u)}{dr}\overline{\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}H}\right)\\
&-\chi_{+}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}>0}y_{-}\Re\left(\frac{d(\mathfrak{w}_{-}^{-1}u)}{dr}\overline{\mathfrak{w}_{-}^{-1}H}\right)\,drd\omega\\
&\>+C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1}r^{2}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}\Delta^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}r^{-1}r^{2}|G_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau,\end{split}

(6.98)

where

\zeta_{\rm trap}(r):=1-\mathbf{1}_{r_{{\rm trap},1}\leq r\leq r_{{\rm trap},2}}(r),

and we can take
r_{{\rm trap},i}
, with
i=1,2
, arbitrarily close to
1+\sqrt{2}
. The term
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}}\cdot\Re(\mathbf{f}\cdot(u,u^{\prime})\cdot\overline{H})
is schematic notation for products involving
u
or
u^{\prime}
with
\overline{H}
and a function
\mathbf{f}
belonging to the set:

\mathbf{f}\in\{f,f_{\sim},\hat{y},\tilde{y}_{\flat},{y}_{\flat,1},{y}_{\flat,2},h,h_{\flat},\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\},

where the functions
\mathbf{f}
are bounded near
r=1
.
The factor
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}}
denotes an indicator function supported on one of the frequency ranges
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}_{\rm sr},\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\omega}_{\rm nsr},\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\Lambda}_{\rm nsr},\mathcal{G}^{\sharp,\sim}_{\rm nsr},\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
, or
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{>}
. All the relevant functions are precisely defined in §§
6.5.2
–
6.5.10
.

Proof.

To obtain control over
(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}r^{-1-\epsilon}|u^{\prime}|^{2}
and
\widetilde{\omega}^{2}(1-r^{-1})^{-1}
near
r=1
and

r^{-1-\delta}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2})+r^{-3}\Lambda|u|^{2}

as required to estimate the left-hand side of (
6.98
), we apply the
y
-current from the proof of Proposition
6.10
for all admissible frequencies and then apply the already established estimates for each frequency range to absorb any terms with a bad sign.

We can remove the frequency restrictions in the
T
-energy and
K
-energy currents by also considering
T
- and
K
-energy currents (with cut-offs) for
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
and absorbing the resulting boundary terms using the estimates already established for
\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
.

Similarly, we apply the
(r-1)
-weighted estimates with
p=1-\epsilon
for all frequencies and absorb the resulting error terms using the already established estimates. Note that we do note entirely remove the restriction to frequency ranges on the right-hand side of (
6.98
) in the estimates involving
f
,
y
and
h
-currents, as indicated by the schematic notation
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}}
.

Finally, we note that the estimates involving the current
\check{j}^{y_{\pm}}[\check{u}_{\rm inf}]
can be extended to frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)\notin\mathcal{G}^{\flat}_{<}
in the region
(r-1)\geq z_{0}|m|^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|
. This restriction is the reason why we need to include the indicator function
\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}
on the right-hand side of (
6.98
).
∎

7.
Integrated energy estimates

In this section, we will convert the integrated estimates in
frequency space
from Proposition
6.23
to local integrated energy estimates in
physical space
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\phi_{m}
be a fixed azimuthal mode solution to (
3.1
) satisfying Assumptions
1
and
2
. Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a
C_{m}=C_{m}(m,\mathbbm{h},M,W_{0},\epsilon)>0
such that:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[\chi_{+}(1-Mr^{-1})^{2-\epsilon}|X\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}+(1-Mr^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}r^{1-\epsilon}|{\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}\\
+(1-Mr^{-1})^{\epsilon}(r^{-1}|\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}+\zeta_{\rm trap}(1-Mr^{-1})^{-1}r^{-1}|K\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}+\zeta_{\rm trap}r^{1-\epsilon}|T\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}+r^{-1}\zeta_{\rm trap}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2})]d\sigma dr\,d\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\left[(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}|X\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}+|T\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}_{m}|^{2}\right]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}+r^{1+\delta}]|G_{m}|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau,

(7.1)

with
\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}+1}
the indicator function supported in
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}+1
.

Proof.

For the sake of notational convenience, we will omit the hat in the notation
\widehat{\phi}
,
\widehat{\psi}
and simply write
\phi
and
\psi
, respectively. We set
r_{+}=1
. The key ingredients in the derivation of (
7.1
) are the estimate (
6.98
), Plancherel identities and the local energy estimate from Proposition
3.3
. Let
g_{i}:(1,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
with
i=1,2,3
be arbitrary continuous functions and
w:(1,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
a weight function. By Plancherel:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geq|m|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}}\Re((g_{1}+i\omega g_{2}+i\widetilde{\omega}g_{3})u\cdot\overline{u})+g_{4}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr_{*}d\omega=\int_{\mathcal{R}}g_{1}|\uppsi_{m}|^{2}+g_{2}\Re(T\uppsi_{m}\cdot\uppsi_{m})

(7.2)

\displaystyle\>+g_{3}\Re(K\uppsi_{m}\cdot\uppsi_{m})+g_{4}|{\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\uppsi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drdt,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geq|m|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}}w(r)|H|^{2}\,dr_{*}=\int_{\mathcal{R}}(r^{2}+1)^{-2}w(r)\Delta|\rho^{2}F_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drdt

(7.3)

and

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\sum_{\ell\geq|m|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}}\Re((g_{1}+i\omega g_{2}+i\widetilde{\omega}g_{3})u\cdot\overline{H})+\Re(g_{4}u^{\prime}\cdot\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
=\int_{\mathcal{R}}(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{1}\Re(\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}_{m})+(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{2}\Re(T\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}_{m})+(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{3}\Re(K\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}_{m})\\
+(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{4}\Re({\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}_{m})\,d\sigma drdt.

(7.4)

Note moreover that
dt=d\tau+\left(\mathbbm{h}-\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+1}\right)dr
. Hence the left-hand side of (
6.98
) controls:

\tilde{c}_{m}\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{I}\}}\Big[(1-Mr^{-1})^{2+\epsilon}|X\phi_{m}|^{2}+(1-Mr^{-1})^{\epsilon}(r^{-2}|\phi_{m}|^{2}\\
+\zeta_{\rm trap}(1-Mr^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}|K\phi_{m}|^{2}+r^{-2}\zeta_{\rm trap}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{m}|^{2})\Big]d\sigma dr\,d\tau

(7.5)

for some suitably small constant
\tilde{c}_{m}
.

We will now estimate the terms involving restrictions to different frequency ranges

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\sum_{\ell\geq|m|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}}\cdot\Re(\mathbf{f}\cdot(u,u^{\prime})\cdot\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega

by a weighted energy along
\Sigma_{0}
. Since these terms involve indicator functions restricting to subsets of frequencies, we cannot apply (
7.4
).

We will encounter in particular terms as above involving the function
g_{1}
, with
g_{1}
compactly supported and
|g_{1}|\leq C(1-r^{-1})^{2}
. Hence, we can apply Young’s inequality to conclude that there exists an
R>1
such that

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}}\Re(g_{1}u\cdot\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\right|\leq\int_{1}^{R}\eta(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}|u|^{2}+C\eta^{-1}(1-r^{-1})^{-\epsilon}|H|^{2}\,dr.

By (
7.2
), we can estimate

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\sum_{\ell\geq|m|}\int_{1}^{R}(1-r^{-1})^{-\epsilon}|H|^{2}\,dr\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(1-r^{-1})^{2-\epsilon}(|F_{\xi}|^{2}+|G_{m}|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Applying Proposition
3.3
with
p=0
, gives moreover:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(1-r^{-1})^{2-\epsilon}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|G_{m}|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

We will also encounter terms involving
g_{4}
, with
g_{4}
either compactly supported or exponentially decaying in
r
, which can be similarly estimated via Young’s inequality, as above.

We consider now the integral of
E\omega\Re(iu\overline{H})
, which is supported on all frequency ranges, so we can apply Plancherel in the form (
7.4
). We then split via (
6.8
)

\int_{\mathcal{R}}(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{2}\Re(T\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}_{\xi})\,d\sigma drdt=\int_{\mathcal{R}}-2g_{2}\Re(T(\xi\psi_{m})\cdot T(\xi)\overline{X\psi_{m}})\,d\sigma drdt\\
+\int_{\mathcal{R}}\Re(T\upphi_{m}\cdot[O(1)T(\xi)\overline{X\psi_{m}}+O(1)T(\xi)\overline{T\phi_{m}}+O(1)mT(\xi)\overline{\phi_{m}}+O(1)T(\xi)\overline{\phi_{m}}+O(1)T^{2}(\xi)\overline{\phi_{m}})\,d\sigma drdt.

The second integral on the right-hand side above can easily be estimated via Proposition
3.3
with
p=0
. We rewrite the first integral on the right-hand side above:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathcal{R}}&-2g_{2}\Re(T(\xi\psi_{m})\cdot T(\xi)\overline{X\psi_{m}})\,d\sigma drdt=\int_{\mathcal{R}}-g_{2}X(T(\xi)^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2})-g_{2}T(\xi^{2})\Re(T\psi_{m}\cdot\overline{X\psi_{m}})\,d\sigma drdt\\
=&\>\int_{\mathcal{R}}-X\left(g_{2}T(\xi)^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}g_{2}T(\xi^{2})T(|\psi_{m}|^{2})\right)+g_{2}T(\xi^{2})\Re(XT\psi_{m}\cdot\overline{\psi_{m}})\\
&\>+\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}\left[T(\xi)^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}T(\xi^{2})T(|\psi_{m}|^{2})\right]\,d\sigma drdt.\end{split}

Note that
\frac{dg_{2}}{dr}=0
in this case. We use the compact support of
T(\xi)
in
\tau
and the fundamental theorem of calculus to infer that the total
X
-derivative can be controlled by the boundary terms

\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}}e^{-\tau}|\psi_{m}|^{2}+|T\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r=1\}}|\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}}e^{-\tau}|\psi_{m}|^{2}+|K\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau,

where the exponential weight is chosen for convenience.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus and a Hardy inequality, we can control the second integral above by

\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq R\}}|X\psi_{m}||\psi_{m}|\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}\leq\{r\leq R\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\phi]\,d\sigma dr.

Using that
\psi_{m}
is compactly supported along
\Sigma_{0}
, we can also apply a Hardy inequality in
\tau
to estimate the above boundary term along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
by
\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}}|T\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau
and the boundary term along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
by

\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}}|K\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r=1\}}|\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma.

The integrals of the time derivatives along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and
\mathcal{I}^{+}
can then be estimated via Proposition
3.3
with
p=0
.

Finally, the term involving a factor
XT\psi_{m}
can now be estimated by using that
\square_{g}\psi_{m}=G_{m}
and applying (
3.3
). The resulting terms that do not involve
G_{m}
can then be estimated straightforwardly by integrating by parts in the
X
- and
T
-directions and applying Proposition
3.3
with
p=0
. The term involving
G_{m}
leads to the following additional term on the right-hand side:

\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|\rho^{2}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

When estimating the integral

\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R\}}(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{2}\Re(T\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}G_{m})\,d\sigma drdt\right|,

we cannot make use of compact support in
\tau
. Instead, we apply Young’s inequality to control this integral by

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\eta r^{1-\delta}|T\phi_{m}|^{2}+C\eta^{-1}r^{1+\delta}|rG_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau,

The integral of
\chi_{K}\Re(i\widetilde{\omega}\psi_{m}\cdot\overline{H})
can be estimated in an analogous way, with the role of
T
taken on by
K
and where we apply (
3.5
) instead of (
3.3
). The only difference here, is that we estimate the integral

\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{3}\Re(K\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}G_{m})\,d\sigma drdt\right|,

via Young’s inequality by

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}\eta(r-1)^{\epsilon-1}|K\phi_{m}|^{2}+C\eta^{-1}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|G_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We now turn to the integral of
-2y\Re(u^{\prime}\overline{H})
. We apply Plancherel in the form (
7.4
) with the function
g_{4}=-2y
and we use that
|g_{4}|\leq C
to infer that the integral is bounded by:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathcal{R}}&(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{4}\Re({\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}_{\xi})\,d\sigma drdt\\
\leq&\>C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1}|{\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\psi_{m}|(|\rho^{2}F_{\xi}|+|\rho^{2}G_{m}|)\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\eta(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}r^{-1-\epsilon}|{\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\psi_{m}|^{2}\\
+&\>\eta^{-1}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}r^{-1+\epsilon}|\rho^{2}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

We can absorb the term with the factor
\eta
for
\eta
suitably small and estimate the integral in
\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}
via Proposition
3.3
with
p=0
.

Consider now the integral of
(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}\chi_{+}\Re\left(v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\Delta^{-\frac{im}{2}}\overline{H}\right)
. Recall the definition of
v
for
a=1
:

v=e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\Delta^{\frac{im}{2}}u.

Hence

v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}\Delta^{-\frac{im}{2}}\overline{H}=\left[u^{\prime}+i\widetilde{\omega}u-im(r^{2}+1)^{-1}(r-1)u\right]\cdot\overline{H}.

Note moreover that

{\mathfrak{X}}_{*}=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+1}Y+K-\frac{r-1+\frac{1}{2}(r-1)^{2}}{r^{2}+1}\Phi.

By Plancherel in the form of (
7.4
) with
g_{1}=im(r^{2}+1)^{-1}(r-1)
,
g_{3}=1
and
g_{4}=1
we can therefore estimate the corresponding integral by:

\int_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Re\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+1}Y\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F}\right)+O((r-1)^{1+\epsilon})\Re(\Phi\uppsi_{m}\cdot\rho^{2}\overline{F})\,d\sigma drdt,

with
\chi_{+}
supported in
\{r\leq R_{+}-1\}
.

This integral can be further controlled by:

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\infty}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r-1)^{1+\epsilon}(|X\psi_{m}|+|T\psi_{m}|+|\Phi\psi_{m}|)(|F_{\xi}|+|G_{m}|)\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\phi_{m}]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&\>+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}\eta(r-1)^{2+\epsilon}(|X\psi_{m}|^{2}+|K\psi_{m}|^{2}+|\Phi\psi_{m}|^{2})+C\eta^{-1}|G_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau,\end{split}

where, as above, we absorb the term with a factor
\eta
and estimate the integral in
\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}
via Proposition
3.3
with
p=1-\epsilon
.

Finally, we will estimate

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\int_{1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0}\chi_{+}y_{+}\Re\left(\frac{d(\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}u)}{dr}\overline{\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}H}\right)\,drd\omega.

The integral involving
\mathfrak{w}_{-}
can be estimated analogously. We would like to apply Plancherel (
7.4
) with a suitable function
g_{4}
, but due to the presence of
\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0}
, we need to replace
\uppsi_{m}
on the right-hand side of (
7.4
) with the convolution:

\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{\omega}(\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0})*\uppsi_{m},

where
\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{\omega}
denotes the inverse Fourier transform with respect to the frequency
\omega
.

Note that for
m<0
,

\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{\omega}(\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0})(t,r)=\frac{e^{i\frac{m}{2}t}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}t}\,d\widetilde{\omega}=\frac{e^{i\frac{m}{2}t}}{i\sqrt{2\pi}t}(1-e^{iz_{0}^{-1}m(r-1)t}).

For
m>0
, we similarly obtain:

\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{\omega}(\mathbf{1}_{|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)}\mathbf{1}_{m\widetilde{\omega}<0})(t,r)=-\frac{e^{i\frac{m}{2}t}}{i\sqrt{2\pi}t}(1-e^{iz_{0}^{-1}m(r-1)t}).

By Plancherel, we need to control

\int_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{+}y_{+}\Re\left({\mathfrak{X}}_{*}\left(\mathfrak{w}_{+}^{-1}\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}\right)\cdot\overline{\mathfrak{w}_{+}}^{-1}\rho^{2}\overline{F}\right)\,d\sigma drdt,

which can be estimated by

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r-1)^{-1}((r-1)^{2}|X\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|+|m|(r-1)|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|)(|F_{\xi}|+|G_{m}|)\,d\sigma drd\tau,

where we moreover used that
|K\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|^{2}\leq z_{0}^{-2}m^{2}(r-1)^{2}|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|
by
|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq z_{0}^{-1}|m|(r-1)
.

Note that
|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|
can be estimated as follows: by Cauchy–Schwarz together with

|1-e^{iz_{0}^{-1}m(r-1)s}|\leq C|m|(r-1)\quad\textnormal{for $|s|\leq 1$}

we obtain for
0\leq\tau\leq 1
:

\begin{split}|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)\leq&\>\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|s|}|1-e^{iz_{0}^{-1}m(r-1)s}||\uppsi_{m}|(\tau-s,r,\theta,\varphi)\,ds\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\>Cm^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-1,1)}|s|^{-2}\,ds\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r-1)^{2}\xi^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau-s,r,\theta,\varphi)\,ds\\
+&\>C\int_{-1}^{1}\xi^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau-s,r,\theta,\varphi)\,ds\\
\leq&\>C\int_{0}^{\infty}(r-1)^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime}+C\int_{0}^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime}.\end{split}

Similarly,

\begin{split}|X\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)\leq&\>C\int_{0}^{\infty}(r-1)^{2}|X\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime}+C\int_{0}^{2}|X\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime}.\end{split}

Therefore

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r-1)^{-1}((r-1)^{2}|X\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|+|m|(r-1)|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|)|F_{\xi}|\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>\eta\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}m^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}+(r-1)^{1+\epsilon}|X\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>\eta\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r-1)^{1+\epsilon}m^{4}|\psi_{m}|^{2}+(r-1)^{3+\epsilon}|X\psi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}C\eta^{-1}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|F_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

By taking
\eta
suitably small (depending also on
|m|
) and applying a Hardy inequality to control the
(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}m^{2}|\psi_{m}|^{2}
term by the
(r-1)^{1+\epsilon}|X\psi_{m}|^{2}
, we can either absorb the terms with a factor
\eta
or control them via the local energy estimates via Proposition
3.3
with
p=1-\epsilon
. The
F_{\xi}
term can similarly be estimated via Proposition
3.3
with
p=1+\epsilon
.

To estimate the terms involving
|G_{m}|
, we use that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|^{2}(t,r,\theta,\varphi)\,dt\leq&\>\int_{0}^{\infty}|\psi_{m}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau,\end{split}

so

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\infty}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}(r-1)^{-1}((r-1)^{2}|X\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|+|m|(r-1)|\uppsi_{m}^{\rm conv}|)|G_{m}|\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{+}\}}\eta(r-1)^{2+\epsilon}|X\psi_{m}|^{2}+\eta m^{2}(r-1)^{\epsilon}|\psi_{m}|^{2}+C\eta^{-1}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|G_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

We absorb the terms with a factor
\eta
.
∎

We can apply (
7.1
) to obtain different (higher-order) integrated energy estimates in different spacetime regions.

Theorem 7.2
.

Assume that (
6.85
) holds and let
0<\epsilon<1
. Let
\phi
supported on azimuthal modes with azimuthal number
|m|\leq m_{0}
.

(i)

There exists a
C=C(m_{0},\mathbbm{h},M,\epsilon)>0
such that such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq 2M\}}(1-Mr^{-1})^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\phi]+(1-Mr^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}|K\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

(7.6)

(ii)

Let
M<r_{H}<2M<r_{I}
. Then there exists a
C=C(m,\mathbbm{h},M,\epsilon,r_{H},r_{I})>0
such that such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(|\phi|^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{2}[\phi])\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

(7.7)

(iii)

Let
M<r_{H}<2M
and
4M<r_{I}<\infty
. Denote with
Z
an element of
\{(1-Mr^{-1})\underline{L},rL,\Phi,\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\}
. Then there exists a
C=C(\mathbbm{h},M,W_{0},\epsilon,r_{H},r_{I})>0
such that such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\sum_{|I|\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{I}-M\leq r\leq r_{I}\}\cup\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+M\}}(|Z^{I}\phi|^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{2}[Z^{I}\phi])\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[T^{k}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

(7.8)

Proof.

Let
V
be a timelike vector field in
\{r>M\}
such that
V=L+(r-M)^{-1+\epsilon}\underline{L}
for
r\leq 2M
and
V=T
for
r\geq 2\frac{1}{4}M
. Since the ergoregion is contained in
\{r\leq 2M\}
, such a vector field exists. Since
V
is Killing,
\nabla_{\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\mu\nu}[\phi]V_{\nu})
is supported in
\{r\leq 2\frac{1}{4}M\}
.

We can write

V=K+O_{1}(r-M)\Phi+\frac{1}{4M^{2}}(r-M)^{1+\epsilon}(1+O_{1}((r-M)^{1-\epsilon})\underline{Y}
