|\nabla_{\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\mu\nu}[\phi]V_{\nu})|\leq C(r-M)^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\phi]+|K\phi||\Phi\phi|\leq C(r-M)^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\phi]+(r-1)^{-\epsilon}|K\phi|^{2}

By construction,
\zeta_{\rm trap}=1
in this region. We can therefore apply (
7.1
) with
\Sigma_{0}
replaced by
\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}
to control the spacetime integral of
|\nabla_{\mu}(\mathbb{T}^{\mu\nu}[\phi]V_{\nu})|
and conclude (
7.6
).

Consider now (ii). Since the integral to be estimated includes values of
r
for which
\zeta_{\rm trap}
vanishes, the left-hand side of (
7.1
) fails to control
|T\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}
. By replacing
\phi
with
T\phi
, we can obtain control over
|T\phi|^{2}
without degeneracy at the expense of an extra
T
-derivative on the right-hand side. Since the degeneracy of
\zeta_{\rm trap}
occurs outside the ergoregion where
T
is timelike, we can apply standard elliptic estimates to control also obtain moreover
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}
. Since
|m|
is bounded, we can equivalently express the estimate in terms of extra
K
-derivatives on the right-hand side and we obtain (
7.7
).

Finally, (
7.8
) follows by observing that the integration region is bounded, excludes the values of
r
for which
\zeta_{\rm trap}
vanishes and lies strictly outside the event horizon. The result then follows from the observation that commutation with
K
and
\Phi
controls all other derivatives via standard elliptic estimates.
∎

8.
r
-weighted and
(r-M)^{-1}
-weighted energy estimates

In this section we derive additional
r
-weighted estimates in far-away regions and
(r-M)^{-1}
-weighted energy estimates near the event horizon. These weighted estimates will play a key role in deriving quantitative energy decay estimates and the powers appearing in the weight factors determine the corresponding decay rates. We will assume that
|a|=M
in this section.

Throughout this section and §
9
,
\phi
will denote a solution to (
3.1
) such that Theorem
7.2
applies to
\phi
.
We will later apply these estimates to
\widehat{\phi}
(and its
K
-integral) instead of
\phi
.

8.1.
Estimates near
\mathcal{H}^{+}

We first consider
(r-M)^{-1}
-weighted energy estimates near the event horizon. These rely on the following key identity:

Proposition 8.1
.

The following identity holds in
M\leq r\leq r_{H}
for solutions
\psi
to (
3.6
):

K\Bigg[\left(1-\frac{\mathbbm{h}\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r-M)^{-2-p}|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}+\mathbbm{h}\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{4}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\\
+\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}|K\psi|^{2}+(r-M)^{-p}O((|\underline{L}\psi|+(r-M)|\Phi\psi|+\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)|K\psi|)^{2})\Bigg]\\
+\widetilde{X}\Bigg[(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(r-M)^{-p}|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}-\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{4}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}|K\psi|^{2}\\
+(r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)\Bigg]+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+\left[\frac{p(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{2}(r-M)^{-1-p}+O((r-M)^{-p})\right]|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}+\frac{2-p}{8}(r-M)^{1-p}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{8(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{1-p}|K\psi|^{2}\\
=+(r-M)^{-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)+O((r-M)^{2-p})|\Phi\psi|^{2}+(r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||G|)+(r-M)^{-p}O(|\underline{L}\psi||G|).

(8.1)

Proof.

We multiply both sides of (
3.6
) with
-\frac{1}{2}(r-M)^{2-p}\overline{\underline{L}\psi}
to obtain:

-\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{2}\Re(\rho^{2}G\overline{\underline{L}\psi})=\overbrace{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(r-M)^{-p}\Re(L\underline{L}\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})}^{=:J_{1}}\overbrace{-\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(K^{2}\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})}^{=:J_{2}}\\
\overbrace{-a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(K\Phi\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})}^{=:J_{3}}\overbrace{-\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{2}\Re((\not{\mathcal{D}}-2a\upomega_{+}\Phi^{2})\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})}^{=:J_{4}}\\
\overbrace{-\frac{ar(r-M)^{2-p}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Re(\Phi\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})}^{=:J_{5}}+\overbrace{\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{2r\Delta}\frac{d}{dr}\left(r^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3}\Delta^{2}\right)\Re(\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})}^{=:J_{6}}.

(8.2)

We start by rewriting
J_{1}
, using that
L(r)=\frac{\Delta}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})}
:

\begin{split}J_{1}=&\>2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(r-M)^{-p}\Re(L\underline{L}\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(r-M)^{-p}L(|\underline{L}\psi|^{2})\\
=&\>L((r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(r-M)^{-p}|\underline{L}\psi|^{2})+\left[\frac{p(r^{2}+a^{2})}{2}(r-M)^{1-p}+O((r-M)^{2-p})\right]|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}.\end{split}

Now, we turn to
J_{2}
, using that
\underline{L}(r)=-\frac{\Delta}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})}
:

\begin{split}J_{2}=&\>-\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(K^{2}\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})\\
=&\>K\left((r-M)^{2-p}\sin^{2}\theta O(|K\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)\right)+\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}\underline{L}\left(|K\psi|^{2}\right)\\
=&\>K\left((r-M)^{2-p}\sin^{2}\theta O(|K\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)\right)+\underline{L}\left(\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}|K\psi|^{2}\right)\\
+&\>\frac{1}{8(r^{2}+a^{2})}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{3-p}|K\psi|^{2}\end{split}

To rewrite
J_{3}
, we will use the schematic notation
\Phi(\ldots)
and
\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)
to indicate total
\Phi
-derivative terms and divergence terms on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, respectively, which both vanish after integration on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. We will moreover use that:

K=L+\underline{L}-a\frac{r^{2}-r_{+}^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})}\Phi.

We then obtain

\begin{split}J_{3}=&\>-a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(K\Phi\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})\\
=&\>-K\left(a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(\Phi\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})\right)+a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(\Phi\psi K\overline{\underline{L}\psi})\\
=&\>K\left((r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)\right)+a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(\Phi\psi L\overline{\underline{L}\psi})\\
&\>+a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(\Phi\psi\overline{\underline{L}^{2}\psi})+\Phi(\ldots).\end{split}

We estimate the term involving
L\underline{L}\psi
by applying (
3.6
) again:

a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(\Phi\psi L\overline{\underline{L}\psi})=a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)O((r-M)^{4-p})\Big[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\Re(\Phi\psi\overline{K^{2}\psi})\\
+2a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)\Re(\Phi\psi\overline{K\Phi\psi})+\Re(\Phi\psi\overline{(\not{\mathcal{D}}-2a\upomega_{+}\Phi^{2})\psi})+\Re(\Phi\psi\rho^{2}\overline{G})\Big]\\
+O((r-M)^{4-p})|\Phi\psi|^{2}+\Phi(\ldots).

Hence,

a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)(r-M)^{2-p}\Re(\Phi\psi L\overline{\underline{L}\psi})=K((r-M)^{4-p}O(|\Phi\psi|(\sin^{2}\theta|K\psi|+|\Phi\psi|))+\Phi(\ldots)\\
+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)+O((r-M)^{4-p})|\Phi\psi|^{2}+O((r-M)^{4-p})|\Phi\psi||G|.

We conclude that

J_{3}=K((r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi|((r-M)^{2}\sin^{2}\theta|K\psi|+(r-M)^{2}|\Phi\psi|+|\underline{L}\psi|))+O((r-M)^{4-p})|\Phi\psi|^{2}\\
+\underline{L}((r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|))+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)+O((r-M)^{4-p})|\Phi\psi|^{2}+(r-M)^{3-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)\\
+(r-M)^{4-p}O(|\Phi\psi||G|).

We integrate by parts on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
to rewrite
J_{4}
as follows:

\begin{split}J_{4}=&\>-\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{2}\Re((\not{\mathcal{D}}-2a\upomega_{+}\Phi^{2})\psi\overline{\underline{L}\psi})\\
=&\>\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{4}\underline{L}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\\
=&\>\underline{L}\left[\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{4}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\right]+\frac{2-p}{8}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}(r-M)^{1-p}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\\
&+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

Since
J_{5}
only involves first-order derivatives, we can directly write:

J_{5}=(r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|).

This term cannot be absorbed into the non-negative definite terms in
J_{1}-J_{6}
!
For this reason, the spacetime integral of this term cannot be estimated directly and is the
key source of difficulties
for obtaining
(r-M)
-weighted estimates near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
in extremal Kerr.

We can also immediately write:

J_{6}=(r-M)^{3-p}O(|\psi||\underline{L}\psi|).

In order to integrate in spacetime, we combine all
J_{1}--J_{6}
and we express
\underline{L}
and
L
as follows in terms of
K
and
\widetilde{X}
:

\displaystyle\underline{L}=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\widetilde{X}-\mathbbm{h}K),

\displaystyle L=

\displaystyle\>\left(1-\frac{\mathbbm{h}\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)K+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\widetilde{X}+a\frac{r^{2}-r_{+}^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})}\Phi.

We obtain:

\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\sum_{i=1}^{6}J_{i}=K\Bigg[\left(1-\frac{\mathbbm{h}\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}(r-M)^{-2-p}|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}\\
+\mathbbm{h}\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{4}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\\
+\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}|K\psi|^{2}+(r-M)^{-p}O((|\underline{L}\psi|+(r-M)|\Phi\psi|+\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)|K\psi|)^{2})\Bigg]\\
+\widetilde{X}\Bigg[(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(r-M)^{-p}|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}-\frac{(r-M)^{2-p}}{4}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{2-p}|K\psi|^{2}\\
+(r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)\Bigg]+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+\left[\frac{p(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{2}(r-M)^{-1-p}+O((r-M)^{-p})\right]|\underline{L}\psi|^{2}+\frac{2-p}{8}(r-M)^{1-p}\left(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+2a\upomega_{+}|\Phi\psi|^{2}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{8(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(r-M)^{1-p}|K\psi|^{2}+(r-M)^{-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)+O((r-M)^{2-p})|\Phi\psi|^{2}\\
+(r-M)^{2-p}O(|\Phi\psi||G|).

Then (
8.1
) follows immediately.
∎

The following corollary allows us to conveniently characterize solutions to (
3.1
) with
G=0
that satisfy the qualitative integrability properties needed to apply the frequency-localized estimates in §
6
.

Corollary 8.2
.

Assume that there exists a
r_{H}>M
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}|\phi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau

\displaystyle\><\infty,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r=r_{H}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{m}|^{2}+|K\phi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau

\displaystyle\><\infty.

Then, for
r_{H}
suitably small,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}|\underline{L}\phi_{m}|^{2}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\,d\sigma drd\tau<\infty.

Proof.

We integrate (
8.1
) with
p=1
and
G=0
in
\mathcal{R}
and use that the initial data for
\phi_{m}
is smooth and compactly supported to conclude that the integral along
\Sigma_{0}
is finite. We moreover apply Young’s inequality to absorb the integral of
(r-M)^{-p}O(|\Phi\psi||\underline{L}\psi|)
into the integrals of
|\underline{L}\phi_{m}|^{2}
and
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{m}|^{2}
.
∎

Before we apply Proposition
8.1
to derive weighted energy estimates near
r=M
, we will derive some commutation relations that we will need when considering appropriate, weighted, higher-order energies.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
M=1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Denote
Z=\frac{r-1}{2(r^{2}+1)}Y
. Then we have in the region
r\leq 2
:

\left[Z^{N},-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}\right](\cdot)=-\frac{N}{4}(1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\left(-\frac{r^{2}+1}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N-1}(\cdot)\right)+\frac{N(r^{2}+1)}{2}Z^{N+1}(\cdot)\\
+\sum_{k=1}^{N}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}(\cdot)+\sum_{k=1}^{N}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}\Phi(\cdot)+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}((r-1)^{-2})L\underline{L}Z^{k}(\cdot)

(8.3)

Proof.

We will prove (
8.3
) via an induction argument. We will first establish (
8.3
) for
N=1
. Let
f
be an arbitrary function on
\mathcal{R}
. Then we can express:

\begin{split}-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}f=&\>\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}\left(T+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi+\frac{(r-1)^{2}}{2(r^{2}+1)}Y\right)\frac{(r-1)^{2}}{2(r^{2}+1)}Yf\\
=&\>\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}\left(T+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi+(r-1)Z\right)((r-1)Zf)\\
=&\>\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{r-1}\left(T+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi+(r-1)Z^{2}f+\frac{r-1}{2(r^{2}+1)}Zf\right).\end{split}

Acting with
Z
on both sides of the equation above, we obtain:

Z\left(-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}f\right)=-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Zf-\frac{1}{4}(1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\left(-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}f\right)+\frac{r^{2}+1}{2}Z^{2}f\\
+O_{\infty}(1)Zf+O_{\infty}(1)\Phi f.

Now we take our that (
8.3
) holds with
N
replaced by
k
and for all
1\leq k\leq N
. By applying (
8.3
), we obtain

ZZ^{N}\left(-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}f\right)=Z\left(-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N}f\right)-\frac{N}{4}Z\left((1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\left(-\frac{r^{2}+1}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N-1}(\cdot)\right)\right)\\
+Z\left(\frac{N(r^{2}+1)}{2}Z^{N+1}f\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{N+1}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}f+\sum_{k=1}^{N+1}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}\Phi f+\sum_{k=0}^{N}O_{\infty}((r-1)^{-2})L\underline{L}Z^{k}f

Subsequently, we apply (
8.3
) holds with
N
replaced by
1\leq k\leq N
to conclude that:

ZZ^{N}\left(-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}f\right)=-\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N+1}f-\frac{N+1}{4}(1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\left(-\frac{r^{2}+1}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N}f\right)\\
+\frac{(N+1)(r^{2}+1)}{2}Z^{N+2}f+\sum_{k=1}^{N+1}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}f+\sum_{k=1}^{N+1}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}\Phi f+\sum_{k=0}^{N}O_{\infty}((r-1)^{-2})L\underline{L}Z^{k}f.

so (
8.3
) holds also with
N
replaced by
N+1
, which concludes the induction argument.
∎

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
Z\in\{K,\Phi,(r-M)Y\}
. Then for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
0<p\leq 2
, there exists
r_{H}
suitably small and a constant
C=C(r_{H},\mathbbm{h},p,N)>0
, such that:

\begin{split}\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[Z^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{p-1}\mathcal{E}_{0}[Z^{N}\phi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[Z^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r=r_{H}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Z^{k}\phi|^{2}+|KZ^{k}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>C\sum_{0\leq k+l\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{1-p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N+1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{1-p}|K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\end{split}

(8.4)

or, alternatively,

\begin{split}\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[Z^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{1-p}\mathcal{E}_{0}[Z^{N}\phi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[Z^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r=r_{H}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Z^{k}\phi|^{2}+|KZ^{k}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>C\sum_{0\leq k+l\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{1-p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{-1-p}|\underline{L}K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\phi|^{2}+(r-M)^{1-p}|K^{k_{1}+1}\Phi^{k_{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

(8.5)

Proof.

Suppose
N=0
. Then the estimates follows directly by integrating (
8.1
). Furthermore, for any
k_{1},k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we can replace
\psi
by
K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\psi
and apply (
8.1
) again, since
\Phi
and
K
are Killing.
Now let
Z=(r-M)^{-1}\underline{L}=\frac{r-M}{2(r^{2}+M^{2})}Y
and consider
Z^{N}\phi
. By acting with
Z^{N}
on both sides of (
3.6
), we obtain

Z^{N}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{2}G)=-4Z^{N}\left(\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}\psi\right)+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta K^{2}Z^{N}\psi+2a(1-a\upomega_{+}\sin^{2}\theta)K\Phi Z^{N}\psi\\
+(\not{\mathcal{D}}-2a\upomega_{+}\Phi^{2})Z^{N}\psi+\sum_{k\leq N}O(1)\Phi Z^{k}\psi+O((r-M))\sum_{k\leq N}Z^{k}\psi.

(8.6)

For the sake of convenience, we now set
M=1
. By (
8.3
), we can write:

-4Z^{N}\left(\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}\psi\right)=-4\frac{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N}\psi-N(1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\left(-\frac{r^{2}+1}{\Delta}L\underline{L}Z^{N-1}\psi\right)\\
+2N(r^{2}+1)(r-1)^{-1}\underline{L}Z^{N}\psi+\sum_{k=1}^{N}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}\psi+\sum_{k=1}^{N}O_{\infty}(1)Z^{k}\Phi\psi+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}((r-1)^{-2})L\underline{L}Z^{k}\psi.

(8.7)

We then proceed inductively by assuming that the estimates (
8.4
) and (
8.5
) hold and then considering the case where
N
is replaced by
N+1
. We then proceed as in the proof of Proposition
8.1
by multiplying both sides of (
8.6
) by
-\frac{1}{2}(r-M)^{2-p}\overline{\underline{L}Z^{N+1}\psi}
and taking the real part.

We use (
3.6
) and (
8.6
) to express
L\underline{L}Z^{k}\psi
with
k\leq N
in terms of
T
,
\underline{L}
and angular derivatives.

The extra terms present when
N>0
either result in a a good sign or can be straightforwardly estimated via Young’s inequality or integration by parts in the
T
or
\mathbb{S}^{2}
directions together via an application of the induction assumption.
∎

In the corollary below, we combine the
(r-M)^{-1}
-weighted estimates from Proposition
8.4
with the integrated estimates from Theorem
7.2
.

Corollary 8.5
.

For all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\delta,\epsilon>0
, there exists
r_{H}
suitably small and a constant
C=C(r_{H},\mathbbm{h},\epsilon,\delta,N,l)>0
, such that for
Z\in\{K,\Phi,(r-M)Y\}
:

\begin{split}\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}\phi]\\
+&\>(r-M)^{-1+\epsilon}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}K\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+&\>C\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-M)^{\epsilon}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{l}Z^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&\>+C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

(8.8)

Proof.

Consider first the case
l=0
. Then the desired estimate follows by using (
7.6
) together with (
8.4
). The case
l>0
can be estimated by replacing
\psi
with
(-1)^{l}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi
and integrating an additional
l
times by parts on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. We omit the details of this additional step and refer to
[
AAG23
, Lemma 2.5]
for inequalities on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
required to obtain the necessary control of the angular derivatives.
∎

8.2.
Estimates near
\mathcal{I}^{+}

In this section, we state the
r
-weighted estimates in regions
r>r_{I}
, with
r_{I}\gg M
required for energy decay estimates. These do not depend on special geometric properties of extremal Kerr spacetimes, but are valid much more generally on asymptotically flat spacetimes. They involve “standard”
r
-weighted estimates, introduced in
[
DR10
]
as well as
r
-weighted estimates with an additional commutation
r^{2}L
, introduced in
[
AAG18c
]
, which do not hold for general
\phi
, but they do hold for
\phi
supported on azimuthal modes with
|m|\geq 1
or
|m|\geq 2
.

In the context of rotating Kerr, with
G\equiv 0
,the estimates in the proposition below fall under the special case
\ell=0
and
n\leq 1
in
[
AAG23
, Proposition 5.7]
. The generalization to
G\not\equiv 0
is straightforward.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
0<p<2
and
\epsilon>0
suitably small. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p}|L\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}pr^{p-1}|L\psi|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2},d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p}|L\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\Phi\phi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p+1}|rG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|\Phi G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau

(8.9)

and

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p}|L(r^{2}L)\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p-1}|L(r^{2}L)\psi|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r^{2}L)\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p}|L(r^{2}L)\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p+1}|L(r^{3}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

(8.10)

When we restrict to
\phi_{|m|\geq 2}
, we can obtain additional, higher-order estimates involving
(r^{2}L)^{2}\phi_{|m|\geq 2}
, which were also introduced in
[
AAG18c
]
; see the
\ell=0
and
n\leq 2
case in
[
AAG23
, Proposition 5.7]
.

Proposition 8.7
.

Let
0<p<2
and
\epsilon>0
suitably small. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p}|L(r^{2}L)^{2}\psi_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p-1}|L(r^{2}L)^{2}\psi_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r^{2}L)^{2}\psi_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p}|L(r^{2}L)^{2}\psi_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\phi_{|m|\geq 2}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{p+1}|(L(r^{2}L(r^{3}G_{|m|\geq 2}))|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-Mr^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}G_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

(8.11)

9.
Energy decay for
K
-derivatives

In this section, we consider hierarchies of
(r-M)^{-1}
- and
r
-weighted energy estimates, as derived in §
8.1
and §
8.2
and use them to derive decay-in-time estimates for (weighted) energies. Rather than considering
\phi
, we derive decay for
K\phi
and
K^{2}\phi
. In §
11
, we will use these decay estimates in combination with the
K
-inversion theory from §
10
to infer energy decay estimates at the level of
\phi
, without additional
K
-derivatives.

The following lemma illustrates that
K
derivatives can be “exchanged” for factors
(r-1)^{2}
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
M\leq r\leq r_{H}
. Then there exists a
C=C(r_{H})>0
, such that

(r-M)^{-2}|\underline{L}K\psi|^{2}\leq C\sum_{0\leq k\leq 1}|\underline{L}Z^{k}\psi|^{2}\\
+C(r-M)^{2}\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 1}\left[|\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+|K^{k_{1}}\Phi^{1+k_{2}}\psi|^{2}+\sin^{4}\theta|K^{2}\psi|^{2}+|G|^{2}\right]+(r-M)^{3}|\psi|^{2}.

(9.1)

We will employ the notation
\widetilde{Z}
for differential operators in
\{K,\Phi,(1-Mr^{-1})\underline{L},\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\}
and we set
M\equiv 1
in the remainder of this section.

We will first make the following
additional assumptions
on the inhomogeneity
G
: for all suitably small
\epsilon>0
and
k,l\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq 2\}}[(1+\tau^{\prime})^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+1

(9.2)

\displaystyle+(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}(1+\tau^{\prime})^{-1}](1+\tau^{\prime})^{2k}|K^{k}\widetilde{Z}^{l}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau<\infty,

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq 2\}}r^{2-\epsilon}(\tau^{\prime}+r)(1+\tau^{\prime})^{2k}|K^{k+1}(Xr^{2})^{l}rG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau

(9.3)

\displaystyle+\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq 2\}}|r^{2}G|^{2}\,d\sigma dr<\infty.

(9.4)

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small and assume (
9.2
) and (
9.2
). Then there exist
\epsilon>0
suitably small, a
r_{I}>1
suitably small and a constant
C>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}D_{\rm inhom}[\phi](0),

(9.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4+\eta}D_{{\rm inhom},1}[\phi](0),

(9.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}D_{\rm inhom,2}[\phi](0).

(9.7)

with

\displaystyle D_{\rm hom}[\phi](\tau)=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k+l\leq 2,l\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+l+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{l}K^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr

(9.8)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\epsilon}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

\displaystyle D_{\rm inhom}[\phi](\tau)=

\displaystyle\>D_{\rm hom}[\phi](\tau)

(9.9)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k\leq 1}\sum_{l\leq 1}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}[(1+\tau^{\prime})^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]r^{2}(1+\tau^{\prime})^{2k}|K^{k+l}G|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>r^{2-\epsilon}(\tau^{\prime}r^{\epsilon}+r)|rKG|^{2}+(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}|\widetilde{Z}^{l}K^{k}G|^{2}d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle D_{{\rm hom},1}[\phi](\tau)=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k+l\leq 4,l\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+l+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{l}K^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr

(9.10)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}|LK^{k+1}\psi|^{2}+r^{2-\delta}|L((r^{2}L)K^{2}\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

\displaystyle D_{{\rm inhom},1}[\phi](\tau)=

\displaystyle\>D_{{\rm hom},1}[\phi](\tau)

(9.11)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k\leq 2}\sum_{l\leq 2}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}[(1+\tau^{\prime})^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau\leq\tau\leq\tau+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]r^{2}(1+\tau^{\prime})^{2k}|K^{k+l}G|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>r^{2-\delta}(\tau^{\prime}+r)(1+\tau)^{2k}|rK^{k}G|^{2}+r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau^{\prime}+r)|L(r^{3}K^{2}G)|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}|\widetilde{Z}^{l}K^{k}G|^{2}+(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}(1+\tau^{\prime})|\widetilde{Z}^{l}KG|^{2}d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle D_{{\rm hom},2}[\phi](\tau)=

\displaystyle\>D_{\rm hom}[\phi](\tau)+D_{\rm hom}[K\phi](\tau)+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

(9.12)

\displaystyle D_{{\rm inhom},2}[\phi](\tau)=

\displaystyle\>D_{\rm inhom}[\phi]+D_{\rm inhom}[K\phi]+\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{2-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr

(9.13)

\displaystyle\>+\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{3-\eta}(1+\tau)^{2}|rKG|^{2}+r^{2-\eta}(r^{\eta}\tau+r)|L(r^{3}KG)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}.

Proof.

We will start by deriving decay estimates for the spacetime integral of
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]
. The proof proceeds in
four steps
and revolves around the repeated application of an interpolation inequality (Step 0).

Step 0
: an interpolation inequality

By using (
7.6
) directly, we can only obtain a (boundedness) estimate for the energy corresponding to the energy density
\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K\phi]
. We will use that the presence of a
K
-derivative allows us to gain factors of
r-1
via (
9.1
). We apply the following interpolation inequality:
let
0\leq\tau_{A}<\tau_{B}
and
\gamma\geq 0
, then:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr=\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma}\leq r-1\leq r_{I}-1\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r-1\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r-1\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{-3-\epsilon}|\underline{L}K\phi|^{2}+|K^{2}\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|LK\phi|^{2}+|K^{2}\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim\underbrace{(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{I}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr}_{=:J_{1}}\\
+\underbrace{(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-4+\epsilon}|\underline{L}K\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr}_{=:J_{2,a}}\\
+\underbrace{(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}(|K^{2}\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}K\phi|^{2})\,d\sigma dr}_{=:J_{2,b}}\\
+\underbrace{\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|LK\phi|^{2}+|K^{2}\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau}_{=:J_{3}},

where we used that
r-1\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma}
implies that
r<r_{H}
, for
r_{H}
suitably small and
\gamma\geq 0
.

We start by estimating
J_{2,a}
. By applying (
9.1
) and (
8.8
), we can estimate

J_{2,a}\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}\Bigg\{\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{\epsilon}|\widetilde{Z}^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg\}\\
\leq C(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}D_{2}[\phi](\tau_{A}),

(9.14)

with

D_{2}[\phi](\tau_{A}):=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{\epsilon}|\widetilde{Z}^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.

We estimate
J_{2,b}
directly by applying (
8.8
):

\begin{split}J_{2,b}\leq&\>C(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}D_{2}[\phi](\tau_{A}).\end{split}

We can estimate
J_{1}
, by directly applying (
7.6
) and (
7.7
):

\begin{split}J_{1}\lesssim&\>(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
&\>+(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|KG|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}\Bigg[\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+CD_{1}[\phi](\tau_{A})\Bigg],\end{split}

with

D_{1}[\phi](\tau_{A}):=\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|KG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Finally, we estimate
J_{3}
. By (
8.9
) with
p=1
, we obtain:

J_{3}\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K\phi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|KG|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\\
=:C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K\phi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CD_{1}[\phi](\tau_{A})+CD_{3}[\phi](\tau_{A}),

with

D_{3}[\phi](\tau_{A}):=C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Putting together the estimates for
J_{1}
,
J_{2,a}
and
J_{2,b}
, we are left with:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C(1+\tau_{A})^{\gamma(1+2\epsilon)}D_{1}[\phi](\tau_{A})+C(1+\tau_{A})^{-\gamma(1-2\epsilon)}D_{2}[\phi](\tau_{A})+CD_{3}[\phi](\tau_{A}).

(9.15)

The next two steps of the proof consist of starting with
\gamma=0
and then taking
\gamma
larger and larger at each step.

Step 1
:
\gamma=0

Let
\{\tau_{i}^{(0)}\}
be a dyadic sequence. We first apply (
9.15
) with
\gamma=0
and
\tau_{A}=\tau_{i}^{(0)}
,
\tau_{B}=\tau_{i+1}^{(0)}
, together with the mean value theorem to obtain another dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{i}^{(1)}\}
with
\tau^{(0)}_{2i}\leq\tau^{(1)}_{i}\leq\tau^{(0)}_{2i+1}

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(1)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C\sum_{k\leq 1}(\tau_{i}^{(1)})^{-1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2i}^{(0)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2i}^{(0)}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+D_{1}[\phi](\tau_{2i}^{(0)})+D_{2}[\phi](\tau_{2i}^{(0)})+D_{3}[\phi](\tau_{2i}^{(0)})\Bigg].

(9.16)

To conclude that the integral of
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]
decay along a dyadic sequence, we need to estimate the RHS by an an analogous expression evaluated at
\tau=0
.

Note that for all
\tau_{A}\geq 0

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq\int_{0}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr,

so we can alternatively replace
\tau=\tau_{2i}^{(0)}
on the RHS of (
9.16
) with
\tau=0
to obtain

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(1)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(\tau_{i}^{(1)})^{-1}\sum_{k\leq 1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+D_{1}[\phi](0)+D_{2}[\phi](0)+D_{3}[\phi](0)\Bigg].

(9.17)

Step 2
:
\gamma=1-\frac{1}{2^{l}}

By (
8.9
) with
p=2-\delta
together with the mean-value theorem and a standard interpolation argument (to interchange powers of
\tau
and
r
), we can ensure that along the dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{i}^{(1)}\}
from Step 1:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(1)}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(\tau_{i}^{(1)})^{-1+\delta}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{3-\delta}|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg].

(9.18)

To extend the above estimates for all times
\tau
, we consider
\tau_{i}^{(1)}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{i+1}^{(1)}
and apply (
8.9
) with
p=1
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(1)}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(1)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
&\>+C\int_{\tau_{i}^{(1)}}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{eq:rweightestinfKpsi}\&\eqref{eq:step1edecay}\\
\leq\end{subarray}&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1+\delta}\sum_{k\leq 1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&\>+D_{1}[\phi](0)+D_{2}[\phi](0)+D_{3}[\phi](0)\\
&\>+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau+r)|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg].\end{split}

(9.19)

Consider the dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{i}^{(1)}\}
from Step 1. We apply (
9.19
) in combination with (
9.15
) with
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
and
\tau_{A}=\tau_{i}^{(1)}
,
\tau_{B}=\tau_{i+1}^{(1)}
, together with the mean value theorem to obtain another dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{i}^{(2)}\}
with
\tau^{(1)}_{2i}\leq\tau^{(2)}_{i}\leq\tau^{(2)}_{2i+1}
, such that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(2)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C\sum_{k\leq 1}(\tau_{i}^{(2)})^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+2\epsilon)}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2i}^{(1)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+D_{1}[\phi](\tau_{2i}^{(1)})\\
+C(\tau_{i}^{(2)})^{-2+\frac{1}{2}(1+2\epsilon)}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C(\tau_{i}^{(2)})^{-2+\frac{1}{2}(1+2\epsilon)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau+r)|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(D_{2}[\phi](\tau_{2i}^{(1)})+D_{3}[\phi](\tau_{2i}^{(1)})).

Proceeding as in Step 1, but appealing additionally to (
9.17
) and using that

D_{1}[\phi](\tau_{A})\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{-2}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{2}|KG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau

we conclude that:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(2)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(\tau_{i}^{(2)})^{-\frac{3+2\epsilon}{2}}\sum_{k\leq 1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}(\tau+r)|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{2}|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+D_{2}[\phi](0)+D_{3}[\phi](0)\Bigg].

(9.20)

We can repeat this procedure by taking
\gamma=1-2^{-l}
with
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
l\geq 2
. Given
\eta>0
arbitrarily small, we can find a
L
sufficiently large such that repeating the procedure until
l=L
gives:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{(L+1)}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(\tau_{i}^{(L+1)})^{-2+\eta+\epsilon}\sum_{k\leq 1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau+r)|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{2k}|K^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+D_{2}[\phi](0)+D_{3}[\phi](0)\Bigg].

(9.21)

By applying (
7.6
), we can extend the estimate (
9.20
) to all
\tau
at the expense of additional degenerate factors. We obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta+\epsilon}\sum_{k\leq 1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau+r)|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{2}|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+D_{2}[\phi](0)+D_{3}[\phi](0)\Bigg].

(9.22)

To obtain decay for the integral of
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]
at all times
\tau
, we consider
\tau_{i}^{(L+1)}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{i+1}^{(L+1)}
.

We interpolate again to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr=&\>\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r-1\geq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r-1\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>(1+\tau)^{2\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
&\>+(1+\tau)^{-2+\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r-1\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}(r-1)^{-5+\epsilon}|\underline{L}K\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\end{split}

The first term on the very RHS we can estimate using (
9.22
). The second term on the very RHS we can estimate analogously to
J_{2,a}
(which is a spacetime integral). We apply (
9.1
) and (
8.8
) to obtain:

(1+\tau)^{-2+\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r-1\geq\tau\}}(r-1)^{-5+\epsilon}|\underline{L}K\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2+\epsilon}D_{2}[\phi](0).

We conclude (
9.5
).

In the next step, we will show that we can obtain faster decay by considering
K^{2}\phi
instead of
K\phi
.

Step 3
: Stronger decay for
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi]
.

To obtain stronger decay for
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi]
, we need to establish appropriate decay of the terms
D_{1}[K\phi]
,
D_{2}[K\phi]
and
D_{3}[K\phi]
on the right-hand side of (
9.16
) and improve the decay when
\phi
is replaced by
K\phi
. We also need to improve the decay rate of
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2i}^{(0)}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr
by two powers.

Note first of all that

D_{1}[K\phi](\tau_{A})\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{-4}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{4}|K^{2}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Furthermore,

D_{3}[\phi](\tau_{A})\leq C(1+\tau_{A})^{-2}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}(1+\tau)^{2}|rK^{2}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

To obtain sufficient decay for
D_{2}[K\phi](\tau_{A})
and the integral of
r|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}
, we will establish in particular decay for

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr.

We apply (
8.5
) with
N=1
and
p=2-\epsilon
, together with (
8.10
) with
p=1
and (
7.8
) in order to obtain:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr\,d\tau\leq C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}|\widetilde{Z}^{k}KG|^{2}+r^{2}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+1]|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K^{1+k}\phi]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(9.23)

We now apply the mean-value theorem along a dyadic sequence, together with (
8.5
) with
p=1-\epsilon
and (
7.8
), to conclude that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1}\sum_{k\leq 1}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr+(1+\tau)^{-1}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau^{\prime})\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}(1+\tau)|\widetilde{Z}KG|^{2}+r^{2}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}\\
+r^{2}\chi_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C(1+\tau)^{-\delta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau}(1+\tau^{\prime})^{\delta+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K^{1+k}\phi]\,d\sigma drd\tau.\Bigg].

(9.24)

In order to improve the decay above from
(1+\tau)^{-1}
to
(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}
, we estimate further the spacetime integral of
\mathcal{E}_{2-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}K\phi]
. We first use (
9.1
) to estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr=&\>\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}K\phi]+(r-1)^{-4+\epsilon}|\underline{L}\widetilde{Z}^{k}K\phi|\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
&\>+C\sum_{k\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}\phi]\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

Note that we have already established
\tau^{-1}
decay for the first term on the very right-hand side and the second term can also be shown to decay like
\tau^{-1}
by applying (
8.8
) with
N=2
and
p=1-\epsilon
. We then conclude that:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}K\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1}\sum_{k,l,m\leq 1}\Bigg[(1+\tau)^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k+l}\phi]\,d\sigma dr+(1+\tau)^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+l}\phi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{-1}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau^{\prime})\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{\epsilon}(1+\tau)^{k}|\widetilde{Z}^{l+m}K^{k}G|^{2}+(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}(1+\tau)|\widetilde{Z}KG|^{2}\\
+r^{2}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{k}|K^{k+l}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C(1+\tau)^{-\delta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau}(1+\tau^{\prime})^{\delta+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[K^{1+k}\phi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg].

(9.25)

From the above estimate, we obtain:

D_{2}[K\phi](\tau)\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\sum_{k\leq 1}D_{\rm hom}[K^{k+1}\phi]+\sum_{k+l\leq 4,l\leq 2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}(1+\tau)^{2k}|\widetilde{Z}^{l}K^{k}G|^{2}\\
+(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\epsilon}(1+\tau)|\widetilde{Z}KG|^{2}+r^{2}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}+r^{1+\epsilon}](1+\tau)^{2k}|K^{k}G|^{2}\\
+r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau+r)(1+\tau)^{2l}|rK^{k}G|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(9.26)

We are left with improving the decay of the integral of
r|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}
. To improve the decay rate in (
9.19
), we need to establish decay of the integral of
r^{2}|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}
.

Note first of all that by an application of a Hardy inequality and an averaging of the boundary terms, we obtain

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{I}-M\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|L(r^{2}LK^{2}\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

Now we apply (
8.10
) with
p=1
and then
p=2-\eta
to
K^{2}\phi
, together with a standard interpolation argument. We obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-3+\eta}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}|L(r^{2}LK^{2}\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}(r^{\delta}\tau+r)|r^{2}L(rK^{2}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\ldots\Bigg],

with
\ldots
denoting the terms that are already contained on the right-hand side of (
9.26
).

To improve the decay rate for
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi]
, it is also necessary to obtain better decay for the term
J_{3}
when
K\phi
is replaced by
K^{2}\phi
.

We can improve the decay rate for
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi]
by repeating the estimates in Step 1 and Step 3, taking
\gamma=1-2^{-l}
and starting from the already established
\tau^{-2+\eta}
decay for
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi]
and the decay estimates for the remaining terms on the RHS of (
9.16
) that follow from Step 3.

Via a similar argument to the one above for improving decay for
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr
, we can also obtain decay with a rate
(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}
for
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK^{2}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr
. We first observe, as above, that

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{I}-M\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&\>+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{eq:mornearho}\&\eqref{eq:rpinfcomm}\\
\leq\end{subarray}&\>C(\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi](\tau_{A})+\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi](\tau_{A}))+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&\>+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|L(r^{3}KG)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&\>+C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\end{split}

The only term for which we cannot immediately conclude
(1+\tau)^{-1+\eta}
decay from the above estimates is
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr
. To estimate this term, we apply (
8.10
) with
N=1
and
p=2-\eta
. We obtain

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{B}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}}&r^{2-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\begin{subarray}{c}\leq\end{subarray}&\>C(\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi](\tau_{A})+\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi](\tau_{A}))+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+&\>C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{3-\eta}|L(r^{3}KG)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{\tau_{A}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{A}+1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

We apply the mean-value theorem together with an interpolation argument to obtain
(1+\tau)^{-1+\eta}
decay for
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr
. We then conclude that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}&r^{2}|LK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}}\left[(1+\tau^{\prime})^{2-\eta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\phi](\tau^{\prime})+(1+\tau^{\prime})^{1-\eta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{2}\phi](\tau^{\prime})\right]\\
+&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{2-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{3-\eta}(1+\tau)^{2}|rKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
+&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\eta}(r^{\eta}\tau+r)|L(r^{3}KG)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[(1+\tau^{\prime})^{1+\delta}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}+r^{2}\chi_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}+r^{1+\epsilon}]\\
\times&\>(1+\tau^{\prime})^{2-\eta}|K^{k+1}G|^{2}\,r^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\eta}\Big[D_{\rm inhom}[\phi]+D_{\rm inhom}[K\phi]+\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{2-\eta}|L(r^{2}LK\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&\>+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{3-\eta}(1+\tau)^{2}|rKG|^{2}+r^{2-\eta}(r^{\eta}\tau+r)|L(r^{3}KG)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\Big].\qed\end{split}

10.
Elliptic estimates and
K
-inversion theory

In this section, we will set
M\equiv 1
for convenience. Let
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
and consider the differential operator

\mathcal{L}u:=\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}u)-(r^{2}-1)\widetilde{X}\Phi u-r\Phi u+(\not{\mathcal{D}}-\Phi^{2})u.

(10.1)

We will assume that
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
in this section. The operator
\mathcal{L}
can be obtained by considering all the terms in the wave operator
\square_{g}
without
K
-derivatives; see (
3.5
).

We will construct the corresponding inverse operator
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
and use it to construct the initial data for (
3.1
) corresponding to the
K
-integral
of solutions
\phi
to (
3.1
), with an appropriate choices of inhomogeneity
G
. For this construction, we will need to derive appropriate, weighted elliptic estimates.
7
7

7

In contrast with the standard elliptic estimates, the estimates in this section will only control (weighted)
L^{2}
norms of derivatives up to order 1 in terms of a (weighted)
L^{2}
norm of
\mathcal{L}u
, rather than derivatives up to order 2. This is related to the fact that
\mathcal{L}
is not really an elliptic operator, since
K
is not a timelike vector field. Nevertheless, we will refer to these estimates as elliptic estimates.

10.1.
Elliptic estimates

We first derive a preliminary,
global
estimate for
u
. For this, we introduce the rescaled variable:

\widetilde{u}=\sqrt{r^{2}-1}u.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and
0<p\leq 2
. Consider the rescaled variable
\widetilde{u}=\sqrt{r^{2}-1}u
. Then for
r_{H}>1
arbitrarily small and
\delta\geq 0
,
\epsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(r_{H})>1
such that

\int_{\Sigma}pr^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{2}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq\epsilon\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\delta}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\Sigma}r^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{-\delta}|\mathcal{L}u|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.2)

Proof.

We start by deriving an equation for
\widetilde{u}
in terms of
\mathcal{L}u
. We have that

\begin{split}(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}u)=&\>(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}((r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{u}))=(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}-1}(r^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}\right)\\
-&(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{X}(r\Delta(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\widetilde{u})\\
=&\>\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}-1}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}\right)-(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{dr}(r\Delta(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}})\widetilde{u}.\end{split}

We moreover obtain:

-(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-1)\widetilde{X}\Phi((r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{u})=-\widetilde{X}\Phi\widetilde{u}+(r^{2}-1)^{-1}r\Phi\widetilde{u}.

Note that
-(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}r\Phi u=-(r^{2}-1)^{-1}r\Phi\widetilde{u}
, which cancels with the last term on the RHS above.
Hence, we obtain

(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}u=\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}-1}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}\right)-\widetilde{X}\Phi\widetilde{u}+(r^{2}-1)^{-1}(\not{\mathcal{D}}-\Phi^{2})\widetilde{u}-(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{dr}(r\Delta(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}})\widetilde{u}.

We now consider the vector field multiplier
-(r+1)^{p}\frac{r-1}{r+1}\widetilde{X}\overline{\widetilde{u}}
. We obtain the identity:

\Re\left(-(r+1)^{p}\frac{r-1}{r+1}\widetilde{X}\overline{\widetilde{u}}(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}u\right)=\overbrace{-(r+1)^{p}\Re\left(\frac{r-1}{r+1}\widetilde{X}\overline{\widetilde{u}}\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}-1}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}\right)\right)}^{=:J_{1}}+\Phi(\ldots)\\
\overbrace{-(r+1)^{p-2}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{\widetilde{u}}(\not{\mathcal{D}}-\Phi^{2})\widetilde{u})}^{=:J_{2}}\\
+\overbrace{(r+1)^{p-\frac{3}{2}}(r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{dr}(r\Delta(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}})\widetilde{u}\widetilde{X}\overline{\widetilde{u}}}^{=:J_{3}}.

We first rewrite
J_{1}
. We have that

\begin{split}J_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p}\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{(r-1)^{2}}{(r+1)^{2}}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\right)=\widetilde{X}\left(-\frac{1}{2}(r-1)^{p-2}(r-1)^{2}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\right)+\frac{p}{2}(r+1)^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{2}|X\widetilde{u}|^{2}.\end{split}

We then rewrite
J_{2}
by applying the Leibniz rule on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and in the
\widetilde{X}
direction:

\begin{split}J_{2}=&\>\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p-2}\widetilde{X}(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{u}|^{2}-|\Phi\widetilde{u}|^{2})+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
=&\>\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p-2}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{u}|^{2}-|\Phi\widetilde{u}|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}(2-p)(r+1)^{p-3}(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{u}|^{2}-|\Phi\widetilde{u}|^{2}).\end{split}

Finally, we consider
J_{3}
. We use that

\frac{d}{dr}(r\Delta(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}})=\frac{2r-1}{(r+1)^{\frac{5}{2}}(r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}

to obtain

\begin{split}J_{3}=&\>\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p-\frac{3}{2}}(r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{dr}(r\Delta(r^{2}-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}})\widetilde{X}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})=\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p-4}(2r-1)\widetilde{X}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\\
=&\>\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{X}((r+1)^{p-4}(2r-1)|\widetilde{u}|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p-5}((6-2p)(r-1)-p)|\widetilde{u}|^{2}.\end{split}

Note that only term in
J_{1},J_{2},J_{3}
that is not manifestly non-negative definite is
\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{p-5}((6-2p)(r-1)-p)|\widetilde{u}|^{2}
for
r-1<\frac{p}{6-2p}
. However, since

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}(2-p)(r+1)^{p-3}(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{u}|^{2}-|\Phi\widetilde{u}|^{2})\geq\frac{1}{8}(2-p)(r+1)^{p-3}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\Phi\widetilde{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma,

we have that
J_{2}+J_{3}
is non-negative definite for
p
suitably small, after integrating over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

After integrating
J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}
and using that all boundary terms vanish (using the compactness of the support of
\widetilde{u}
), we therefore conclude that for
p>0
sufficiently small,

\int_{\Sigma}pr^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{2}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}+r^{p-3}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}(1-r^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{p-1}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}||\mathcal{L}u|\,d\sigma dr.

(10.3)

Then, equipped with (
10.3
), we can immediately extend (
10.3
) to
0<p\leq 2
, by estimating the non-negative definite term in
J_{3}
with (
10.3
) for
p
small.

By applying Young’s inequality, we can further estimate, for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small and
\delta\geq 0
,

(1-r^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{p-1}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}||\mathcal{L}u|\leq\epsilon r^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\delta}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\delta}r^{p-1}|\mathcal{L}u|^{2}.

Note that away from the horizon, we can absorb the
|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}
term above into the LHS of (
10.3
) and conclude (
10.2
).
∎

To improve (
10.2
) and absorb the term on the RHS with a factor
\epsilon
, we will first assume that
u
is supported on the
m
th azimuthal mode and decompose
u(r,\theta,\tilde{\varphi})=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{|m|}}u_{m\ell}(r)S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\tilde{\varphi}}
. Let

u^{\flat}:=\sum_{|m|\leq\ell\leq L_{m}}u_{m\ell}(r)S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\tilde{\varphi}},

where
L_{m}\geq|m|
will be chosen suitably large later on. Denote
u^{\sharp}=u-u^{\flat}
. Recall from §
2.4
that

\not{\mathcal{D}}(S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\tilde{\varphi}})=-\Lambda_{+,m\ell}S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\tilde{\varphi}}.

We first derive an analogue of (
10.2
) for
u^{\sharp}
with fewer degenerate factors at
r=1
on the LHS and expressed in terms of
u
.

Proposition 10.2
.

For
0\leq p\leq 2
and
L_{m}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\int_{\Sigma}pr^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{2}|\widetilde{X}(ru^{\sharp})|^{2}+r^{p-1}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{p-1}|(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.4)

Proof.

We restrict to
r\leq r_{H}
, where
r_{H}
will be chosen suitably small, by considering the vector field multiplier
-\chi(r-1)^{q}\overline{u}^{\sharp}
, with
\chi:[1,\infty]\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\leq r_{H}
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\geq r_{H}+1
.

Then

\begin{split}-\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\overline{u}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp})=&-\Re(\chi\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}))+(r^{2}-1)(r-1)^{q}\chi\Re(\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}\Phi u^{\sharp})\\
&\>+(r-1)^{q}(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u^{\sharp}|^{2}-|\Phi u^{\sharp}|^{2})+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

We further rewrite

-\Re(\chi(r-1)^{q}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}))=-\widetilde{X}(\chi(r-1)^{q}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\Delta\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp})+\chi(r-1)^{2+q}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\\
+\left(\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+2}+q\chi(r-1)^{q+1}\right)\Re(\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}).

We estimate
(r^{2}-1)(r-1)^{q}\chi\Re(\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}\Phi u^{\sharp})
via Young’s inequality:

(r^{2}-1)(r-1)^{q}\chi|\Re(\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}\Phi u^{\sharp})|\leq\frac{1}{2}\chi(r-1)^{2+q}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(r+1)^{2}(r-1)^{q}|\Phi u^{\sharp}|^{2}+\Phi(\ldots).

The first term on the RHS can be absorbed into
\chi(r-1)^{q}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}
. To absorb the second term into
(r-1)^{q}(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u^{\sharp}|^{2}-|\Phi u^{\sharp}|^{2})
, we need to take
L_{m}
appropriately large, so that
\Lambda_{+,m\ell}
is suitably large for all
\ell>L_{m}
.

Note that we can estimate the term involving
\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}
further by applying the Leibniz rule:

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+2}+q\chi(r-1)^{q+1}\right)\Re(\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+2}+q\chi(r-1)^{q+1}\right)\widetilde{X}(|u^{\sharp}|^{2})\\
=&\>\widetilde{X}\left(\left(\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+2}+q\chi(r-1)^{q+1}\right)|u^{\sharp}|^{2}\right)\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{d^{2}\chi}{dr^{2}}(r-1)^{q+2}+(2q+2)\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+1}+\chi q(q+1)(r-1)^{q}\right]|u^{\sharp}|^{2}.\end{split}

We now fix
q=0
. Integrating over
\Sigma
and combining with (
10.2
) applied to
u^{\sharp}
with
\epsilon
suitably small. Here, we use that can absorb the term involving derivatives of
\chi
into the LHS of (
10.2
) and the term involving an
\epsilon
on the RHS of (
10.2
) can similarly be absorbed (for
\epsilon>0
suitably small). We conclude (
10.4
).
∎

To improve (
10.2
) for
u^{\flat}
, we introduce the rescaled variables
\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}=\frac{u_{m\ell}}{w_{\pm,m\ell}}
, with
w_{\pm,m\ell}
defined in §
3.2
. Then
w_{\pm,m\ell}
are solutions to:

\mathcal{L}(w_{\pm,m\ell}S_{m\ell}e^{im\tilde{\varphi}})=0,

(10.5)

with boundary conditions imposed at
r=1
.

By using that
\mathcal{L}(w_{\pm,m\ell}S_{m\ell}e^{im\tilde{\varphi}})=0
, it follows easily that
\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}
must satisfy:

w_{\pm,m\ell}^{-1}(\mathcal{L}u)_{m\ell}=\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell})+2\Delta w_{\pm,m\ell}^{-1}\frac{dw_{\pm,m\ell}}{dr}\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}-im(r^{2}-1)\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}.

(10.6)

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
\delta>0
, then there exists a constant
C=C(\delta)>0
, such that

\int_{\Sigma}pr^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{2+\delta}|\widetilde{X}(ru^{\flat})|^{2}+r^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u^{\flat}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{p-1}(1-r^{-1})^{-\delta}|(\Phi\mathcal{L}u)^{\flat}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.7)

Proof.

We restrict to
r\leq r_{H}
, where
r_{H}
will be chosen suitably small, by considering
\chi:[1,\infty]\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\leq r_{H}
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\geq r_{H}+\eta
, with
\eta>0
suitably small.

We will suppress the subscripts in the notation by writing
\check{u}=\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}
and
w=w_{\pm,m\ell}
. Consider the vector field multiplier
-\chi(r-1)^{q}\widetilde{X}\overline{\check{u}}
acting on both sides of (
10.6
):

\begin{split}-\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{\check{u}}w^{-1}(\mathcal{L}u)_{m\ell})=&\>-\chi(r-1)^{2+q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{\check{u}}\widetilde{X}^{2}\check{u})-2(r-1)^{q+1}\left[1+\Re\left(w^{-1}\frac{dw}{dr}\right)\right]|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\\
=&\>-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{X}\left(\chi(r-1)^{2+q}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\right)\\
&\>+(r-1)^{q+1}\left[\frac{q}{2}-1-2\Re\left(w^{-1}\frac{dw}{dr}\right)\right]|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}+\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{2+q}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}.\end{split}

By (
3.17
), it follows that

\frac{q}{2}-1-2\Re\left(w^{-1}\frac{dw}{dr}\right)=\frac{q}{2}\pm 2\beta+O(r-1),

with
\beta=\Re(\sqrt{\Lambda_{m\ell}-2m^{2}+\frac{1}{4}})
. If
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, we will only consider
w_{+,m\ell}
and not
w_{-,m\ell}
.

Hence, for
q=2\beta+\eta
, we can integrate along
\Sigma
to obtain

\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-2\beta+\eta+1}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+1\}}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\\
+C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}+1\}}(r-1)^{-2\beta+\eta-1}|w|^{-2}|(\mathcal{L}u)_{m\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.8)

We have that in
r\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}\check{u}=

\displaystyle\>\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{(r^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{w}\widetilde{u}\right),

\displaystyle(r-1)^{-2\beta}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}+C\left|\Lambda_{m\ell}-m^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right|(r-1)^{-2+2\beta}|\widetilde{u}|^{2},

\displaystyle|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r-1)^{-2\beta}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}+C\left|\Lambda_{m\ell}-m^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right|(r-1)^{-2-2\beta}|\check{u}|^{2}.

The restriction
\ell\leq L_{m}
ensures moreover that
|\Lambda_{m\ell}-m^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\leq Bm^{2}
for some
B>0
.

Hence, we can combine (
10.8
) with (
10.2
) to obtain:

\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-2\beta+1+\eta}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}+(r-1)^{1+\eta}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\epsilon m^{2}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1-2\beta+\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}\\
+Cm^{2}\int_{\Sigma}(r-1)^{-\eta}|(\mathcal{L}u)_{m\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.9)

To absorb the first term on the RHS of (
10.9
) into the LHS, we apply the following Hardy inequality: for
\beta=0

\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq\int_{\Sigma\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}(r-1)^{-\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma+C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{1+\eta}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr

and for
\beta>0
and
\eta<2\beta
:

\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1+\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r=r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma+C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{1+\eta}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

The boundary term at
\mathcal{H}^{+}
vanishes and the boundary term at
r=r_{H}
can be estimated via (
10.2
). Hence, we conclude that

\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-2\beta+1+\eta}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}|^{2}+(r-1)^{1+\eta}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{u}|^{2}+(r-1)^{-1-2\beta+\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\epsilon m^{2}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r-1)^{-1-2\beta+\eta}|\check{u}|^{2}+Cm^{2}\int_{\Sigma}(r-1)^{-\eta}|(\mathcal{L}u)_{m\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.10)

To absorb, we therefore need
\epsilon\leq C^{-1}m^{-2}
and then (
10.7
) follows after combining (
10.10
) with (
10.2
).
∎

10.2.
Additional estimates for
u^{\sharp}

In this section, we derive additional higher-order estimates for
u^{\sharp}
by commuting
\mathcal{L}
with
\widetilde{X}
.

Lemma 10.4
.

The following commutation properties holds:

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L},\widetilde{X}]u=

\displaystyle-2(r-1)\widetilde{X}^{2}u-2\widetilde{X}u+2r\Phi\widetilde{X}u+\Phi u

(10.11)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L},\widetilde{X}^{2}]u=

\displaystyle-4(r-1)\widetilde{X}^{3}u-6\widetilde{X}^{2}u+4r\Phi\widetilde{X}^{2}u+4\Phi\widetilde{X}u.

(10.12)

Proof.

We have that

\widetilde{X}\mathcal{L}u=\widetilde{X}^{2}(\Delta\widetilde{X}u)-(r^{2}-1)\widetilde{X}^{2}\Phi u-2r\widetilde{X}\Phi u-r\widetilde{X}\Phi u-\Phi u+(\not{\mathcal{D}}-\Phi^{2})\widetilde{X}u

We can further rewrite:

\widetilde{X}^{2}(\Delta\widetilde{X}u)=\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}^{2}u)+\widetilde{X}(2(r-1)\widetilde{X}u)=\widetilde{X}(\Delta\widetilde{X}^{2}u)+2(r-1)\widetilde{X}^{2}u+2\widetilde{X}u.

Combining the above gives (
10.11
).

To obtain (
10.12
), we use that

[\mathcal{L},\widetilde{X}^{2}]u=[\mathcal{L},\widetilde{X}](\widetilde{X}u)+\widetilde{X}([\mathcal{L},\widetilde{X}]u)

and we apply (
10.11
).
∎

Proposition 10.5
.

Let
1\leq n\leq 2
. Then for sufficiently large
L_{m}
depending on
n
,

\sum_{k\leq n}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{I}\}}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq n-1}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{I}+1\}}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma}|(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.13)

Proof.

We restrict to
r\leq r_{I}
, where
r_{I}
will be chosen suitably small, by considering the vector field multiplier
-\chi(r-1)^{q}\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}
, with
\chi:[1,\infty]\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\leq r_{I}
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\geq r_{I}+1
.

By repeating the proof of Proposition
10.2
, but with
u^{\sharp}
replaced by
\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}
, we obtain the identity:

\begin{split}-\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{L}\widetilde{X}u)^{\sharp})=&\widetilde{X}\left[\left(\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+2}+q\chi(r-1)^{q+1}\right)|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}-\chi(r-1)^{q}\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\Delta\widetilde{X}^{2}u^{\sharp}\right]\\
&\>+\Phi(\ldots)+\not{\textnormal{div}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)+\chi(r-1)^{2+q}|\widetilde{X}^{2}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+(r-1)^{q}(|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}-|\Phi\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2})\\
&\>+(r^{2}-1)(r-1)^{q}\chi\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}^{2}\Phi u^{\sharp})-\frac{1}{2}\chi q(q+1)(r-1)^{q}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{d^{2}\chi}{dr^{2}}(r-1)^{q+2}+(2q+2)\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+1}\right)|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}.\end{split}

By (
10.11
), we also have that

\begin{split}\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{L}\widetilde{X}u)^{\sharp})=&\>\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}(\widetilde{X}\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp})-2\chi(r-1)^{q+1}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\widetilde{X}^{2}u^{\sharp})-2\chi(r-1)^{q}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\\
&\>+\Phi(\ldots)+\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\Phi u^{\sharp})\\
=&\>(q-1)\chi(r-1)^{q}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}(\widetilde{X}\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp})+\chi(r-1)^{q}\Re(\widetilde{X}\overline{u}^{\sharp}\Phi u^{\sharp})\\
&-\widetilde{X}\left[(r-1)^{q+1}\chi|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\right]+\frac{d\chi}{dr}(r-1)^{q+1}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}.\end{split}

Hence after integrating over
\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{I}+1\}
and taking
L_{m}
sufficiently large to absorb the
|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}
terms with a bad sign, we obtain

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{I}\}}&(1-r^{-1})^{2+q}|\widetilde{X}^{2}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+(1-r^{-1})^{q}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{I}+1\}}(1-r^{-1})^{q}|\Phi u^{\sharp}|^{2}+(1-r^{-1})^{q}|(\widetilde{X}\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r_{I}\leq r\leq r_{I}+1\}}|\widetilde{X}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

Hence, taking
q=1-\epsilon
, we obtain (
10.13
) for
n=1
by combining the above with (
10.4
) . The
n=2
case follows similarly by considering an extra
\widetilde{X}
above and applying the commutation formula (
10.12
).
∎

In order to obtain sufficiently strong weighted
L^{2}
estimates for
u^{\sharp}
and its derivatives away from the horizon in terms of the derivative
X
rather than
\widetilde{X}
, we need additional estimates, which require more decay of
(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}
as
r\to\infty
.

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
1\leq n\leq 2
. Then for sufficiently large
L_{m}
depending on
n
,

\sum_{k\leq n}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{4+4k}|\widetilde{X}^{k+1}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+r^{2+4k}|\widetilde{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq n}\int_{\Sigma}(r-1)^{2+4k}|\widetilde{X}^{k}(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma}|(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.14)

and

\sum_{k\leq n}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|X(r^{2}X)^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+|{\not{\nabla}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r^{2}X)^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq n}\int_{\Sigma}(r-1)^{2+4k}|\widetilde{X}^{k}(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma}|(\mathcal{L}u)^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(10.15)

Proof.

We repeat the estimates in the proof of Propositions
10.2
and
10.5
, but we consider the estimates globally on
\Sigma
by omitting
\chi
and taking
q=2+4k
when considering
(\mathcal{L}\widetilde{X}^{k}u)^{\sharp}
with
k=0,1,2
.
∎

10.3.
Construction of
\mathcal{L}^{-1}

The aim of this section is to construct the time integral

K^{-1}\phi(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})=-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\phi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}

corresponding to solutions
\phi
to (
3.1
) with suitable
G
. We will construct time integrals by solving (
3.1
) with
G
replaced by
G^{(1)}=-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}G(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}
and initial data
(K^{-1}\phi|_{\Sigma},\phi|_{\Sigma})
. We construct
K^{-1}\phi|_{\Sigma}
by inverting the operator
\mathcal{L}
, i.e. by solving
\mathcal{L}u=F
for appropriate choices of
F
, depending on the initial data for
\phi
and the inhomogeneity
G
.

We start by constructing the coefficients
u_{m\ell}
with
\ell\leq L_{m}
corresponding to solutions of

\mathcal{L}u=F

via integration in
r
.

Proposition 10.7
.

We define

u_{m\ell}(r):=-w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\left[\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx\right]\,dr^{\prime},

(10.16)

where we assume enough regularity and decay towards
r=\infty
for
F_{m\ell}
and for the RHS above to be well-defined and in the case
\Lambda_{+,m\ell}\leq 2m^{2}
, we assume additionally that

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}F_{m\ell}(r)\,dr=0.

(10.17)

Then
u_{m\ell}\in C^{2}((1,\infty))
and
\mathcal{L}u_{m\ell}=F_{m\ell}
.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
r\geq 2
and
q\geq 0
:

|ru_{m\ell}|(r)+|rX(ru_{m\ell})|(r)\leq r^{-q}\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}|r^{1+q}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|,

(10.18)

where
X=\widetilde{X}-im\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{2}
.

(i)

When
\Lambda_{+,m\ell}\geq 2m^{2}
, we have that for
r\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 2}|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|+1)\sum_{k\leq 2}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}F_{m\ell})(r^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 2}|X((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|+1)(r-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\leq 2\\
j\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}^{j}((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}F_{m\ell})(r^{\prime})|.

(ii)

When
\Lambda_{+,m\ell}<2m^{2}
, we have that for
r\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 2}|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{\pm,m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{k\leq 2}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}F_{m\ell})(r^{\prime})|,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{\pm,m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\leq 2\\
j\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}^{j}((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}F_{m\ell})(r^{\prime})|.

Proof.

We consider
(\mathcal{L}u_{m\ell})=F_{m\ell}
. Denote

\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}=\frac{u_{m\ell}}{w_{\infty,m\ell}}.

We then rewrite (
10.6
) with
\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}
replacing
\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}
and
w_{\infty,m\ell}
replacing
w_{\pm,m\ell}
as the following ODE:

\widetilde{X}(\Delta w_{\infty,m\ell}^{2}e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell})=e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}w_{\infty,m\ell}F_{m\ell}=:\check{F}_{\infty,m\ell},

(10.19)

with
r_{0}>1
arbitrary.

We assume that
\lim_{r\to\infty}\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)=0
and integrate from
r=\infty
to obtain

\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)=-(r-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r)e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{r}^{\infty}\check{F}_{\infty,m\ell}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}.

Integrating once more, starting from
r=1
and assuming that
\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(1)=0
, we obtain:

\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)=-\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}\check{F}_{\infty,m\ell}(x)\,dx\,dr^{\prime}.

Note that

|X(ru_{m\ell})|=|X(rw_{\infty}(r)\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell})|=|X(e^{imr_{*}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell})|\leq C|(X+im)\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}|+Cr^{-2}|\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}|.

Furthermore,

|(X+im)\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}|=\left|\left(\widetilde{X}+im\left(1-\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{2}\right)\right)\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}\right|=\left|\left(\widetilde{X}+imO(r^{-1})\right)\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}\right|.

Hence, when
r\geq 2
, we can estimate for
q\geq 0

\begin{split}|X(ru_{m\ell})|\leq&\>Cr^{-1-q}\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}|r^{1+q}\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r^{\prime})|+Cr^{-1-q}\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}|r^{q}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r^{\prime})|\\
\leq&\>Cr^{-1-q}\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}|r^{1+q}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|.\end{split}

Suppose
