\alpha_{m\ell}:=\Lambda_{+,m\ell}+\frac{1}{4}-2m^{2}\leq 0
. Then, in fact, in view of the asymptotics of
w_{\infty,m\ell}
near
r=1
, to conclude that the above integral is well-defined, it is necessary to impose

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}F_{m\ell}(r)\,dr=0,

(10.20)

so that we can express:

\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)=\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}\check{F}_{\infty,m\ell}(x)\,dx\,dr^{\prime},

(10.21)

and hence,

u_{m\ell}(r)=w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx\,dr^{\prime}.

Suppose
0<\alpha_{m\ell}<\frac{1}{4}
. Then we will still impose (
10.20
). We can immediately estimate in
r\leq 2
:

\displaystyle|\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|,

\displaystyle|\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|.

In order to obtain boundedness for
u_{m\ell}
and
\widetilde{X}u_{m\ell}
near
r=1
, we now consider
u_{\pm,m\ell}
. We have that in
r\leq 2

\begin{split}|\Delta w_{\pm}^{2}\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}(r)|=&\>\left|(r-1)^{2}w_{\pm}^{2}\widetilde{X}\left(\frac{w_{\infty}}{w_{\pm}}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}\right)(r)\right|\\
\leq&\>C|B_{\mp}||\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)|+C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)(r-1)^{1-\Re(\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}})\mp\Re(\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}})}|\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)|\\
\leq&\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)(r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}\pm\Re(\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}})}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|.\end{split}

Hence,
\Delta w_{\pm}^{2}\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}
vanishes at
r=1
for
\alpha_{m\ell}<\frac{1}{4}
and we can integrate (
10.19
) starting from
r=1
to obtain:

\widetilde{X}\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}(r)=(r-1)^{-2}w_{\pm,m\ell}^{-2}(r)e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{1}^{r}{F}_{\infty,m\ell}(r^{\prime})w_{\pm,m\ell}(r^{\prime})e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}\,dr^{\prime}.

(10.22)

Since

\begin{split}|\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}(r)|=\left|\frac{w_{\infty}}{w_{\pm}}\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\right|\leq&\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)(1+(r-1)^{\pm 2\sqrt{\alpha}})\left|\check{u}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\right|\\
\leq&\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)(r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}+\Re(\sqrt{\alpha})\pm 2\Re(\sqrt{\alpha})}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|,\end{split}

we have that
\check{u}_{\pm,m\ell}(1)=0
. And hence, we can integrate (
10.22
) to obtain for all
\alpha_{m\ell}<\frac{1}{4}
an alternative expression for
u_{m\ell}(r)
:

u_{m\ell}(r)=w_{\pm,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\pm,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}w_{\pm,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx\,dr^{\prime}.

The above expression of
u_{m\ell}(r)
will allow us to obtain more refined estimates for
u_{m\ell}(r)
and to obtain a bound on
|\widetilde{X}u_{m\ell}(r)|
.

We first expand:

F_{m\ell}(r)=F_{m\ell}(1)+\int_{1}^{r}\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})dr^{\prime}.

Hence, we can expand

w_{\pm,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx=e^{-im(r_{0}-1)}(r-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-3im}(1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\\
\times\left(F_{m\ell}(1)+\int_{1}^{x}\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(y)dy\right).

We integrate the the above expansion to obtain:

\begin{split}u_{m\ell}(r)=&\>e^{-im(r_{0}-1)}(r-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-im\mp\sqrt{-\alpha}}(1+O((r-1))\\
&\>\times\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-1\pm 2\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+4im}(1+O_{\infty}(r^{\prime}-1))\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha}-3im}(r^{\prime}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-3im}F_{m\ell}(1)\,dr^{\prime}\\
&\>+w_{\pm,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\pm,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}w_{\pm,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}\int_{1}^{x}\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(y)dy\,dx\,dr^{\prime}\\
=&\>\frac{\frac{1}{2}+im\pm\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}{\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-3im}e^{-im(r_{0}-1)}F_{m\ell}(1)(1+O_{\infty}(r-1))\\
&\>+w_{\pm,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\pm,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}w_{\pm,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}\int_{1}^{x}\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(y)dy\,dx\,dr^{\prime}.\end{split}

From this, we can estimate in
r\leq 2
:

|\widetilde{X}u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq C\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|.

It is straightforward to commute the above estimates with
(r-1)\widetilde{X}
and obtain for
r\leq 2
:

\sum_{k\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq C\sum_{k\leq 2}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}((r-1)^{k}F_{m\ell})(r^{\prime})|.

For
\alpha_{m\ell}\geq\frac{1}{4}
, we will not impose (
10.20
), as this would require
F_{m\ell}(1)=0
. Instead, we express

u_{m\ell}(r)=-w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx\,dr^{\prime}.

Note that for
\alpha_{m\ell}\geq\frac{1}{4}

w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)=B_{+}(r-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}+(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)O((r-1)^{\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}).

We first split:

\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx=\int_{r^{\prime}}^{2}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx\\
+\int_{2}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx

and use that

\left|\int_{2}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx\right|\leq C\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}|rF_{m\ell}|(r^{\prime}).

We then write
F_{m\ell}(x)=F_{m\ell}(1)+\int_{1}^{x}F_{m\ell}(y)\,dy
and use the asymptotic properties of
w_{\infty,m\ell}
near
r=1
to obtain for
r\leq 2
:

\left|\widetilde{X}^{k}\left(\int_{r^{\prime}}^{2}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}(x)\,dx-\frac{B_{+}}{-\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+2im}(r^{\prime}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-2im}F_{m\ell}(1)\right)\right|\\
\leq C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)\widetilde{X}^{k}(1+(r^{\prime}-1)^{\frac{3}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}})\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}(|F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|+|\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|),

with
k=0,1
.

Hence, by plugging the above expression into
u_{m\ell}
as above, we obtain:

\displaystyle|u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)\sup_{r^{\prime}}|r^{\prime}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|

\displaystyle|Xu_{m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)(r-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}(\sup_{r^{\prime}}|r^{\prime}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|+\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|).

It is straightforward to commute the above estimates with
(r-1)\widetilde{X}
and
((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{2}
in order to derive in the region
r\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 2}|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)\sum_{k\leq 2}\sup_{r^{\prime}}|r^{\prime}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 2}|X((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}u_{m\ell}(r)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|)(r-1)^{-\frac{3}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}(\sup_{r^{\prime}}|F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|+\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}|\widetilde{X}F_{m\ell}(r^{\prime})|).

∎

By combining the above estimates, we arrive at a weighted energy estimate for
u
along
\Sigma
, which will be important for establishing the finiteness of appropriate initial energy norms for
K^{-1}\phi
in terms of initial data for
\phi
.

Proposition 10.8
.

Let
u_{m\ell}
with
\ell\geq|m|
be the functions defined in Proposition
10.7
. Then

\displaystyle u=

\displaystyle\>u^{\flat}+u^{\sharp}\in L^{2}(\Sigma),\quad\textnormal{with}

\displaystyle u^{\flat}(r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{|m|\leq\ell\leq L_{m}}u_{m\ell}(r)S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\tilde{\varphi}},

\displaystyle u^{\sharp}(r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{L_{m}<\ell<\infty}u_{m\ell}(r)S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\tilde{\varphi}}

is well-defined. For
\delta>0
arbitrarily small,

\begin{split}\sum_{k\leq 2}&\int_{\Sigma\cap{\{r\leq 2\}}}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\delta}|\widetilde{X}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}u^{\flat}|^{2}+(1-r^{-1})^{-1+\delta}|((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}u^{\flat}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&\>+\int_{\Sigma\cap{\{r\geq 2\}}}|u^{\flat}|^{2}+r^{2}|Xu^{\flat}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(|B_{+}|+|B_{-}|+1)\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\leq 2\\
j\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r=r^{\prime}\}}|\widetilde{X}^{j}((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}F^{\flat})(r^{\prime})|^{2}\,d\sigma\\
+&\>\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r=r^{\prime}\}}|rF^{\flat}(r^{\prime})|^{2}\,d\sigma\Bigg].\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\sum_{k\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma\cap{\{r\leq 2\}}}&(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}u^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\int_{\Sigma\cap{\{r\geq 2\}}}|u^{\sharp}|^{2}+r^{2}|Xu^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq 2\}}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}((1-r^{-1})\widetilde{X})^{k}F^{\sharp}|^{2}+|F^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+&\>C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\geq 2\}}|F^{\sharp}|^{2}+r^{2}|\widetilde{X}F^{\sharp}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

Proof.

The estimate for
u^{\flat}
follows directly from the pointwise estimates in Proposition
10.7
. The estimates for
u^{\sharp}
are the result of combining Proposition
10.2
with
p=1
with Proposition
10.5
.
∎

10.4.
An inhomogeneous wave equation

In this section, we will apply the elliptic theory from §
10.7
to construct
K
-integrals of solutions to appropriate inhomogeneous wave equations.

We start by considering the homogeneous wave equation (
3.8
) with
|a|=M
:

\square_{g_{M,\pm|M|}}\phi=0.

Recall that
\alpha_{m\ell}=\Lambda_{+,m\ell}+\frac{1}{4}-2m^{2}
. We will consider in this section
m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
such that (
4.1
) holds, i.e.

\alpha_{m\ell}\neq 0.

Recall the definitions of
\Pi
and
\widehat{\phi}
from §
4
. Then
\widehat{\phi}
satisfies the following inhomogeneous wave equation:

\square_{g}\widehat{\phi}=-\square_{g}\Pi.

(10.23)

In Proposition
10.9
below, we establish suitable decay of
\square_{g}\Pi
in
\tau
and
r
that will be required to be able to apply the energy decay estimates from §
9
to the time integral
K^{-1}\widehat{\phi}
that we will construct in §
10.5
.

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
K,L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a
C=C(K,N,m)>0
, such that for all
k\leq K
and
l\leq L
:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}K^{l}\square_{g}\Pi|(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-1-\frac{1}{2}\nu_{m}-l}\quad\textnormal{for $r\leq 2$},

(10.24)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}|(Xr^{2})^{k}K^{l}\square_{g}\Pi|(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-3}(1+\tau)^{-2-l}\quad\textnormal{for $r\geq 2$},

(10.25)

with
X=\widetilde{X}-im\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{2}
and

\nu_{m}=\min\left(\{\alpha_{m\ell}\,|\,\alpha_{m\ell}>0\}\cup\{1\}\right).

Proof.

We apply Proposition
A.2
and
A.3
to estimate
|(\widetilde{X}^{k}K^{l}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}|^{2}
and then sum over
\ell
to obtain the estimate in
r\leq 2
. Similarly, we apply Proposition
A.4
to estimate
|(\widetilde{X}^{k}K^{l}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}|^{2}
and then sum over
\ell
to obtain the estimate in
r\geq 2
.
∎

10.5.
Construction of
K
-integral initial data

In this section, we consider the equation (
10.23
) and we apply
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
to appropriate functions of the initial data for
\widehat{\phi}
, in order to construct the initial data along
\Sigma_{0}
that evolves into the
K
-integral
K^{-1}\widehat{\phi}
.

Let
\widetilde{\phi}
be a solution to

\square_{g}\widetilde{\phi}=-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\square_{g}\Pi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}

(10.26)

such that
K\widetilde{\phi}=\phi
. Then we must have that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}\widetilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}=&-2(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}\Delta)\widetilde{X}(\phi-\Pi)|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}-2\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\Phi(\phi-\Pi)|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\\
&-\left(\mathbbm{h}(\Delta\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))-\sin^{2}\theta\right)K(\phi-\Pi)|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+\left(2r-\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta\mathbbm{h})\right)(\phi-\Pi)|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\\
&\>-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\square_{g}\Pi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime}.\end{split}

We define:

\displaystyle F_{\rm hom}[\phi]:=

\displaystyle-2(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}\Delta)\widetilde{X}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}-2\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\Phi\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle-\left(\mathbbm{h}(\Delta\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))-\sin^{2}\theta\right)K\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}+\left(2r-\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta\mathbbm{h})\right)\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},

\displaystyle F_{\Pi}:=

\displaystyle 2(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}\Delta)\widetilde{X}\Pi|_{\Sigma_{0}}+2\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\Phi\Pi|_{\Sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle\>+\left(\mathbbm{h}(\Delta\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))-\sin^{2}\theta\right)K\Pi|_{\Sigma_{0}}-\left(2r-\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta\mathbbm{h})\right)\Pi|_{\Sigma_{0}}-\int_{0}^{\infty}\square_{g}\Pi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}

and write
F[\phi]:=F_{\rm hom}[\phi]+F_{\Pi}
. Then

\mathcal{L}\widetilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}}=F[\phi].

(10.27)

In view of (
10.27
), we consider the following data that we will evolve into a solution
K^{-1}\phi
to (
10.26
) with
K(K^{-1}\phi)=\phi
in a suitable spacetime region:

(\widetilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}},(K\widetilde{\phi})|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\chi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F[\phi]),\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}),

(10.28)

where
\chi_{*}
is a smooth cut-off function in
r
such that
\chi_{*}(r)=1
for
r-1\geq r_{\rm initial}
and
\chi_{*}(r)=0
for
r-1\leq\frac{1}{2}(r_{\rm initial}-1)
, with
r_{\rm initial}-1>0
arbitrarily small. The cut-off function
\chi_{*}
will ensure that the initial energies are finite and we will be able to interpolate additional growth of initial energies of
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F[\phi])
as
r\downarrow 1
into growth in
\tau
.

We let
\widetilde{\phi}
be the solution to the initial value problem corresponding to the initial data (
10.28
) and the equation:

\displaystyle\square_{g}\widetilde{\phi}=

\displaystyle\>G,

(10.29)

\displaystyle G(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})=

\displaystyle\>-\xi_{*}(\tau)\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\square_{g}\Pi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime},

(10.30)

with
\xi_{*}(\tau)
a smooth cut-off function, satisfying
\xi_{*}(\tau)=1
for
\tau\leq\tau_{*}
and
\xi_{*}(\tau)=0
, with
\tau_{*}\geq 1
arbitrarily large.

We first verify the assumptions (
6.1
), (
9.2
) and (
9.3
) on
G
and establish
\tau_{*}
-dependent bounds on the integrals involving
G
in
D_{1,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]
and
D_{2,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]
.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
G
be defined by (
10.30
). Then
G
satisfies (
6.1
), (
9.2
) and (
9.3
). Furthermore, for
\delta>0
arbitrarily small, there exists a constant
C>0
(independent of
\tau_{*}
) such that

\displaystyle D_{1,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]-D_{1,{\rm hom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]\leq C\tau_{*}^{1-\nu_{m}+\delta}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2},

(10.31)

\displaystyle D_{2,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]-D_{2,{\rm hom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]\leq C\tau_{*}^{1-\nu_{m}+\delta}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}

(10.32)

Proof.

By integrating (
10.24
) and (
10.25
) in
\tau
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\sup_{(\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widetilde{X}^{k}K^{l}\square_{g}\Pi|(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}\nu_{m}-l}\quad\textnormal{($r\leq 2)$}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\delta}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\delta-\frac{1}{2}\nu_{m}-l},

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\sup_{(\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}|(Xr^{2})^{k}K^{l}\square_{g}\Pi|(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-3}(1+\tau)^{-1-l}\quad\textnormal{($r\geq 2$)},

with
0<\delta<\nu_{m}
. Then (
6.1
), (
9.2
) and (
9.3
), as well as the estimates (
10.31
) and (
10.31
), follow immediately.
∎

In the proposition below, we show that
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F[\phi])
is well-defined.

Proposition 10.11
.

Let
\phi
be a solution to (
3.8
) arising from initial data in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})
and assume that
\alpha_{m\ell}\neq 0
for all
\ell\geq|m|
. Then the expressions

u_{m\ell}(r):=-w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\int_{1}^{r}(r^{\prime}-1)^{-2}w_{\infty,m\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})e^{im\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}\left[\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(x)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{x}\frac{y+1}{y-1}\,dy}F_{m\ell}[\phi]\,dx\right]\,dr^{\prime}

(10.33)

are well-defined, and in the case
\Lambda_{+,m\ell}<2m^{2}
, there exists a unique
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\in\mathbb{C}
for each
\ell,m
such that

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}F_{m\ell}[\phi]\,dr=0,

(10.34)

with
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}=0
if and only if the initial data satisfy

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}(F_{\rm hom})_{m\ell}[\phi]\,dr=0.

If we assume instead that there exists an
\ell_{*}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\alpha_{m\ell_{*}}=0
, then we impose the following restriction on the initial data for
\phi
:

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell_{*}}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}(F_{\rm hom})_{m\ell_{*}}[\phi]\,dr=0\quad\forall\ell\geq|m|.

(10.35)

Furthermore,
F_{m\ell}\in C^{1}(1,\infty)
,
u_{m\ell}\in C^{2}(1,\infty)
and
\mathcal{L}u_{m\ell}=F_{m\ell}[\phi]
.

Define
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F[\phi])=\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}u_{m\ell}
. Then
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F[\phi])\in L^{2}(\Sigma_{0})
and
\widetilde{\phi}
satisfies: let
\epsilon>0
, then there exists a constant
C_{m}>0
, such that

D_{1,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]+D_{2,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]\leq C_{m}(\tau_{*}^{1-\nu_{m}+2\epsilon}+(1-r_{\rm initial})^{-2\epsilon})D^{(1)}[\phi],

(10.36)

with

D^{(1)}[\phi]:=\sum_{|m|\leq l\leq l_{m}}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}+\sum_{k+l\leq 4,l\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+l}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}\sum_{k\leq 2}r^{2-\delta}|LK^{k}\psi|^{2}+\sum_{k\leq 1}r^{2-\delta}|L((r^{2}L)K^{k}\psi)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\leq 2\\
j\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sup_{r^{\prime}\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r=r^{\prime}\}}|\widetilde{X}^{j}((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}F_{\rm hom}[\phi])|^{2}\,d\sigma+\sup_{r^{\prime}\geq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r=r^{\prime}\}}|rF_{\rm hom}[\phi]|^{2}\,d\sigma\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq 2\}}r^{2}|\widetilde{X}F_{\rm hom}[\phi]|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

Proof.

The statements in the proposition follow from an application Propositions
10.7
and
10.8
. We therefore need to show that the assumptions in Proposition
10.7
hold. That is to say, we need to show that

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}(F_{\Pi})_{m\ell}[\phi]\,dr<\infty

(10.37)

is well-defined. We split the integral into a
\int_{1}^{2}
and
\int_{2}^{\infty}
part. For
r\leq 2
, we have by Propositions
A.2
and
A.3
that

\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\left|\square_{g}\Pi_{m\ell}\right|(\tau^{\prime},r)\leq C_{m}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|.

We can directly estimate the remaining terms in
F_{\Pi}
from the definition of
\Pi
to obtain: for
r\leq 2

|(F_{\Pi})_{m\ell}|(r)\leq C_{m}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|.

In order to estimate
|(F_{\Pi})_{m\ell}|(r)
for
r\geq 2
, we first observe that we can rewrite:

\begin{split}F_{\Pi}=&2r^{2}(1+O(r^{-2}))\left(\widetilde{X}-\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{2}\Phi\right)\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+(2r+O(r^{-1}))\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+2\left(O(1)-\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\Phi\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\\
&\>+\left(O(1)-\sin^{2}\theta\right)K\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\square_{g}\Pi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}\\
=&2r(1+O(r^{-2}))\left(\widetilde{X}-\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{2}\Phi\right)(r\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}})+O(r^{-1})\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+2\left(O(1)-\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\Phi\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\\
&\>+\left(O(1)-\sin^{2}\theta\right)K\Pi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}-\int_{0}^{\infty}\square_{g}\Pi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\tau^{\prime}.\end{split}

For
r\geq 2
, we apply Proposition
A.4
to obtain

\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\left|\square_{g}\Pi_{m\ell}\right|(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\leq C_{m}r^{-3}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|.

We can directly estimate the remaining terms in
F_{\Pi}
in order to obtain

|(F_{\Pi})_{m\ell}[\phi]|(r)\leq C_{m}r^{-1}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|.

We conclude that (
10.37
) holds.

We may assume without loss of generality that

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}(F_{\Pi})_{m\ell}\,dr\neq 0,

for
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\neq 0
. Indeed, if the above integral were vanishing, we could redefine
f_{\pm}^{m\ell}
by replacing
\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+1\right)
with
\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+\beta\right)
with
\beta>0
,
\beta\neq 1
and similarly, we could redefine
f_{\pm}^{m\ell,{\rm exp}}
by replacing
\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+e^{r-1}\right)
with
\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+\beta e^{r-1}\right)
. As these modifications occur at subleading order in
\tau
,
r
and
\frac{1}{r-1}
, they do not affect the estimates involving
\Pi
.

Hence, we can choose
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}
such that

\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}F_{m\ell}[\phi]\,dr=\int_{1}^{\infty}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)e^{-im\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{x+1}{x-1}\,dx}((F_{\Pi})_{m\ell}+(F_{\rm hom})_{m\ell}[\phi])\,dr=0.

The estimate (
10.36
) then follows by using that
K^{k+1}\widetilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}}=K^{k}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}
and applying Proposition
10.8
to estimate the terms involving
\widetilde{\phi}
in
D_{1,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]+D_{2,{\rm inhom}}[\widetilde{\phi}]
that do not involve
K\phi
and Lemma
10.10
to estimate the terms involving
G
.

Note that we pick up a factor
\tau_{*}^{2\epsilon}
from the terms involving
\widetilde{\phi}
since in
r\leq 2

\begin{split}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}}|^{2}=&\>(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|X(\chi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F))|^{2}\\
\leq&\>\frac{1}{2}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|\chi_{*}|^{2}|\widetilde{X}(\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F))|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(r-1)^{1-\epsilon}|\frac{d\chi_{*}}{dr}||\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)|^{2}\\
\leq&\>(1-r_{0})^{-2\epsilon}(r-1)^{-1+\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}(\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F))|^{2}+(1-r_{0})^{-\epsilon}|\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)|^{2}.\end{split}

We similarly pick up a factor
\tau_{*}^{1-\nu_{m}+\delta}
from Lemma
10.10
and we set
\delta=2\epsilon
.
∎

We now introduce in the following subset of the domain of dependence of
\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{\rm initial}\}
:

\{\tau\leq\tau_{*}\}\cap D^{+}(\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{\rm initial}\}),

the
K
-integral as follows:

K^{-1}\widehat{\phi}:=\widetilde{\phi}

as
K\widetilde{\phi}=\phi
in this region, by construction.

11.
Decay estimates and late-time asymptotics

In this section, we will apply the energy decay estimates for
K\phi
and
K^{2}\phi
from Proposition
9.2
to
K^{-1}\widehat{\phi}
for
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{*}
, in order to obtain energy decay for
\widehat{\phi}
and
K\widehat{\phi}
. We recall that

\nu_{m}=\min\left(\{\alpha_{m\ell}\,|\,\alpha_{m\ell}>0\}\cup\{1\}\right).

We will moreover assume that either the condition (
4.1
) holds on
\Lambda_{m\ell}
or that the initial data for
\phi
satisfy the restriction (
10.35
).

11.1.
Energy decay estimates

We first establish appropriately weighted energy decay estimates for
\widehat{\phi}
.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small and
r_{I}>1
suitably large. Then there exist
\delta,\epsilon>0
suitably small, such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi],

(11.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi],

(11.2)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi].

(11.3)

Proof.

Let
\tau_{*}=\tau
. There exists a constant
B>0
such that with the choice
(r_{\rm initial}-1)^{-1}:=\frac{1}{(r_{1}-1)}+B\tau_{*}
, we have for all
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{*}
that
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}\subset D_{+}(\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{\rm initial}\})
. Hence,
K(K^{-1}\phi)=\phi
on
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}
.

We first take
r_{1}=1+\tau^{-1}
and apply Proposition
9.2
together with Proposition
10.11
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq 1+(\tau+1)^{-1}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq&\>(\tau+1)^{-\delta}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\widetilde{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta-\delta}D_{\rm inhom}[\widetilde{\phi}]\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta-\delta+2\epsilon}D^{(1)}[\phi].\end{split}

We can take
\delta>2\epsilon
. It remains to estimate the integral when
r\in[1,1+(\tau+1)^{-1})
. We define
r_{1}=1+(\tau+1)^{-1}
and
r_{j}=1+2^{-j}(\tau+1)^{-1}
for
j\geq 1
. Note that
\{\frac{1}{r_{j}-1}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is dyadic. We then have the following estimate in the dyadic intervals
[r_{j+1},r_{j}]
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{j+1}\leq r\leq r_{j}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq&\>2^{-j\delta}(\tau+1)^{-\delta}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K\widetilde{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C2^{-j\delta}(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta-\delta}D_{\rm inhom}[\widetilde{\phi}]\\
\leq&\>C2^{-j\delta}(\tau+1)^{-\delta}(2^{j+1}+B)^{2\epsilon}(\tau+1)^{2\epsilon}D^{(1)}[\phi]\\
\leq&\>2^{-j(\delta-2\epsilon)}C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta-\delta+2\epsilon}D^{(1)}[\phi].\end{split}

Using that
\delta>\epsilon
, we can sum over
j
, since the right-hand side forms a geometric series in
j
. We obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq 1+(\tau+1)^{-1}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta-\delta+2\epsilon}D^{(1)}[\phi].

We therefore conclude (
11.1
).

The estimate (
11.2
) follows in the same manner, since
K\phi=K^{2}\widetilde{\phi}
. Finally, (
11.3
) follows similarly with
(r_{\rm initial}-1)^{-1}:=B\tau_{*}
as it concerns the region
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}
, which is contained in
D_{+}(\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{\rm initial}\})
.
∎

We can obtain an additional energy decay estimate for the non-degenerate energy with energy density
\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}]
.

Proposition 11.2
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi].

(11.4)

Furthermore,

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi].

(11.5)

Proof.

We let
r_{1}
and
r_{\rm initial}
be as in the proof of Proposition
11.1
. Let
r_{1}-1\geq(\tau+1)
. Then it follows immediately from (
11.1
) that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq(\tau+1)^{1-\epsilon-\delta}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi].

for a suitably small
\eta>0
.

Now replace
r_{1}
with
r_{j}=1+2^{j}(1+\tau)
for
j\geq 1
and consider the corresponding
r_{\rm initial}
. We apply (
9.1
) to express
(r-1)^{-2}\underline{L}\phi
in terms of derivatives of
K^{-1}\phi
: let
\widetilde{Z}\in\{(r-1)\widetilde{X},K,\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\}
, then,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{j+1}\leq r\leq r_{j}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq&\>C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{j+1}\leq r\leq r_{j}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}\widetilde{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C2^{-j}(1+\tau)^{-1+\epsilon}\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{j+1}\leq r\leq r_{j}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\widetilde{Z}^{k}\widetilde{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr.\end{split}

We apply Corollary
8.5
with
\phi
replaced by
\widetilde{\phi}
to estimate the right-hand side further and use that the term on the right-hand side of (
8.8
) is bounded by
((r_{\rm initial}-1)^{-2\epsilon}+(1+\tau)^{-1+\nu_{m}+\delta})D^{(1)}[\phi]
. This gives:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{j+1}\leq r\leq r_{j}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\leq&\>C2^{-j}(1+\tau)^{-1+\epsilon}[(2^{j}(1+\tau)+B)^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau)^{-1+\nu_{m}+\delta}]D^{(1)}[\phi]\\
\leq&\>C2^{-(1-\epsilon)j}(1+\tau)^{-1+\nu_{m}+\delta}D^{(1)}[\phi],\end{split}

for suitable
\delta>0
. We conclude the argument by summing over
j
and combining with the estimate in the region
r\geq 1+(1+\tau)
.

We obtain (
11.5
) by interpolating (
11.4
) with (
11.1
) to get rid of the degenerate factor
(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}
in (
11.1
).
∎

In the proposition below, we show that energies with growing
r
-weights, restricted to far-away regions
r>r_{I}
also decay suitably fast, provided we restrict to
|m|\geq 2
. This will be important for deriving the precise late-time asymptotics for
r\phi
all the way up to
\mathcal{I}^{+}
.

Proposition 11.3
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exist a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|LK\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-3}D_{2}^{(1)}[\phi],

(11.6)

with

D_{2}^{(1)}[\phi]:=\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+D^{(1)}[\phi]+D^{(1)}[K\phi].

Proof.

We apply (
8.11
) with
p=1
(and
\psi
replaced by
\widehat{\psi}
), and we estimate the terms on the RHS of (
8.11
) using (
10.25
) and (
7.8
) to conclude that for all
0\leq\tau_{B}

\int_{0}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CD^{(1)}[\phi].

By the mean-value theorem, there exists a dyadic sequence such that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau_{n})^{-1}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+D^{(1)}[\phi]\right].

By a Hardy inequality, this implies in particular that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|L(r^{2}L)K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau_{n})^{-1}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+D^{(1)}[\phi]\right].

(11.7)

We now apply (
8.10
) with
p=2
and
0\leq\tau_{A}\leq\tau_{B}
to estimate

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}|L(r^{2}L)K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\widehat{\phi}]\,d\sigma dr.

By interpolating between (
11.2
) and (
11.4
) applied to
K^{k}\widehat{\phi}
instead of
\widehat{\phi}
, we can estimate for all
\tau\geq 0

\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k+1}\widehat{\phi}]\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-3-\nu_{m}+\eta}(D^{(1)}[\phi]+D^{(1)}[K\phi]).

Hence, after another application of the mean-value theorem along an appropriate dyadic sequence, together with (
11.7
), we obtain

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C(1+\tau_{n}^{(1)})^{-2}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+D^{(1)}[\phi]+D^{(1)}[K\phi]\right],

with
\tau_{n}^{(1)}
another dyadic sequence.

We repeat the last step by applying (
8.10
) with
p=1
to obtain along a dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{n}^{(2)}\}
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}|L(r^{2}L)K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C(1+\tau_{n}^{(2)})^{-3}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r|L(r^{2}L)^{2}K\widehat{\psi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+D^{(1)}[\phi]+D^{(1)}[K\phi]\right]

We then infer that (
11.6
) holds along the sequence
\{\tau_{n}^{(2)}\}
by once more applying a Hardy inequality and applying (
11.2
) to estimate the resulting boundary terms. We then obtain (
11.6
) for all
\tau\geq 0
by taking
\tau_{n}^{(2)}<\tau\leq\tau_{n}^{(2)}
and applying (
8.9
) with
p=2
in the interval
[\tau_{n}^{(2)},\tau]
.
∎

Remark 11.1
.

The restriction
|m|\geq 2
in Proposition
11.3
is in fact not necessary. It is made to ensure a long enough hierarchy of
r
-weighted energy estimates in the region
r>r_{I}
, obtained by commuting with
(r^{2}L)^{2}
. In the
|m|=1
case, one cannot directly commute with
(r^{2}L)^{2}
, as in (
8.11
), as this estimate relies on a Poincaré inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
with constants that need to be suitably large. Instead, it is possible to obtain a suitably long hierarchy of
r
-weighted energy estimates by considering Newman–Penrose quantities; see
[
AAG23
, §5.2]
.

Note that the expected sharp decay rates for
|m|=1
would be larger than the decay rates for
|m|\geq 2
, since
\alpha_{m|m|}>0
for
|m|=1
and
<0
for
|m|\geq 2
, so the
|m|=1
part of
\phi
would contribute to sub-leading order in the late-time asymptotics of
\phi
. For this reason, we have decided to exclude it from the decay estimates in this section.

Proposition 11.4
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small and let
r_{I}>1
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}_{|m|\geq 2}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1+\delta}|L\widehat{\phi}_{|m|\geq 2}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2-\frac{\nu_{m}}{2}+\eta}D_{2}^{(1)}[\phi].

(11.8)

Proof.

Let
\phi
be restricted to
|m|\geq 2
. We apply (
7.7
) and (
8.10
) with
p=2-\delta
to estimate for all
0\leq\tau_{A}\leq\tau_{B}
:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}^{2}|^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[K^{k}\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C(1+\tau_{A})^{-1-\nu_{m}}D^{(1)}[\phi].

By the mean-value theorem and application of (
11.5
) and (
11.3
), we therefore obtain that along a suitable dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{n}\}

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau_{n})^{-2-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi].

It remains to extend the above estimate to all
\tau\geq 0
. Let
\tau_{n}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{n+1}
. Then we apply the fundamental theorem of calculus in the
K
-direction to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}K((1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}])\,r^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&\>+\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}K(r|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2})\,\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}d\sigma dr\\
&\>+C\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}(\tau_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]+\tau_{n}\mathcal{E}_{0}[K\widehat{\phi}])\,r^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&\>+C\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}\tau_{n}^{-1+\frac{\nu_{m}}{2}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\tau_{n}^{1-\frac{\nu_{m}}{2}}r^{1-\delta}|LK\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

We estimate the spacetime integrals on the very RHS above via (
7.7
) and (
8.10
) with
p=2-\delta
by applying additionally (
11.6
) and (
11.2
) to the terms involving
K
-derivatives:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\delta}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{1-\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2-\frac{\nu_{m}}{2}+\eta}(D^{(1)}[\phi]+D^{(1)}[K\phi]).\end{split}

We finally interpolate the above estimate with (
11.3
) and (
11.1
).
∎

11.2.
Pointwise decay estimates

We can apply the energy decay estimates from Propositions
11.2
and
11.4
to obtain pointwise estimates for
\widehat{\phi}
. We will assume in this section that
|m|\geq 2
.

Proposition 11.5
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small and
r_{I}>1
. Then there exist
\epsilon>0
suitably small, such that for
\delta,\eta>0
arbitrarily small

\displaystyle||r^{\frac{1}{2}}(\phi-\Pi)||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}=

\displaystyle\>||r^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\phi}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\nu_{m}}{2}+\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{2k+l\leq 2}D^{(1)}[Q^{l}K^{k}\phi]},

(11.9)

\displaystyle||(1-r^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\phi-\Pi)||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}=

\displaystyle\>||(1-r^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\phi}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\})}

(11.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-\frac{\nu_{m}}{4}+\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{2k+l\leq 2}D_{2}^{(1)}[Q^{l}K^{k}\phi]},

\displaystyle||r(\phi-\Pi)||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\})}=

\displaystyle\>||r\widehat{\phi}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{I}\})}

(11.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-\frac{\nu_{m}}{4}+\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{2k+l\leq 2}D_{2}^{(1)}[Q^{l}K^{k}\phi]},

with
Q
the Carter operator.

Proof.

We use that
\lim_{r\to\infty}r\phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})=0
and apply the fundamental theorem of calculus and Cauchy–Schwarz to estimate

|\widehat{\phi}|(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\leq\int_{r}^{\infty}|\widetilde{X}\widehat{\phi}|(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,dr^{\prime}\leq\sqrt{\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-2}(1-r^{\prime-1})^{-1+\epsilon}\,dr^{\prime}}\sqrt{\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{2}(1-r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,dr^{\prime}}.

Hence,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\theta,\widetilde{\varphi}}}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma\leq Cr^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr.

We conclude (
11.9
) by applying (
11.1
) and a Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
with the fact that second-order derivatives on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
are controlled by
|Q\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+|K\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+|\Phi^{2}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}
. Since
Q,K,\Phi
are Killing operators, we can repeat all estimates with
\widehat{\phi}
replaced by
Q\phi,\Phi^{2}\phi,K\phi
to obtain additionally control over second-order derivatives on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

We apply the fundamental theorem of calculus again, together with Young’s inequality, to estimate

\begin{split}r^{-2+\epsilon}&(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}|r\widehat{\phi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})=\int_{1}^{r}\widetilde{X}(r^{-2+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}|r\widehat{\phi}|^{2})(\tau,r^{\prime},\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>\int_{1}^{r}(2r^{-2+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}|r\widehat{\phi}||\widetilde{X}(r\widehat{\phi})|+(1+\epsilon)r^{-4+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2})(\tau,r^{\prime},\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>C\int_{r}^{\infty}(r^{-3+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}|r\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+r^{-1+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{2+\epsilon}|\widetilde{X}(r\widehat{\phi})|^{2})(\tau,r^{\prime},\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,dr^{\prime}.\end{split}

Hence,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\theta,\widetilde{\varphi}}}r^{-2+\epsilon}(1-r^{-1})^{1+\epsilon}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{\epsilon}r^{-1+\epsilon}\left[(1-r^{-1})^{2}|\widetilde{X}(r\widehat{\phi})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr.

We derive (
11.10
) by proceeding as in the derivation of (
11.9
), but distributing the weights in
(1-r^{-1})
differently, so that we see an integral of
\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]
instead of
\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\widehat{\phi}]
:

|\widehat{\phi}|(r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\leq\int_{r}^{\infty}|\widetilde{X}\widehat{\phi}|(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,dr^{\prime}\leq\sqrt{\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-2}(1-r^{\prime-1})^{-2}\,dr^{\prime}}\sqrt{\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{2}(1-r^{-1})^{2}|\widetilde{X}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,dr^{\prime}}.

Hence,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\theta,\widetilde{\varphi}}}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma\leq C(r-1)^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr.

Then (
11.10
) follows after applying (
11.8
) together with a Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
as above.

We can improve (
11.10
) with a growing
r
-weight on the LHS by applying the fundamental theorem of calculus to
|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}
(with respect to
X
instead of
\widetilde{X}
):

\begin{split}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(r_{I},\theta,\varphi_{*})+2\int_{R}^{r}|\widehat{\psi}||X\widehat{\psi}|(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r_{I},\theta,\varphi_{*})+\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{-1-\delta}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,dr^{\prime}+\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{1+\delta}|X\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r_{I},\theta,\varphi_{*})+C\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{1+\delta}|X\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r_{I},\theta,\varphi_{*})+C\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{1+\delta}|X\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r_{I},\theta,\varphi_{*})+C\int_{R}^{\infty}(r^{1+\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+r^{-1+\delta}|K\phi|^{2}+r^{-1+\delta}|\Phi\phi|^{2})(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,dr^{\prime}\end{split}

Hence, after integrating over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we can estimate

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\theta,\phi_{*}}}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma\leq r_{I}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\theta,\phi_{*}}}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}(\tau,r_{I},\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}r^{2}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\phi}]+r^{1+\delta}|L\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

The first term on the RHS above can be estimated by (
11.10
) and the second term by (
11.8
). We conclude (
11.11
) by applying a Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
again.
∎

We now rewrite the
L^{\infty}
estimates in Proposition
11.5
to obtain global late-time asymptotics, which is the main result of this section.

Corollary 11.6
.

Let
|m|\geq 2
and assume that
\nu_{m}>0
. Let
0<\eta<\frac{\nu_{m}}{4}
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C_{m}>0
such that

\left|\phi_{m}-\Pi\right|(\tau,r,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\\
\leq C_{m}r^{-1}((\tau+1)(1-r^{-1})+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\nu_{m}}{4}+\eta}\sqrt{\sum_{2k+l\leq 2}D_{2}^{(1)}[Q^{l}K^{k}\phi]}.

(11.12)

12.
Azimuthal instabilities

In this section, we will use the decay results for
\widehat{\phi}
from §
11
to derive
non-decay
and instability results. As we are proving lower bounds for non-negative definite quantities, we can restrict without loss of generality to a single (or bounded set of)
\phi_{m}
. We will take
|m|\geq 2
so that by Lemma
2.1

\alpha_{m|m|}<0
.

Throughout this section we will assume that either (
4.1
) holds or that we restrict to initial data satisfying (
10.35
).

We first demonstrate the manifestation of an instability in the form of non-decay at the level of the non-degenerate energy
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr
, with
\phi
a solution to the homogeneous wave equation
\square_{g}\phi=0
.

We define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}_{m}:=\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|\leq\ell\leq l_{m}\\
\alpha_{m\ell}<0\end{subarray}}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}}

Proposition 12.1
.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists constants
c_{m},C_{m}>0
such that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\phi_{m}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\geq c_{m}\mathfrak{h}_{m}^{2}-C_{m}(1+\tau)^{-\nu_{m}+\eta}D^{(1)}[\phi_{m}].

In particular, for each
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exists a
\tau^{*}_{m}\geq 0
suitably large (depending on the initial data) such that for all
\tau\geq\tau^{*}_{m}

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\geq\frac{c_{m}}{2}\mathfrak{h}_{m}^{2}.

Proof.

We have that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\phi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}+\Pi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\geq(1-\epsilon)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\Pi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr-C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\phi}]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr.

By Proposition
11.2
, the second term on the very RHS above decays appropriately in
\tau
, so it remains to show that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\Pi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\geq c|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}-C(1+\tau)^{-\nu_{m}+\eta}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell\leq l_{m}}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}.

We have that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\Pi]\,r^{2}d\sigma dr\sim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(|\widetilde{X}\Pi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Pi|^{2}+|K\Pi|^{2}\right)\,r^{2}d\sigma dr.

It follows immediately that the integral of the terms
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Pi|^{2}+|K\Pi|^{2}
as well as the integral of
|\widetilde{X}\Pi|^{2}
restricted to
r\leq r_{H}
, with
r_{H}
arbitrarily small, decays with a rate faster than
(1+\tau)^{-1}
. It therefore remains to derive a uniform lower bound for the integral:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}|\widetilde{X}\Pi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|\leq\ell\leq l_{m}\\
\alpha_{m\ell}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int_{1}^{r_{H}}|\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell}|^{2}\,dr.

Let
\alpha_{m\ell}<0
. We consider without loss of generality the case
\widetilde{B}_{-}\neq 0
and
\widetilde{B}_{+}\neq 0
, in which we can appeal to the decomposition of
\Pi_{m\ell}
in (
A.2
) in the case
\alpha_{m\ell}<0
:

\displaystyle\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>b_{+,m\ell}{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+b_{-,m\ell}{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}(\tau,r),

\displaystyle\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\left[E_{+}(f_{+,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{+}^{m\ell})(\tau,r)+E_{-}(f_{-,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{-}^{m\ell})(\tau,r)\right].

The cases
\widetilde{B}_{-}=0
or
\widetilde{B}_{+}\neq 0
are strictly easier as the expressions (
4.3
) and (
4.3
) immediately give the right decomposition, with no need for
\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}
.

By Lemma
A.1
, it follows that
\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}
decays at least as fast as
(1+\tau)^{-1}
. We now compute:

\begin{split}\widetilde{X}(w_{m\ell,\pm}f_{\pm}^{m\ell})=&\>\widetilde{X}\left((1+O(r-1))\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}\right)(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im},\\
=&\>\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im\right)((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-\frac{3}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im}+O((\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}).\end{split}

Hence, for
c_{\pm,m\ell}=b_{\pm,m\ell}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im\right)
,

\begin{split}&|\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell}|^{2}\\
=&\>|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}|c_{+,m\ell}((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}+c_{-,m\ell}((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}|^{2}((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-3}(\tau+1)\\
&+\ldots\\
=&|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}(|c_{+,m\ell}|^{2}+|c_{-,m\ell}|^{2}+2\Re(c_{+,m\ell}\overline{c_{-,m\ell}}((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}})((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-3}(\tau+1)\\
&+\ldots\\
=&-\frac{|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}}{2}\frac{d}{dr}\left[|c_{+,m\ell}|^{2}+|c_{-,m\ell}|^{2})((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-2}\right]+\ldots\\
&\>-2|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}\frac{d}{dr}\Re\left[(2+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}})^{-1}c_{+,m\ell}\overline{c_{-,m\ell}}((\tau+1)(r-1)+4)^{-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-2}\right]+\ldots,\end{split}

where
\ldots
indicates terms that decay at least as fast as
(1+\tau)^{-1}
. Integrating the right-hand side in
r\in[1,r_{H}]
results in a constant that we can estimate further with a Young’s inequality

\begin{split}\int_{1}^{r_{H}}|\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell}|^{2}\,dr^{\prime}=&\>\frac{1}{32}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}(|c_{+,m\ell}|^{2}+|c_{-,m\ell}|^{2})+\frac{1}{16}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}\Re\left((2+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}})^{-1}c_{+,m\ell}\overline{c_{-,m\ell}}e^{-\log 4\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}\right)\\
+&\>|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}O(r_{H}-1)+\ldots\\
\geq&\>\frac{1}{32}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{4+|\alpha_{m\ell}|}}\right)(|c_{+,m\ell}|^{2}+|c_{-,m\ell}|^{2})+O(r_{H}-1)+\ldots\\
\geq&\>\frac{1}{64}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}(|c_{+,m\ell}|^{2}+|c_{-,m\ell}|^{2})\end{split}

for
r_{H}-1
suitably small.
∎

In the next proposition, we will show that the late-time asymptotics in Corollary
11.6
imply blowup of
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|Y\phi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma
along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Proposition 12.2
.

There exists a constant
c_{m}>0
depending only on
m
and a monotonically increasing sequence
\{\tau_{n}\}
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
\tau_{n}\to\infty
along which

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}|Y\phi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma\geq c_{m}\mathfrak{h}_{m}^{2}(1+\tau_{n}).

In particular, for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{-1,0,1,\}
, we can estimate

4\pi||Y\phi||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+})}\geq\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}|Y\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma\geq c_{m}\mathfrak{h}_{m}^{2}(1+\tau_{n}).

Proof.

We evaluate (
3.4
) with
G=0
along
\mathcal{H}+
and rearrange terms to obtain:

K(-4Y\phi)=+(\not{\mathcal{D}}-\Phi^{2})\phi-\Phi\phi+\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\phi-K\Phi\phi)+2K\Phi\phi+2K\phi.

Projecting to the angular functions
S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\widetilde{\varphi}}
, we obtain:

K(-4Y\phi_{m\ell})=(\Lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2}-im)\phi_{m\ell}+(\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\phi-imK\phi))_{m\ell}+2(1+im)K\phi_{m\ell}.

(12.1)

By Corollary
11.6
(applied also with
\phi
replaced by
K\phi
and
K^{2}\phi
), we have that

\left|K(-4Y\phi_{m\ell})-(\Lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2}-im)\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,1)\right|\leq O((1+\tau)^{-\frac{1+\nu_{m}}{2}}).

(12.2)

Without loss of generality, we assume
\widetilde{B}_{-}\neq 0
and
\widetilde{B}_{+}\neq 0
. The cases
\widetilde{B}_{-}=0
or
\widetilde{B}_{+}=0
can be treated with the same argument and are strictly easier to handle.

We integrate both sides of (
12.2
) in
\tau
to obtain

-4Y\phi_{m\ell}(\tau,1)+Y\phi_{m\ell}(0,1)\\
=\frac{1}{2}(\Lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2}-im){\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\int_{0}^{\tau}4^{-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}b_{+,m\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im}+4^{\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}b_{-,m\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im}\,d\tau\\
+O((1+\tau)^{\frac{1-\nu_{m}}{2}}).

Recall that

\alpha_{m\ell}=\Lambda_{m\ell}-2m^{2}+\frac{1}{4}.

We can therefore write

\Lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2}-im=\alpha_{\ell}-m^{2}-\frac{1}{4}=\left(\pm\sqrt{\alpha_{\ell}}+im-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\pm\sqrt{\alpha_{\ell}}-im+\frac{1}{2}\right)

Hence,

\begin{split}Y\phi_{m\ell}(\tau,1)=&\>-\frac{{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}}{8}\left(-\sqrt{\alpha_{\ell}}+im-\frac{1}{2}\right)e^{-2\log 2(\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im)}b_{+,m\ell}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im}\\
&\>-\frac{{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}}{8}\left(+\sqrt{\alpha_{\ell}}+im-\frac{1}{2}\right)e^{2\log 2(\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im)}b_{-,m\ell}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}-im}\\
&\>+O((1+\tau)^{\frac{1-\nu_{m}}{2}}).\end{split}

(12.3)

Denote

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{+,m\ell}=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{8}\left(-\sqrt{\alpha_{\ell}}+im-\frac{1}{2}\right)b_{+,m\ell},

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{-,m\ell}=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{8}\left(\sqrt{\alpha_{\ell}}+im-\frac{1}{2}\right)b_{-,m\ell}.

We therefore have that

|Y\phi_{m\ell}|^{2}(\tau,1)=|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}|\tilde{b}_{+,m\ell}+\tilde{b}_{-,m\ell}e^{i\sqrt{|\alpha_{m\ell}|}(\log(1+\tau)+4\log 2)-2im}|^{2}(1+\tau)+O((1+\tau)^{1-\nu_{m}}).

If
\tilde{b}_{+,m\ell}\neq 0
, it therefore follows that there exists a sequence of times
\{\tau_{n}\}
along which

|Y\phi_{m\ell}|^{2}(\tau_{n},1)=|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}|\tilde{b}_{+,m\ell}|^{2}(1+\tau_{n})+O((1+\tau_{n})^{1-\nu_{m}}).

If
\tilde{b}_{+,m\ell}=0
, we instead obtain

|Y\phi_{m\ell}|^{2}(\tau,1)=|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}|\tilde{b}_{-,m\ell}|^{2}(1+\tau)+O((1+\tau)^{1-\nu_{m}}).

for all
\tau\geq 0
.

By taking the sum over
\ell
, we therefore have that there exists a constant
c_{m}>0
and a sequence of times
\{\tau_{n}\}
along which:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|Y\phi_{m}|^{2}(\tau_{n},1,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma\geq c_{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|m|\leq\ell\leq l_{m}\\
\alpha_{m\ell}<0\end{subarray}}|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}|^{2}(1+\tau_{n}).

Considering the case
m=2
, we then obtain the existence of a numerical constant
c>0
such that:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}|Y\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|Y\phi_{m}|^{2}(\tau_{n},1,\theta,\widetilde{\varphi})\,d\sigma\geq c|H_{2|2|}|^{2}(1+\tau_{n}).\qed

We obtain similarly the existence of stronger instabilities for higher-order
Y
derivatives of
\phi
.

Proposition 12.3
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. There exists a constant
c_{m,k}>0
depending only on
m
and
k
and a monotonically increasing sequence
\{\tau_{n}\}
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
\tau_{n}\to\infty
along which

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}|Y^{k}\phi_{m}|^{2}\,d\sigma\geq c_{m,k}\mathfrak{h}_{m}^{2}(1+\tau_{n})^{1+2k}.

In particular, for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{-1,0,1,\}
, we can estimate

4\pi||Y\phi||^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+})}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{n}}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}|Y^{k}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma\geq c_{m,k}\mathfrak{h}_{m}^{2}(1+\tau_{n})^{1+2k}.

Proof.

Suppose
k=1
. We first evaluate (
3.4
) with
G=-\square_{g}\Pi
along
\mathcal{H}+
and rearrange terms to obtain:

K(-4Y\widehat{\phi})=+(\not{\mathcal{D}}-\Phi^{2})\widehat{\phi}-\Phi\widehat{\phi}+\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\widehat{\phi}-K\Phi\widehat{\phi})+2K\Phi\widehat{\phi}+2K\widehat{\phi}+\square_{g}\Pi.

We projecting the above equation the angular functions
S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\widetilde{\varphi}}
. After applying Corollary
11.6
(applied also with
\phi
replaced by
K\phi
and
K^{2}\phi
) together with (
10.24
), we obtain:

|K^{j}(Y\widehat{\phi}_{m\ell})|\leq C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\nu_{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2k+l\leq 4+2j\\
l\leq 1\end{subarray}}D^{(1)}[Q^{k}K^{l}\phi].

Hence, after integrating and filling in the definition of
\Pi_{m\ell}
, we obtain:

|K^{j}(Y\phi_{m\ell})|\leq C(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}-\nu_{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2k+l\leq 4+2j\\
l\leq 1\end{subarray}}D^{(1)}[Q^{k}K^{l}\phi].

(12.4)

Subsequently, we act with
Y
on both sides of (
3.4
), project onto
S_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{im\widetilde{\varphi}}
and evaluate along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
to obtain schematically:

K(Y^{2}\phi_{m})=(\Lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2}-im)Y\phi_{m\ell}+O((1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}-\nu_{m}+\eta}).

Now we use (
12.3
) to estimate further the right-hand side and we integrate both sides in
\tau
to obtain the late-time asymptotics of
Y^{2}\phi_{m}
and consequently, desired lower bound. This argument, when applied to
K^{j}Y^{2}\widehat{\phi}_{m}
moreover gives
K^{j}(Y^{2}\widehat{\phi}_{m\ell})=O((1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}-\nu_{m}+\eta})
.

Then
k>1
case can be treated inductively, by repeating the above argument, where we start with with late-time asymptotics of
Y^{l}\phi
and
K^{j}(Y^{l}\widehat{\phi}_{m\ell})=O((1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}+l-\nu_{m}+\eta})
for
l\leq k
.
∎

Appendix A
Estimates for
\square_{g}\Pi

We consider the functions
f^{m\ell}_{+}
,
f^{m\ell}_{-}
,
f^{m\ell}_{+,{\rm exp}}
and
f^{m\ell}_{-,{\rm exp}}
, defined in §
10.4
.

Lemma A.1
.

Assume that
\alpha_{m\ell}<0
.

(i)

Let
r\leq 2
and
K,L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C<0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq K
:

|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}K^{l}f_{\pm}^{m\ell}-((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}K^{l}f_{\pm}^{m\ell,{\rm exp}}|(\tau,r)\leq C|m|^{k+l+1}(r-1)(\tau+4(r-1)^{-1}+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-l},\\

(ii)

Let
r\geq 2
and
K,L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a
\delta>0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq K
:

|\widetilde{X}^{k}K^{l}f_{\pm}^{m\ell,{\rm exp}}|(\tau,r)\leq C|m|^{k+l}e^{-\delta r}(\tau+1)^{-1+\delta-l}.

Proof.

We consider first (i) with
K=L=0
. Note that

\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+e^{r-1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}-\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}=h(e^{r-1})-h(1),

with
h:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
defined as follows:

h(x)=\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+x\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}.

By the mean-value theorem applied to
h
, we have that for
r\leq 2

|h(e^{r-1})-h(1)|\leq(e^{r-1}-1)\sup_{1\leq x\leq e^{1}}|h^{\prime}(x)|\leq C|m|(r-1)\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Taking
L>0
, we can repeat the argument above in a straightforward manner.

Denote for the sake of convenience
a=-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im
. In the case
K=1
, we observe that

(r-1)\widetilde{X}\left[\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+e^{r-1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}-\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}\right]\\
=h_{1}(e^{r-1})-h_{1}(1)+a(r-1)\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+e^{r-1}\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im},

with

h_{1}(x)=-4a(r-1)^{-1}\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+x\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}\mp\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}+im}.

By applying the mean-value theorem, it follows that

|h_{1}(e^{r-1})-h_{1}(1)|\leq C|m|^{2}(r-1)\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

The general
K
case follows analogously.

We now consider (ii) with
K=L=0
. We first split:

|f_{\pm,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}|(\tau,r):=\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+e^{r-1}\right)^{-\delta}\left(\tau+\frac{4}{r-1}+e^{r-1}\right)^{\delta-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

Then it follows immediately that

|f_{\pm,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}|(\tau,r)\leq e^{–\delta r}(\tau+1)^{-1+\delta}.

The
K>0
and
L>0
case can be estimated via an analogous splitting.
∎

Proposition A.2
.

Let
K,L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
\alpha_{m\ell}<0
and
r\leq 2
. Then there exists a
C=C(K,N,m)>0
, such that for all
k\leq K
and
l\leq L

|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}K^{l}(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi))_{m\ell}|(r)\leq C\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-l}.

Proof.

We will prove the
K=N=0
case. The general
K
and
L
case follows analogously. Suppose first that
\widetilde{B}_{-},\widetilde{B}_{+}\neq 0
. Then we can apply (
4.2
). By (
3.5
), we have that

(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}=\widetilde{X}((r-1)^{2}\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell})-im(r^{2}-1)\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell}+(\lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2})\Pi_{m\ell}-imr\Pi_{m\ell}\\
+2(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})K\widetilde{X}\Pi_{m\ell}+\mathbbm{h}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))K^{2}\Pi_{m\ell}+im(2+\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1))K\Pi_{m\ell}\\
+(\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\Pi-imK\Pi))_{m\ell}+\left(2r-\frac{d}{dr}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h})\right)K\Pi_{m\ell}.

(A.1)

We will also denote:

\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(\cdot):=\widetilde{X}((r-1)^{2}\widetilde{X}(\cdot))-im(r^{2}-1)\widetilde{X}(\cdot)+(\lambda_{m\ell}+m^{2})(\cdot)-imr(\cdot)+2(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})K\widetilde{X}(\cdot)\\
+\mathbbm{h}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))K^{2}(\cdot)+im(2+\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1))K(\cdot)+\left(2r-\frac{d}{dr}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h})\right)K(\cdot),

so that

(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}=\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(\Pi_{m\ell})+(\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\Pi-imK\Pi))_{m\ell}.

We split

\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,r):={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)+E_{+}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r))f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)+E_{-}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r))f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)\\
+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\left[E_{+}(f_{+,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{+}^{m\ell})(\tau,r)+E_{-}(f_{-,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{-}^{m\ell})(\tau,r)\right]

Suppose
\widetilde{B}_{-}\neq 0
and
\widetilde{B}_{+}\neq 0
. Then further rewrite:

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)+E_{+}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r))f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(B_{+}-\widetilde{B}_{-}^{-1}B_{-}\widetilde{B}_{+})w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)+E_{-}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r))f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(B_{-}-\widetilde{B}_{+}^{-1}B_{+}\widetilde{B}_{-})w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r).

We denote
b_{+,m\ell}:={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(B_{+}-\widetilde{B}_{-}^{-1}B_{-}\widetilde{B}_{+})
and
b_{-,\ell}:={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(B_{-}-\widetilde{B}_{+}^{-1}B_{+}\widetilde{B}_{-})w_{+,m\ell}(r)
, so that

\displaystyle\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>b_{+,m\ell}w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+b_{-,m\ell}w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}(\tau,r),

(A.2)

\displaystyle\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\left[E_{+}(f_{+,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{+}^{m\ell})(\tau,r)+E_{-}(f_{-,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{-}^{m\ell})(\tau,r)\right].

(A.3)

Combining (
A.1
) with (
10.5
) implies:

(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}=\sum_{\square\in\{+,-\}}\overbrace{b_{\square,m\ell}w_{m\ell,\square}\widetilde{X}((r-1)^{2}\widetilde{X}f_{\square}^{m\ell})}^{=:J_{1}}\\
+\overbrace{b_{\square,m\ell}\left(2(r-1)^{2}\frac{\frac{dw_{m\ell,\square}}{dr}}{w_{m\ell,\square}}-im(r^{2}-1)\right)w_{m\ell,\square}\widetilde{X}f_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{2}}\\
+\overbrace{2b_{\square,m\ell}(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})\widetilde{X}(w_{m\ell,\square}Kf_{\square}^{m\ell})}^{=:J_{3}}\\
+\overbrace{b_{\square,m\ell}\left(im(2+\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1))+2r-\frac{d}{dr}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h})\right)w_{m\ell,\square}Kf_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{4}}\\
+\overbrace{b_{\square,m\ell}w_{m\ell,\square}\mathbbm{h}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))K^{2}f_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{5}}+\overbrace{\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}})}^{=:J_{6}}+\overbrace{(\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\Pi-imK\Pi))_{m\ell}}^{=:J_{7}}

We will estimate the terms
J_{i}
defined above. Without loss of generality, we will only consider the case
\square=+
, as the
\square=-
estimates proceed entirely analogously. We will drop the
m\ell
in the notation below.

J_{1}
:

J_{1}=4b_{+,m\ell}(1+O(r-1))\left(-2\sqrt{\alpha}+2im-3\right)\left(-2\sqrt{\alpha}+2im-1\right)\\
\times\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}+im}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}-im}\\

J_{2}
:

J_{2}=2b_{+,m\ell}(1+O(r-1))(2\sqrt{\alpha}+1)(-2\sqrt{\alpha}+2im-1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}+im}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}-im}

J_{3}
:

\begin{split}J_{3}=&\>4b_{+,m\ell}(-4\alpha+2im(4\sqrt{\alpha}+4)-8\sqrt{\alpha}-3+4m^{2})\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}+im}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-im-\sqrt{\alpha}}\\
+&\>2b_{+,m\ell}(2\sqrt{\alpha}+1)(2\sqrt{\alpha}+1-2im)\left(4\tau+1)(r-1)\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}+im}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-im-\sqrt{\alpha}}\\
+&\>O(1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\end{split}

It follows that the leading-order terms in
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
cancel when we sum them up. We obtain:

J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}=O(1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

J_{4}+J_{5}
:

We can immediately conclude that

J_{4}+J_{5}=O(1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

J_{6}
:

We have that:

J_{6}=\sum_{\square\in\{+,-\}}{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}E_{\square}\widetilde{w}_{m\ell,\infty}\widetilde{X}((r-1)^{2}\widetilde{X}(f_{\square,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{\square}^{m\ell}))\\
+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}E_{\square}\left(2(r-1)^{2}\frac{\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{m\ell,\infty}}{\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}}-im(r^{2}-1)\right)\widetilde{w}_{m\ell,\infty}\widetilde{X}(f_{\square,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{\square}^{m\ell})\\
+2{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}E_{\square}(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})\widetilde{w}_{m\ell,\infty}\widetilde{X}(\widetilde{w}_{m\ell,\infty}K(f_{\square,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{\square}^{m\ell}))\\
+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}E_{\square}\left(im(2+\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1))+2r-\frac{d}{dr}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h})\right)\widetilde{w}_{m\ell,\infty}K(f_{\square,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{\square}^{m\ell})\\
+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}E_{\square}\widetilde{w}_{m\ell,\infty}\mathbbm{h}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))K^{2}(f_{\square,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}-f_{\square}^{m\ell}).

In contrast with the analysis of
J_{1}
–
J_{3}
, we do need need to make use of cancellations to conclude that

J_{6}=O(1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Instead, we apply (i) of Lemma
A.1
.

J_{7}
:

Since
J_{7}
features at least one
K
derivative, we immediately get

J_{7}=O(1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Now suppose
\widetilde{B}_{+}=0
. Then,
\widetilde{A}_{+}=0
and
{A}_{+}\neq 0
, and by (
4.3
), we have that

\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,r):={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{+}^{-1}w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r).

Hence, we can repeat the arguments above for estimating
J_{1}
–
J_{5}
and
J_{7}
, with
b_{+,m\ell}
replaced by
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{+}^{-1}
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{B}_{-}=0
. Then,
\widetilde{A}_{-}=0
and
{A}_{-}\neq 0
, and by (
4.4
), we have that

\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,r):={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{-}^{-1}w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{-}^{m\ell}(\tau,r).

In this case we can apply the argument above for estimating
J_{1}
–
J_{5}
and
J_{7}
, with
b_{-,m\ell}
replaced by
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{-}^{-1}
.
∎

Proposition A.3
.

Let
K,L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
0<\alpha_{m\ell}<\frac{1}{4}
and
r\leq 2
. Then there exists a
C=C(K,N,m)>0
, such that for all
k\leq K
and
l\leq L

|((r-1)\widetilde{X})^{k}K^{l}(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi))_{m\ell}|(r)\leq C\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}_{m\ell}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}_{m\ell}}((\tau+1)^{-1}+(r-1)^{2\sqrt{\alpha_{m\ell}}}).

Proof.

By (
4.5
), we can split

\Pi_{m\ell}(\tau,r)={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{+}w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{-}w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}(\tau,r).

As in the proof of Proposition
A.2
, we write

(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}=\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(\Pi_{m\ell})+(\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\Pi-imK\Pi))_{m\ell}={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{+}\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell})\\
+{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{-}\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell}).

The first term on the very right-hand side can be estimated by repeating the argument involving cancellations from the proof of Proposition
A.2
in order to obtain:

|{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{+}\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(w_{+,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell})|=O(1)\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}}.

The term
{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{-}\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell})
, however, will not feature the cancellations seen above. Instead, we use that

\frac{w_{-,m\ell}}{w_{+,m\ell}}=O((r-1)^{2\sqrt{\alpha}})

to obtain

{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}A_{-}\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(w_{-,m\ell}(r)f_{+}^{m\ell})=O((r-1)^{2\sqrt{\alpha}})\left((\tau+1)(r-1)+4\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\alpha}}.

∎

Proposition A.4
.

Let
K,L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
r\geq 2
. Then there exists a
C=C(K,N,m)>0
, such that for all
k\leq K
and
l\leq L

|(K^{l}\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}|(r)\leq Cr^{-1}(1+\tau)^{-2-l}.

and

|K^{l}(Xr^{2})^{l}X\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi))_{m\ell}|(r)\leq Cr^{-1}(1+\tau)^{-2-l},

with
X=\widetilde{X}-im\frac{\mathbbm{h}}{2}
.

Proof.

Split

\displaystyle\Pi_{m\ell}=

\displaystyle\>{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{\infty,m\ell}(r)(f_{+}^{m\ell}+f_{-}^{m\ell})+\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}(\tau,r),

\displaystyle\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}}(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\widetilde{w}_{\infty,m\ell}(r)\left[E_{+}f_{+,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)+E_{-}f_{-,{\rm exp}}^{m\ell}(\tau,r)\right].

Then

(\rho^{2}\square_{g}\Pi)_{m\ell}=\sum_{\square\in\{+,-\}}\overbrace{{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{m\ell,\infty}\widetilde{X}((r-1)^{2}\widetilde{X}f_{\square}^{m\ell})}^{=:J_{1}}\\
+\overbrace{{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\left(2(r-1)^{2}\frac{\frac{dw_{m\ell,\infty}}{dr}}{w_{m\ell,\infty}}-im(r^{2}-1)\right)w_{m\ell,\infty}\widetilde{X}f_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{2}}\\
+\overbrace{2{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})w_{m\ell,\infty}\widetilde{X}Kf_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{3}}+\overbrace{2{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}(r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})\frac{dw_{m\ell,\infty}}{dr}Kf_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{4}}\\
+\overbrace{{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\left(im(2+\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1))+2r-\frac{d}{dr}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h})\right)w_{m\ell,\infty}Kf_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{5}}\\
+\overbrace{{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}w_{m\ell,\infty}\mathbbm{h}((r-1)^{2}\mathbbm{h}-2(r^{2}+1))K^{2}f_{\square}^{m\ell}}^{=:J_{6}}+\overbrace{\mathcal{L}^{K}_{m\ell}(\Pi_{m\ell,{\rm exp}})}^{=:J_{7}}+\overbrace{(\sin^{2}\theta(K^{2}\Pi-imK\Pi))_{m\ell}}^{=:J_{8}}

Without loss of generality, we will consider the terms with
\square=+
below.

J_{1}
:

We have that

J_{1}=O(r^{-3})(\tau+1)^{-2}.

J_{2}
:

We have that

J_{2}=O(r^{-1})(\tau+1)^{-2}.

J_{3}
:

We have that

r^{2}+1-\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2}=-r^{2}+O(1)

by assumptions made on
\mathbbm{h}
in §
2.2
. We therefore obtain

J_{3}=O(r^{-1})(\tau+1)^{-3}.

J_{4}
:

We have that

\frac{\frac{dw_{m\ell,\infty}}{dr}}{w_{m\ell,\infty}}=\frac{r^{2}+1}{(r-1)^{2}}\frac{\frac{dw_{m\ell,\infty}}{dr*}}{w_{m\ell,\infty}}=(im-2imr^{-1}-2r+O(r^{-2}))

so that

J_{4}=-2{\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\left(imr^{2}-2imr-2r+O(1)\right)w_{m\ell,\infty}Kf_{+}^{m\ell}.

J_{5}
:

We have that

\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}-1)=\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2}\frac{r^{2}-1}{(r-1)^{2}}=-2r^{2}-4r+O(1).

and

\frac{d}{dr}(\mathbbm{h}(r-1)^{2})=4r+O(r^{-1}).

Hence

J_{5}={\mathfrak{h}}_{m\ell}\left(-2imr^{2}-4imr+2r+O(1)\right)w_{m\ell,\infty}Kf_{+}^{m\ell}.

We therefore have a cancellation in the leading-order terms in
J_{4}+J_{5}
and we obtain

J_{4}+J_{5}=O(r^{-1})(\tau+1)^{-2}.

J_{6}
:

We have that

J_{5}=O(r^{-1})(\tau+1)^{-3}.

J_{7}
:

We do not need to make use of cancellations in the terms in
J_{7}
. Instead we apply (ii) of Lemma
A.1
to conclude that there exists a
\delta>0
such that

J_{7}=O(e^{-\delta r})(\tau+1)^{-2}

J_{8}
:

We have that

J_{8}=O(r^{-1})(\tau+1)^{-3}.

Since

X^{k}(e^{imr_{*}}r^{-1})=O(r^{-2-k})

and

((r-1)^{2}X^{k})f_{+}^{m\ell}=O((1+\tau)^{-2}),

we conclude the estimate in the proposition.
∎
